# SekaiPop development log

The detailed notes written while SekaiPop was built: what each change touched, what was run
and what was only read through, and how to check it. The short release notes are in
`sekaipop/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Keyboard layout on the lockscreen (issue #110)

**Not run on a live lockscreen.** The desktop it was written on has one keyboard layout (so the tag is hidden there by design), and locking the session from an agent is not something to try on the owner's session. Checked: `tests/lock-layout-test/lock_layout_test.js` (new, 28 checks: the code expression cut out of the service and run, the service's events, the bar reading the service, the tag in both looks) passes and fails when the tag's `visible` rule is broken on purpose; `tests/theme-import-test`, `tests/bar-tip-test`, `tests/keyboard-test` and the other lock tests pass, except `lock-calendar-test/calendar_source_test.js`, `lock-wallpaper-test/lock_wallpaper_unit.js` and `capsule-test/capsule_source_test.js`, which fail the same way on `main`; `qmllint` reports no error for the new service, the tag, both lock looks and `Bar.qml`. `hyprctl -j devices` was read on this machine to check the shape the service parses (`main`, `layout`, `active_keymap`).

- `services/KeyboardLayoutService.qml`: the bar's layout tracking (`hyprctl -j devices` once, then Hyprland's `activelayout` and `configreloaded` events, and a re-read after Settings > Hyprland changes `kb_layout`) moved out of `Bar.qml`, where every bar on every monitor kept and fetched its own copy, into one singleton. It also re-reads when the screen locks, since nobody can correct a stale code from the lockscreen. The code is still the first two letters of Hyprland's keymap name, upper case, and empty with one layout, exactly as before.
- `LockLayoutTag.qml`: the tag. In the gacha look it is a mint tag on the password pill's top-right corner, the counterpart of the Unlock pill's pink "Enter"; in the Calendar look, an ink tag at the end of the plate's label line. Each look narrows its label (Password, the failure text, the fingerprint message) so the two never overlap.
- Not done on purpose: the tag is not a button. The bar's pill cycles the layout on a click; the issue asks only to see which is active, and whether a layout key works on a locked session is the Hyprland bind's `locked` flag.

## Unreleased — Getting and updating plugins from Settings (the core of hako)

**Run on a live Quickshell 0.3.1 session**, in a second shell from this checkout with its own XDG folders and `SEKAIPOP_PLUGIN_LIST` pointing at this branch's `plugins.json`, beside the installed one. Done there, through ipc and on screen: installing masshirodev/sekaipop-tailscale from GitHub (the meter, then the installed note with its next steps), a check, an update, Updated with Go back, going back, pinning, removing (settings kept), the timed check's notification, and About / Update's Plugins and Get plugins cards and Desktop › Plugins with a plugin of each origin (installed, linked to a folder elsewhere, copied in). Checked: `tests/plugin-test/repos_unit.js` (new: the rules, plugins.json against the README, the wiring), `plugin_repos_test.sh` (new: the script against local git repositories, no network), `manifest_unit.js`, `form_unit.js`, `plugin_state_test.sh` and `registry_unit.js` pass; the other JS tests give the same results as `main`.

- The design: "Get and update plugins" on the shared canvas (notes pm1 to pm4). docs/PLUGIN-INSTALL.md is the account, with where it departs from the design.
- `plugin-repos.sh`: one place for git and the network. A repository's key is checked before it becomes a path, nothing is deleted outside the repos folder, git never asks for a password. `install` pipes git's progress through `tr '\r' '\n'` with `grep --line-buffered`, so the service's SplitParser gets each step as it comes.
- `PluginRepos.js`: the rules, pure and tested (the list, search, sources, origins, the lockfile, updates, the notice). The lockfile's `previous` is the commit the clone was really on (the script says), not the lockfile's own idea of it, so a clone moved by hand still goes back right.
- `PluginRepoService.qml`: one queue for every job, so two clicks never run git in one folder at once. It looks for updates on UpdateService's rules and timers (the same two settings).
- The scan (`plugin-state.sh`, `PluginManifest.splitScan`) now carries each folder's link target: that is how an installed plugin, one of your own and one copied in are told apart. `splitScan(out, true)` keeps a repository's root as a record (a plugin at the root of its repository).
- Settings: About / Update gains the Plugins and Get plugins cards (their jump chips come with them); Desktop › Plugins' rows gain where they came from, a behind badge and Update in About; its empty state points at Get plugins instead of saying nothing is downloaded.
- Not done: restore from a screen, a list other than plugins.json, updating a clone with local changes.
- **To check:** Get plugins > From a URL with a real repository off the list (Look, the not-reviewed note, Install it anyway), one that isn't a plugin, and a misspelt one; a repository with several plugins (its own row, its plugins under it, "2 OF 3 INSTALLED" when one is broken); Update all with two repositories; the notification's click opening About on Plugins; offline (the list's last copy with its age, Install waiting).

## Unreleased — App fonts: the font of GTK, Qt and KDE apps (issue #98)

**Not run on a live Quickshell before this entry** (the shell running on the desktop it was written on is an install, not this checkout). Checked: `tests/app-fonts-test/app_fonts_test.js` (new, 64 checks) passes, and fails where it should when the Qt 6 font string is broken on purpose; the other JS tests give the same results as `main`; `qmllint` passes `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `SettingText.qml`, and `AppFontService.qml` once its `IpcHandler` is cut out (`DesktopPresetService.qml` and `shell.qml` exit 255 with no message, as on `main`); `app-fonts.sh get` / `check` / `list` read this machine's settings correctly (296 families, 29 monospaced). `set` was only run against throwaway folders: on this machine it would have changed the owner's fonts.

- The issue: "after they are defined in the configuration in .config, they reset after some time". Nothing in SekaiPop writes a font key (`system-theme.sh`'s `ini_set` only touches the colour keys), so the likely cause is where they were set: SekaiPop's `hypr/hyprland/env.lua` sets `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=kde`, so Qt apps read `kdeglobals` and qt6ct isn't loaded at all; GTK on Wayland reads GNOME's `font-name` over `settings.ini`. Checked against plasma-integration's `qt6/src/platformtheme/kfontsettingsdata.cpp` (the keys it reads, `Noto Sans 10` when none is set, and the `refreshFonts` signal it reloads on).
- `scripts/theming/app-fonts.sh` (new), on the model of `icon-cursor.sh` (same `ini_set`, dconf before gsettings, the same KDE signal helper): `list ui|mono`, `get`, `set ui|mono <family> <size>`, `check`.
- `services/AppFontService.qml` (new singleton, started from `shell.qml`): what GTK has now is shown until something is picked; a pick is saved (`appFonts.*`) and put back at start when the system's differs; a refused `set` takes its saved choice back, so it isn't retried at every start. Writes run one at a time; sizes wait 0.5 s for the stepper to stop.
- `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`: the App fonts section after Icons & cursor: a field that searches as you type with up to 8 matches as chips under it (a machine easily has 300 families, too many for a row of chips), sizes, Use the system's. `SettingText.qml`: `edited` and `reset()`, so picking a match doesn't send the half-typed text as a submit when the field loses focus.
- `DesktopPresetService.qml`: `appFonts.` in `keepPrefix`.
- Found live right after the merge: `SettingsPageDesktop.qml` had no `import qs.modules.common`, so the section's intro text logged `ReferenceError: Theme is not defined` and drew with no colour. qmllint doesn't resolve the `qs.` imports, so it couldn't say; `tests/theme-import-test` now reads every QML file for a `Theme.` without that import (208 files, this was the only one).
- **To check:** with the shipped config (kde platform theme), pick a font: Dolphin, a Qt app and a GTK app (Files, a GTK text editor) change at once; `app-fonts.sh check` shows it in dconf, both settings.ini, kdeglobals; change it with `gsettings set org.gnome.desktop.interface font-name "Other 9"`, restart the shell: it is put back (the log says so); Use the system's, restart: it stays "Other 9". With `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=qt6ct`, a Qt app follows qt6ct.conf's `general`. Typing a name that isn't installed shows "No match"; Enter on it changes nothing.

## Unreleased — Plugins: bar entries, their popups, and a shared service

**Run on a live Quickshell 0.3.1 session** (Hyprland, three outputs): a second shell from this checkout, with its own `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` / `XDG_STATE_HOME` / `XDG_DATA_HOME` / `XDG_CACHE_HOME` (copies of the installed shell's) and `SEKAIPOP_PLUGINS_DIR`, beside the installed one, with `examples/plugins/hello-bar` and a real third-party plugin (masshirodev/sekaipop-tailscale) loaded. Seen: both entries in the bar in the shell's pill, next to the built-in ones; the Tailscale popup opening under its entry (`ipc call plugins popup tailscale`) with the entry held pressed, scrolling past nine rows, and each of its states; the entry's hover tag; the same plugin's desktop widget and its compact look, reading the one shared `Service.qml`. Checked: `tests/plugin-test/manifest_unit.js` (bar entries, the file list, the hosts, the wiring), `form_unit.js`, `plugin_state_test.sh` and `preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js` pass; the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (the same 12 fail).

- What it is: a plugin may ship `Bar.qml` (its entry, with `"bar": { "label", "icon" }` in plugin.json), `Popup.qml` (the card under it) and `Service.qml` (one poller shared by every surface), beside or instead of `Widget.qml`. All optional, so the api stays 1. The design: masshirodev/sekaipop-tailscale `doc/briefs/handoff-tailscale/` (canvas notes ts2 and ts3).
- The scan (`plugin-state.sh scan`) puts each folder's QML files on its line (`\036<folder>\037<files>`); `PluginManifest.parse` takes the list and fills `plugin.files` and `plugin.bar`. Without a list (an older script) a plugin reads as a widget alone. A `bar` without `Bar.qml`, a `Bar.qml` without `bar`, or nothing to draw is skipped with the new code `files`, which Settings says in plain words.
- On / off: `SettingsService.set()` refuses keys it doesn't declare, so a plugin entry can't have a `bar.<id>` switch. Its master switch is `PluginState.barShown`, saved in the plugin's own state file, off until turned on. `BarLayoutService` goes through `masterOn()` / `setMaster()` for every module, so Edit Desktop's BAR tab, per-monitor bars, RESET and CLEAR ALL treat a plugin entry like a built-in one without knowing it is one.
- The layout: `modules` is the built-in list plus `PluginService.barModules`. A `plugin:<id>` name is kept in a saved layout even before the scan has found that plugin (the layout is read first); one not in the layout yet goes at the end of the right zone (`withPlugins`). `customizedFor` / `customized` leave plugin entries out, so an appended one doesn't read as a change.
- The bar: `Status_Bar` makes a `PluginBarItem` per plugin entry with an Instantiator (a Repeater's delegates can't be parented to different zones) and places it by `zoneItem` / `slotOf` like the built-in pills; `itemsOf()` knows them. `PluginBarItem` draws the status pill's housing, takes the left button only (middle and right go through to the plugin), shows the plugin's `tip`, and drops out when the plugin says `present: false`.
- The popup: `Bar.qml` has `pluginPopup` (the key whose card is open), one `PluginPopup` (a `SekaiPopup`) per plugin with a popup, and every built-in toggle closes it (one popup at a time). `PluginPopup` builds `Popup.qml` the first time it opens and clamps it to 280-480 wide and 70 % of the screen tall.
- The service: `PluginState` makes `Service.qml` once (`Qt.createComponent`, asynchronous) and keeps `claims`: each widget window, bar entry and open popup says it is up under its own key, so `wanted` and `working` are true while any of them shows. The widget's `host` gained `service` and `wanted` too.
- Settings: Bar > Modules lists the plugin entries after the built-in ones ("ON · PLUGIN", a Settings key to its plugin form); Desktop > Plugins says "In the bar" and hides "On the desktop" and the place key for a plugin without a widget.
- Theme: `warnFill` (the Updates badge's amber, now read from the theme there) and the bar family (`surface`, `ink`, `line`, ...) are promised to plugins.
- Not done: a plugin entry's on / off isn't part of a desktop preset (its place is, as part of the bar layout); a short standing bar drops plugin entries with the weather (`tighterV`).
- **To check:** install a plugin with a bar entry, reload: it is OFF in Settings > Bar > Modules and in Edit Desktop's BAR tab; switch it on: it appears at the end of the right zone; move it in Edit Desktop; click it: its card opens, and clicking the weather pill swaps the card for the weather one; Esc and a click away close it; on a left, right and bottom bar and in the Capsule style the pill and card look like the built-in ones; `ipc call plugins popup <id>` toggles it on the focused monitor; removing the plugin's folder and reloading takes the entry away and keeps the rest of the bar as it was.
## Unreleased — Idle times in the Settings: lock, screen off, sleep or hibernate (issue #101)

**Not run on a live Quickshell before this entry** (the shell running on the desktop it was written on is an install, not this checkout). Checked: `tests/idle-test/idle_test.js` (new, 51 checks) passes; the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (80 pass, the same 11 fail); `qmllint` passes `SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, and `IdleService.qml` once its `IpcHandler` is cut out; `DesktopPresetService.qml` and `shell.qml` give no message but exit 255, as they do on `main` (qmllint exits 255 with no message on any file with a typed `IpcHandler`; `SettingsService.qml` on `main` does the same). Corrected after the merge: this said all three were clean; a real `hypridle -c` on the shipped `hypridle.conf` sourcing a written `idle.conf` registered all three rules with the right commands.

- What it is: `idle.lock`, `idle.screenOff`, `idle.sleep` (minutes, 0 = never; 5 / 10 / 15 by default, the old `hypridle.conf`'s times) and `idle.sleepAction` (`suspend` | `hibernate`). Settings > General > Idle & sleep.
- Why hypridle still waits instead of Quickshell's `IdleMonitor`: Keep Awake is a systemd idle inhibitor and browsers inhibit through `org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver`; hypridle honours both, `IdleMonitor` only Wayland's inhibitors.
- `scripts/system/idle-config.sh` (new): `write` renders `~/.config/sekaipop/idle.conf` (only when it changes; restarts hypridle when it is linked and running), `status`, `link`, `restart`. The restart waits for the old hypridle to exit so the new one gets the ScreenSaver name back (a second hypridle logs "Another service is already providing org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver"); a systemd user unit is restarted through systemd.
- `hypr/hypridle.conf`: the listeners are gone, `source = ~/.config/sekaipop/idle.conf` instead. hyprlang expands `~` and `$HOME` there, and a missing file is only a logged error, not a failed start.
- `link` for installs from before: one copy as `hypridle.conf.sekaipop-bak`, listener blocks commented with `# sekaipop-idle: ` (braces counted), the source line appended once; a symlinked file is refused with the line to add, shown in the section.
- `services/IdleService.qml` (new singleton; `shell.qml` reads `IdleService.ready` so it exists from the start): writes at load and 1.2 s after the last change. `DesktopPresetService.qml`: `idle.` in `keepPrefix`. install.sh: writes the default `idle.conf` when there is none and restarts hypridle after a full Hyprland install.
- **To check:** with the shipped config, set Lock after to 1 min and wait: it locks; `cat ~/.config/sekaipop/idle.conf` shows the times; `pgrep -a hypridle` shows a new pid after a change, one only; Keep Awake on: nothing happens; Never on all three: no listeners in the file; Hibernate on a machine without swap: the note says it can't. On an old install: Use these times appears, comments the listeners out, keeps the copy, and goes away.

## Unreleased — The Super tap no longer fires after every Super combination (issue #99)

**Not run on a live Hyprland 0.57 session** (the desktop it was written on runs 0.56.2 and doesn't use SekaiPop's Hyprland config). Read through instead: Hyprland's keybind manager at v0.56.2 (`src/managers/KeybindManager.cpp`) and at v0.57.0 (`src/keybinds/`, the rewrite from hyprwm/Hyprland#15568), and Quickshell 0.3.1's `global_shortcuts`. Checked: `luac -p hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua`; `tests/keybinds-test/keys_tap_unit.js` (three new cases, one changed), `keybinds_layout_unit.js`, `tests/keys-test/keys_unit.js` and `keys_script_test.js` pass.

- Why: Hyprland 0.57.0 (2026-10-28) rewrote the keybind manager. A `global` dispatcher now asks for a release follow-up when it runs on a press (`requestBindRelease` in `LuaBindingsDispatchers.cpp`), and that follow-up is sent when the key comes up without the shadowing check every other bind goes through (`Manager.cpp`, `processEvent`: `releaseFollowUps` skip `canInvokeNow`). So the shell's `onReleased` came after SUPER + N, SUPER + W and every other Super bind, and opened Apps on top of them. The reporter's "after updating this morning" is the Arch package moving to 0.57.0.
- The fix is Hyprland's own tap: a release bind on the modifier (`release = true`, `bindr` in hyprland.conf). On the press it only matches partially and isn't consumed; when another bind is consumed while Super is held, `shadowBinds` shadows it, and a shadowed bind doesn't run on the release. Hyprland's tests cover `SUPER + SUPER_L` with the release flag (`tests/keybinds/Bind.cpp`, `ModifierReleaseBindIncludesTriggerModifier`). With the flag the shortcut gets only a release, never a press; Quickshell emits `released` either way, and the shell only listens to `onReleased`.
- On 0.56: `release` is ignored for `global` binds there (a `global` is a "special dispatcher", so the release check is skipped), so the flag changes nothing on older Hyprland.
- `hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua`: `release = true` on both tap binds, with a comment saying why.
- `KeyBinds.js`: `isTap()` (a modifier key on its own, the test `caps()` already made). `luaLine` adds `release = true` to a tap's options and `confLine` writes `bindrd` / `bindlrd`, so a tap moved to Alt or Ctrl in the keybinds panel doesn't come back with the bug. Ordinary keys are written as before.
- `QuickPanel.qml`: the GlobalShortcut's comment says the bind, not the handler, tells a tap from a combination. `hypr/README.md`: the same, under "Behaviour changes to know about".
- Existing installs: Update now doesn't touch `~/.config/hypr`, so the changelog says which two lines to change. A tap already moved in the panel was saved without the flag; setting it again there writes it with the flag.
- Left alone: an unbound Super combination (Super + a key nothing is bound to) is not consumed, so it doesn't shadow the tap while that key is held; Hyprland shadows it once that key is released, so only letting go of Super first, with the other key still down, still opens Apps. That is how Hyprland's release binds work for everyone.
- **To check (Hyprland 0.57):** tapping Super opens and closes Apps; SUPER + N opens the Quick Panel and nothing else when Super comes up; SUPER + W opens the browser only; SUPER + drag to move a window doesn't open Apps; right Super does the same as left. In Settings > Hyprland > Keybinds, moving Open apps to an Alt tap writes `release = true` into the block, and ALT + Tab (or any Alt bind) doesn't open Apps. On 0.56.2 the Super tap still works.

## Unreleased — The Apps screen in most-used order (issue #94)

**Not run on a live Quickshell before this entry** (the shell running on the desktop it was written on is an install, not this checkout). Checked: `qmllint` on the four changed QML files reports nothing; `tests/app-usage-test/app_usage_test.js` (new) passes; the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (79 pass, the same 11 fail); `scripts/state/app-usage-state.sh` saved, loaded and refused a bad mode under a scratch `XDG_STATE_HOME`.

- What it is: `launcher.order` (`az` | `used`, default `az`), Settings > General > Launcher & lock > Apps order. With `used`, the grid with nothing typed is most used first, then the rest A to Z, and the usage breaks ties in search.
- `AppUsage.js` (new): one decaying score per desktop id (+1 per launch, halves every 14 days), `order()`, `record()`, `prune()` (forgotten below 0.03, at most 200 apps), `parse()` (anything unreadable is an empty store). Every score decays alike, so the order is the same whenever it is asked and needs no timer.
- `AppSource.qml`: `launch()` counts (`recordLaunch`) and saves the store; `ordered` is what the grid shows; the counts are read through `_usageRev`, bumped by `sync()` (the Apps screen opening) after a launch and once the file is loaded, so the tiles don't move while the phone slides away. `rankEntries()` breaks ties by the score with `used` on, and its cache keys on the setting and `_usageRev`.
- `scripts/state/app-usage-state.sh` (new): `~/.local/state/sekaipop/app-usage.json`, written through a temp file.
- `LauncherFace.qml`: the grid shows `source.ordered`. `SettingsService.qml`: the default. `SettingsPageGeneral.qml`: the choice.
- Not done: counting the dock's launches (the dock runs its entries itself) and a separate "Frequent" row (a new section on the Apps screen; reordering the grid is what #94 asked for).
- **To check:** with Most used on, open an app from Apps a few times, open Apps again: it is first, the unused apps follow A to Z; the tiles don't move while the phone goes away after a launch; the order survives a shell restart; typing a letter that two apps start with puts the more used one first; A to Z puts the grid back; deleting `app-usage.json` and restarting gives A to Z order under Most used.
## Unreleased — SUPER + A no longer toggles Keep Awake; no test-notification keys (issue #92)

**Not run on a live Hyprland session** (the desktop it was written on doesn't run SekaiPop's Hyprland config). Checked: `luac -p hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua`; `tests/keys-test/keys_unit.js` (two new cases), `keys_script_test.js` and `tests/keybinds-test/*` pass.

- Why: af5b11a added `shell("SUPER + A", "quickpanel keepAwake", ...)` beside `shell("SUPER + A", "launcherphone toggle", ...)`. Hyprland runs both binds on one key, so every SUPER + A opened Apps and flipped Keep Awake. Issue #92 also asks for fewer shipped binds that the keybinds panel can't change; the notification test keys were the clear leftovers.
- `hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua`: the Keep Awake bind is gone, and it is now one of the commented, keyless examples at the end (SUPER + ALT + A in the example, which nothing else uses). The three `notify-test.sh` binds are gone, replaced by a comment saying how to run the script; `hyprScripts` went with them, since nothing else used it. The script stays.
- `KeyBinds.js`: two actions, `power` (Toggle power options, `phone go power`, on SUPER + Escape) and `keepawake` (Toggle Keep Awake, `quickpanel keepAwake`, `bare`), so both can be bound or moved from the panel.
- `hypr/README.md`: "(more)" no longer mentions test keys.
- Left for the maintainer (the rest of #92 is a matter of taste): the screenshot variants (CTRL + SUPER + SHIFT + S, Print, CTRL + Print), the BracketLeft / BracketRight focus binds, binds with three or more modifiers, the virtual-machine submap, and the ALT + F4 reminder.
- **To check:** on a fresh install, SUPER + A opens and closes Apps and leaves the Quick Panel's Keep Awake tile alone; SUPER + ALT + F11 / F12 / = do nothing; Settings > Hyprland > Keybinds lists Toggle power options on SUPER + Escape, and moving it works; Toggle Keep Awake shows no key, and giving it SUPER + A asks first because Toggle apps is there.
## Unreleased — Auto-hide for the bar, and a key to show or hide it (issue #61)

**Run on the live shell** by the owner of the desktop it runs on, who reported it working (Hyprland, NVIDIA). Checked: `qmllint` reports no errors on the changed files; `tests/keys-test/keys_unit.js` and `keys_script_test.js` pass with the new action; the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (81 pass, the same 9 fail). Which of the cases below were each tried by eye was not itemised.

- What it is: `bar.hideMode` (`off` | `auto`, default `off`), Settings > Bar > Auto-hide. The bar slides off its edge (the preset slide, 220 ms) and comes back while the pointer is on it or on a 3 px line along the edge, while any of its popups is open, in Edit Desktop, and for 2 s on `ipc call bar peek`. It waits 0.45 s before going.
- `Bar.qml`: `revealed`, `hideSlide`, `tucked`, `hotThick`, `peek()`; the mask shrinks to the edge line while tucked; `exclusiveZone` is 0 while it auto-hides; one HoverHandler on top of the contents. `TrayPopups.showing` (dropdown or menu on screen).
- `BarPositionService.reserved` (0 while the bar auto-hides); the pinned dock's Edit Desktop origin uses it.
- `shell.qml`: `ipc call bar toggle | show | hide | peek | hidemode off|auto`. `KeyBinds.js`: the "Toggle bar" action (`bar toggle`, ships without a key); `hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua` lists it with the other keyless ones (SUPER + B in the example).
- Not done: a smart mode, Game Mode (the bar takes no input, so an auto-hiding one stays away), and the popups that keep clear of the bar (they still do). docs/BAR-AUTOHIDE.md.
- **To check:** Auto-hide on with the bar on each edge and in each style: it slides away, the windows grow into its space, touching the edge brings it out, it stays out with a popup open (the tray menu too) and goes 0.45 s after the pointer leaves; a bottom bar leaves the Quick Panel's hot edge working; Edit Desktop shows it; a shell restart with it on starts it away without a flash; `ipc call bar peek`; Settings > Hyprland > Keybinds > Toggle bar given SUPER + B shows and hides it.
## Unreleased — Focus Mode widgets no longer take the keyboard as they come back (issue #72)

**Not run on a live Quickshell before this entry** (the reporter confirmed the cause: with Desktop Focus Mode off, Rofi could be typed into at once). Checked: `qmllint` on the five files; `tests/focus-test/keys_on_point_test.js` (new); the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (81 pass, the same 9 fail).

- Why: To-Do, Calendar, Palette and Sticky Notes kept `WlrKeyboardFocus.OnDemand` whenever Edit Desktop was closed. Hyprland hands the keyboard to an OnDemand layer as it is mapped, and Desktop Focus Mode maps the widgets again, one after another (`FocusMath.slotDelay`), each time an empty workspace comes up. A launcher opened in that second lost the keyboard to each widget as it popped in.
- Changed: `desktopWidgets/KeysOnPoint.qml` (new), a passive HoverHandler over the window: `wanted` is true while the pointer is on the widget or its window has the keyboard (`Window.active`). The four windows are OnDemand only while it is; otherwise None. A window now maps as None, so it never takes the keyboard uninvited; the pointer arriving makes it OnDemand before the click that starts typing, and typing goes on after the pointer leaves.
- Files: `TodoWidget.qml`, `CalendarCardWidget.qml`, `PaletteWidget.qml`, `StickyNotesWidget.qml`, `KeysOnPoint.qml` (new), `tests/focus-test/keys_on_point_test.js` (new).
- **To check:** with Focus Mode on, switch to an empty workspace and open Rofi at once: typing works straight away. Then the widgets still take typing: add a task in the To-Do, an event in the Calendar, type in a sticky note, and keep typing after moving the pointer off them; clicking a window afterwards takes the keyboard back.
## Unreleased — The banner and avatar remove badges ask first

The first click arms the badge: it widens leftwards into a pill in the theme's accent (`accentCyan`, `colBorder` ink) with a bin icon and "Remove?"; the second click removes. It disarms when it hides (the pointer left the picture) or after 3 s.

Checked: `tests/phone-test/phone_wallpaper_test.js` (new) and `tests/launcher-test/lcd_source_test.js`; the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (the same 8 fail). **Not checked live:** the Settings row itself (Off / Desktop / A picture, Change the phone's picture), picking a picture through the chooser, light mode, and the cards with a long notification list over the wallpaper.

It replaces the Apps screen's on / off switch (`launcher.lcdWallpaper`), which still decides while the new key is unset: whoever had it off keeps a plain phone, and with it on (the default) the home screen now shows the wallpaper too.

## Unreleased — Click away follows the phone's shape (issue #47)

**Run on a live Quickshell** (Quickshell 0.3.1, Qt 6.11, Hyprland 0.56.2): the projected outline was logged and drawn over screenshots of the open panel, in 2.5D and in 2D; it follows the body, the side buttons and (2D) the Power button. The log stays clean. Checked: `tests/phone-test/click_away_test.js` (new); the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (the same 8 fail). **Not checked:** the alert card (the area is off then, by design), a monitor with fractional scaling.

- Why: the panel's window takes the mouse inside `hitRect`, the box around the posed phone. The tilted phone leaves big empty corners in it, and a press there landed on the window, hit nothing, and the panel stayed open.
- `PhoneMath.js`: `silhouette()` (front and back outlines plus boxes for what stands out, projected, convex hull), `hull()`, `insideConvex()`. `PhoneBody.outlinePoly` is the polygon for the pose drawn now, built from `frontMatrix` so the 2D phone's direct draw is right.
- `QuickPanel.qml` `besidePhone`: a MouseArea over `hitRect`, z -100, on while the panel is open (Quick and Apps). A press nothing on the phone took reaches it: on the outline (3 units of slack) it lets go, beside it the panel closes.
- Not done: letting the click through to the window below (a mask shaped like the phone would be strips of `Region`, moved every frame of the bounce). docs/PHONE-CLICK-AWAY.md.

## Unreleased — Keybinds panel: change SekaiPop's shortcuts on a keyboard slab (issue #16)

The Settings > Keys page became its own panel (modules/sekai/keybinds/): a keyboard slab with a display strip, Board and List views, modifier keys as layers. The logic is unchanged (KeybindService, KeyBinds.js); only the face is new. Opened from Settings > Hyprland > Keybinds (the phone closes first) or `ipc call keybinds toggle`. tests/keybinds-test covers the board maths.

### Original notes (Settings > Keys)

**Run on a live Quickshell** (Quickshell 0.3.1, Qt 6.11, Hyprland 0.56.2), with the shell on both of Hyprland's config formats: first `hyprland.conf`, then the Lua config. By hand: recording a combination, Set it by hand, taking keys over from another action and from a Hyprland bind, reset one, reset all, the panel closing while recording. Scripted against the live Hyprland: under `hyprland.conf`, a bind written, kept across a reload and removed; under Lua, Toggle equalizer moved from SUPER + M to SUPER + ALT + E (`hl.unbind` dropped the old key, `hyprctl configerrors` empty) and reset (SUPER + M back). Checked: `tests/keys-test/keys_unit.js` and `keys_script_test.js` (new); the other JS tests give the same results as `main` (the same 8 fail). **Not checked:** niri (the page only says keys are niri's there), a screen reader, and a VNC client sending Super (most viewers keep it; Set it by hand is the way round).

- What it is: a seventh Settings page, Keys, between Hyprland and About (the menu is four over three now; keys 1-7). Every shortcut that calls `qs -c sekaipop ipc call ...` (32 actions, the five that ship without a key included), grouped like the cheat sheet. Click a row and press the new keys; it is saved and works at once. A conflict asks before taking keys from another action (left without a key) or a Hyprland bind (switched off until the action is reset or moves on). Reset per row and Reset all (with the list of what goes back).
- How: `modules/common/functions/KeyBinds.js` (pure, node-tested) turns `hyprctl -j binds` and the saved state into rows, finds conflicts, and writes the block; `services/KeybindService.qml` reads and writes through `scripts/hyprland/keybinds.sh`. The state lives in the override file itself, between `sekaipop:keys:begin` / `end`, as JSON on a comment line, so nothing can drift apart: `sekaipop_overrides.lua` (Lua config, the Hyprland page's file) or `sekaipop_overrides.conf` (`hyprland.conf`, which has to `source` it). A change writes the block and reloads Hyprland; never live `keyword` / `eval` calls, because Hyprland already reloads when a sourced file changes and a live call on top bound everything twice.
- Recognising a bind: under Lua every bind reports `__lua`, so by its description's sentence ("Shell: Toggle apps", the convention `hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua` already uses for the cheat sheet); under `hyprland.conf` by the `ipc call <target> <function>` in its command, whose fallbacks (`|| swaylock`) are kept when it is rebound. An action on several keys shows them all, and new keys replace them all. A rebind remembers the keys the config had, so a reset (or a second change) unbinds the right ones.
- Recording pauses every shortcut: an empty submap whose only bind is Escape, so SUPER + A reaches the panel instead of opening Apps, and Escape always gets the shortcuts back. Closing the panel resumes them too (`QuickPanel.onOpenChanged`). The panel keeps its focus grab: taking the keyboard Exclusive cleared the grab, which closes the phone.
- Small changes beside it: `SettingsPage` has an `overlay` slot above the scroll area (and above `WheelScroll`, so a modal's scrim gets the wheel); `SettingChip.fontSize`; `IconMap` maps `warning`; the Settings banner can have its own `headerSub`.
- Departs from the design handoff: the editor is a modal over the page instead of opening inline in the card (inline, it could open half off-screen and be scrolled away while the shortcuts were paused); the setup text for `hyprland.conf` says its line is this page's own (the handoff said the Hyprland page's setup line covers it, true only for Lua); "Until then, SUPER + / shows the cheat sheet" names the cheat sheet's real key and is left out when it has none; niri gets a short note instead of controls.
- Does not do yet: window, workspace and hardware keys (out of scope by design); mouse buttons as keys; telling apart two binds of one action (they move together); live preview of a change before saving.
- **To check by eye:** the Keys menu tile at narrow widths; the modal on the smallest phone size; Light mode (the keycaps are white there).

## Unreleased — The Notifications tile (do-not-disturb) is kept across restarts

**Run on a live Quickshell** (Quickshell 0.3.1, Qt 6.11, Hyprland 0.56.2), partly: `notify.on` set to `false` with `ipc call options set`, the shell restarted twice, and `ipc call options get notify.on` still printed `false` after each. **Not checked by eye:** clicking the tile itself, that the phone stays down for a `notify-send` while it is off, and wearing a preset. Checked: `node tests/notify-dnd-test/dnd_persist_test.js` (new). The other JS tests give the same results as before the change (60 pass; the same 6 fail on `main` without it).

- Found: `QuickSource.notificationsOn` was a plain `property bool notificationsOn: true`, so turning the Quick Panel's Notifications tile off (do-not-disturb: no rising phone, no buzz, no popup) lasted only until the shell restarted. Every login, and every Ctrl+Super+R, announced notifications again until the tile was turned off by hand. Issue #3 runs into the same thing ("you could just disable/enable notifications but that can be annoying to do manually").
- Changed: the tile is the setting `notify.on` (default `true`, so nothing changes for anyone who never turns it off). `notificationsOn` is now read-only and bound to `SettingsService.flag("notify.on")`, and `toggleNotifications()` toggles the setting, which saves it to `settings.json`. `DesktopPresetService.keepExact` gains `notify.on`, so wearing a preset keeps the choice, like the 24-hour clock. Files: `services/QuickSource.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `tests/notify-dnd-test/dnd_persist_test.js` (new).
- Not changed: what do-not-disturb does (`NotificationSource`: critical notifications still break through; everything still lands in the list), the tile and the Settings → Quick toggle themselves. Nothing here does what #3 asks (hold notifications back while a window is fullscreen); this only makes the manual switch stick.
- **Risk:** in the moment before `settings.json` is read at start-up the tile reads the default (on), so a notification arriving in that first instant could still announce itself once.
- **To check:** turn the Notifications tile off; `qs -c sekaipop ipc call options get notify.on` prints `false`; restart the shell (Ctrl+Super+R): the tile is still off; `notify-send test` does not raise the phone and shows in the list; wear a preset: still off; turn it on again: notifications announce as before. From a shell: `qs -c sekaipop ipc call options set notify.on false|true`.
