## Unreleased — Performance pass 3: one `cava` for the bar pill and the desktop visualizer (nothing visible changes)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Quickshell, `qmllint` or GPU in the place this was done): nothing was timed, so no speed-up is claimed beyond what is counted below. Checked: bracket balance of both changed files; `node tests/perf-test/cava_share_unit.js` (new): the pill's numbers and the widget's numbers are identical to the old paths over 20,000 random cava lines, and the demand count is right over 2,000 random runs x 80 steps (widgets added, flipped, loaded twice, destroyed, in any order of signals; Game Mode and lock respected); `tests/perf-test/demand_unit.js` still passes. Marked **reasoned only** (read and traced, not run).

- Found: with the bar's media-pill visualizer on (`VisualizerService.pillStyle` is not `none`) and the desktop visualizer on one of the end4 styles (anything but the Sekai card), **two `cava` processes with the same config (`scripts/cava/cava-pc.conf`) ran side by side**: `PcCavaService.qml` for the pill and a second one inside `VisualizerWidget.qml`. Two 60 fps streams, two parsers, the same 50 numbers.
- Changed (**reasoned only**): `PcCavaService.qml` is now the one tap for both. The widget adds itself to a count (`PcCavaService.demand(+1 / -1)`, the same `counted` pattern as `statusDemand` in `Bar.qml`) while it wants audio, and reads the bands from `PcCavaService.raw`; cava runs while the pill OR any widget wants it, and still never in Game Mode or while locked. `points` (scaled, what the pill reads) is only filled while the pill itself wants the tap, so a widget-only tap does not repaint the bar; `raw` is the numbers as cava sent them, a fresh array per line while a widget wants it (as the widget's own cava gave it, so its engine's signal timing is unchanged). Files: `services/PcCavaService.qml`, `modules/sekai/visualizer/VisualizerWidget.qml` (`cavaPc` Process replaced by `tapDemand`; `pcEngine.raw` is now a binding), `tests/perf-test/cava_share_unit.js` (new).
- Not changed: the Sekai card's own tap (the note analyzer, or `cava-sekai.conf` as its fallback) is a different config and stays in the widget. The widget still stops its tap in Edit Desktop and when Desktop Focus hides it; the shared cava keeps running in those moments only if the pill wants it.
- **Risk:** if the widget's `want` flips while the shared tap is already running for the pill, the widget's first frames come from the running stream (no start-up delay), so the visualizer can show levels a few frames earlier than before: not a regression. If the visualizer draws nothing with an end4 style: `qs -c sekaipop log`, and check `pgrep -a cava` shows one process.
- **To undo:** put `/services/PcCavaService.qml` and `VisualizerWidget.qml` from the previous zip back (the new test file can stay).
- **How to measure:** with music playing, the pill's visualizer on (Settings -> Desktop) and an end4 desktop style (Ribbons, Scope, Pulse, Tide...) on: `pgrep -c cava` should print 1 (it printed 2 before); `pidstat -p $(pgrep -d, cava) 5 6` shows the second cava's CPU gone; then switch the pill off (`ipc call visualizer pillStyle none`): still 1 while the desktop style is on, 0 when both are off; start a track, stop it: 0.

## Unreleased — Clock: `place()` no longer runs inside the window's own size change (experiment, Clock only)

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Seen in a real `qs -c sekaipop log`: "Binding loop detected for property implicitWidth" for the Clock, Weather, To-do, Calendar, Palette and Lyrics widgets (the Visualizer is not listed), 7 at start-up and 6 more (one per widget) every time Edit Desktop was opened and closed. **Cause (reasoned, not proven):** `implicitWidth: win.boxW` reads `card.x` / `card.width`; `onWidthChanged` called `place()` straight away, and `place()` sets `card.x`, while the size was still being worked out.
- Changed: `ClockWidget.qml` only: `onWidthChanged` / `onHeightChanged` call `Qt.callLater(win.place)` instead of `win.place()` (`settle.restart()` as before). **To check:** `qs -c sekaipop log | grep -c "ClockWidget.*Binding loop"` after a restart and after opening / closing Edit Desktop (it should stop growing); then look for the Clock jumping or showing at the wrong spot for a frame when Edit Desktop starts or ends. If it does, or if the warning stays, put the two old lines back (the comment in the file says how). **Confirmed on the real shell:** the Clock's warnings stopped (0 at start and after Edit Desktop); the other five kept logging, one more each per Edit Desktop open. **Then applied to the other five** (`WeatherCardWidget`, `TodoWidget`, `CalendarCardWidget`, `PaletteWidget`, `LyricsWidget`): the same two lines. Not yet confirmed for them. Lyrics logged twice at start-up, so it may keep one: its card also calls `place()` from its own `onWidthChanged` / `onHeightChanged`.

## Audit notes (Performance pass 3): what was checked and found already in good shape

Static read of the whole tree (no run): every `PanelWindow` / `PopupWindow` (`visible` gates, size, mask), every repeating `Timer`, every infinite animation, every `Process` that is started with `running: true`, `HyprlandData`, the Discord bridge, the cava taps.
- Windows: the bar and dock are strips (`compactWindow`), the clock / user card / to-do / lyrics / visualizer / calendar / palette / weather / image widgets are card-sized at rest, the hot edge, OSD, keyboard, notifications, region preview and reload popup are card-sized, and the full-screen ones (Overview, Cheat sheet, Palette tool, Edit guides, Launcher phone's catcher) are `visible` only while open. Still full-screen at rest: the wallpaper (needed), and the **sticky-notes layers** (`StickyNotesWidget.qml`: one full-screen transparent window for the desktop layer and one on `Top` for pinned notes, with an input mask, mapped whenever notes are shown). Same class as the old clock / to-do windows, **not changed** (a note is dragged anywhere on the screen, so a card-sized window needs the Edit-Desktop-style grow/shrink work and a live test).
- Timers: all 40 repeating timers have a state gate (`running:` on the screen being open, media playing, a count of watchers...), or are started on demand (hold-to-repeat keys, the Discord reconnect timer, the display revert countdown).
- Animations: all 12 infinite animations are gated on the state they show (attention, loading, talking, open...).
- Processes: `hyprctl clients / monitors` run only while the Overview / region picker is open and on events (80 ms debounce); the Discord bridge runs only while a Discord client window exists; cava / analyzer run only with audio wanted.
- **Eager trees (not changed, needs a live test):** `EditTray` (790 lines + five boards), `Overview`, `CheatSheet`, `OnScreenKeyboard`, `NotificationPopup`, `OSDPopup`, `ReloadPopup`, `DesktopMenu` and the Quick Panel's phone are built at shell start, hidden by `visible`. Wrapping them in a `LazyLoader` (as `EditGuides` is) would free their trees, but each one has start-up work in `Connections` / `onVisibleChanged` that a later-built instance would miss (`EditTray` sets its tab and keyboard focus from `EditMode.onActiveChanged`), so it needs a run on the real shell to check the first open. Suggested order, smallest first: `ReloadPopup`, `OSDPopup`, `NotificationPopup`, `CheatSheet`, `Overview`, `EditTray`.

- Added: **Windows cursors (.cur / .ani, also animated ones) can be imported: they become a normal cursor theme and show up in Settings -> Desktop -> Icons & cursor.** The converter was run on cursors built for the test and the written files were loaded back with the real libXcursor (sizes, hot spots, frame order and delays all as expected); **the Settings button, the chooser and the phone stepping aside were not run on a live Quickshell** (no Quickshell / Hyprland here), and the QML was only read through (no `qmllint`).
  - `scripts/theming/win-cursor.py` (new, Python 3 standard library only, nothing to install): takes a `.zip`, a folder, an `install.inf` or one `.cur` / `.ani` and writes `~/.local/share/icons/<name>/` (`cursors/` + `index.theme`). Which file is which cursor comes from the pack's `install.inf` when it has one (the order Windows itself uses), else from the file names ("Normal Select", "Busy", "Link", `aero_ns`...), and `--map Role=file` fixes any of them; `--dry-run` only prints the table. Reads 32-bit alpha, 24 / 8 / 4 / 1-bit with the AND mask, and PNG inside a `.cur`; for an `.ani` it keeps the frames, the `seq` order and the `rate` timing (or the display rate). Every cursor is written at 24, 32, 48 and 64 px (`--sizes`), resampled in premultiplied colour so the edge does not darken, with the hot spot scaled with it, so the cursor size stepper works. A role the pack does not have (no Hand, no Help...) uses a close one, the arrow in the end; each role also gets the other names toolkits ask for (`default`, `pointer`, `ew-resize`, `grabbing`, the old hash names...). A theme folder that this script did not make is never overwritten.
  - `scripts/theming/icon-cursor.sh import <pack> [name]` runs it (prints the theme name); `scripts/theming/pick-cursor-pack.sh` is the file chooser (kdialog / zenity / yad, same order and `files.chooser` setting as the other pickers; picking one `.cur` / `.ani` out of a folder of cursors takes the whole folder).
  - `IconCursorService.qml`: `importPack(path, apply)`, `pickPack()`, `importNote`; IPC `ipc call appearance importcursor <path> <true|false>` (true also switches to it) and `ipc call appearance pickcursor`. After an import the theme list is rescanned. **Settings -> Desktop -> Icons & cursor -> Import Windows cursors** opens the chooser and uses the cursor when it is made; `QuickPanel.qml` closes the phone while the chooser is open (like the wallpaper folder chooser) and the Settings come back afterwards.
  - **Limits:** Hyprland and GTK / XWayland apps play the frames of an animated cursor, but a program that reads `XCURSOR_THEME` once at start keeps its old cursor until it is restarted (same as any cursor change); Flatpak apps only see the theme if they may read `~/.local/share/icons`; a cursor whose Windows file uses "invert the screen" pixels gets those pixels drawn black.
  - Tests: `python3 tests/cursor-test/win_cursor_test.py` (64 checks: every bitmap kind, hot spots, premultiplied alpha, animation order and delays, `install.inf`, zip, `--map`, `icon-cursor.sh import` / `list cursors`, the chooser script with a stub chooser).
  - **To check:** (1) Settings -> Desktop -> Icons & cursor -> Import Windows cursors: the phone closes, pick a pack's `.zip` (or its `install.inf`), the Settings come back saying "Added <name>", the cursor changes and the name is in the Cursor theme list; (2) an animated pack: the busy / working cursors play (try `hyprctl setcursor` or open a terminal and wait on a slow command); (3) `ipc call appearance importcursor /path/Pack.zip false` only adds it; (4) cancel the chooser: nothing changes; (5) a zip with no cursors in it: the line under the button says so. To go back to the old cursor: pick it in the Cursor theme list; to remove an imported one, delete its folder in `~/.local/share/icons`.
- Added: **the shell tells you when a new version is out (a notification), and looks by itself.** **Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Quickshell / Hyprland here): the real `announce()` is cut out of `UpdateService.qml` and run by the new `tests/update-test/update_notify_test.js` (15 checks), `update_check_test.sh` gained the `rev` / `seen` checks (all pass), and the QML was only read through (no `qmllint`).
  - `UpdateService.qml`: while `updates.shellNotify` is on (default **on**) it looks once 40 s after the shell starts and then every `updates.shellHours` hours (default 6). A look that finds a newer version sends **one** notification for it (`notify-send`, so it shows on the phone like the others) and remembers which version in `~/.cache/pjsekai/update-seen` (`update-check.sh seen`), so it does not repeat for the same version, also after a restart; the next newer version sends another. A look you asked for (opening the page, Check for updates) counts as seen and sends nothing. Nothing is sent for an installed copy with no version mark yet (it can't tell if it is behind), and not while Game Mode is on (it is tried again at the next look, nothing is marked). A look from the timers keeps the shown answer instead of flashing "Checking...". With the setting off nothing touches the network until you press Check for updates.
  - `update-check.sh check` prints `rev=` (the newest version's short id) in every mode, and `seen [id]` reads / stores the last version told about.
  - `SettingsService.qml`: `updates.shellNotify` (true) and `updates.shellHours` (6). Both start with `updates.`, so presets leave them alone (`keepPrefix`). **Settings -> About / Update -> Update**: a **Tell me about updates** switch and, while it is on, a **Look every** slider (1-48 h). `SettingsFace.qml`: the About tab's badge shows as soon as a look finds an update (not only after the page is opened), but not for "can't tell which version".
  - **To check:** (1) push a commit to `main`, wait for the next look (or lower **Look every** to 1 h and restart the shell): one "SekaiPop update available" notification; (2) restart the shell: no second one; (3) push another commit: a new one; (4) the About tab shows its badge without opening the page; (5) switch **Tell me about updates** off: no more looks and no notifications (`ps` shows no `git ls-remote` every few hours); (6) with Game Mode on, no notification until it is off and the next look comes. To go back to "only when you look": `ipc call options set updates.shellNotify false`.
- Fixed: **About / Update -> "Update now" works on an installed copy, not only on a git checkout.** It used to need the shell folder to be a git clone, which `install.sh` never leaves (it copies the folder and deletes `.git`), and the repo keeps the shell in `sekaipop/`, so the page said "Nothing to compare with". **Not run on a live Quickshell**; `scripts/system/update-check.sh` was run against a local fake remote with the new `tests/update-test/update_check_test.sh` (11 checks, all pass), and the QML edits were only read through (no `qmllint` here).
  - `scripts/system/update-check.sh`: three modes. (1) A git checkout: the shell folder is a clone, **or the `sekaipop/` folder of a SekaiPop clone** (new); same fetch / `pull --ff-only` as before. (2) An installed copy (new): `check` asks GitHub for its newest commit (`git ls-remote`, nothing downloaded) and compares it with the commit in `.source` next to `shell.qml`; `apply` downloads that commit into a temporary folder, builds the new folder beside the old one, keeps the old one as `sekaipop.previous` (one copy) and swaps them, then the shell reloads. Rebuilds the shaders when Qt is older than 6.12, like `install.sh`. Refuses when the folder is a symlink. (3) the VERSION-URL and "nothing to compare with" modes, as before. `apply` now exits non-zero when it fails (it used to exit 0 at the end of the script, so a failed `git pull` still reloaded the shell).
  - `install.sh` writes `.source` (`commit=`, `repo=`, `ref=`) into the installed copy when it installs from a git checkout (what `get.sh` does). A copy with no `.source` (a zip, or installed before this change) is reported as "behind, no version mark yet" once; after the first Update now it has one. Fork / branch: `SEKAIPOP_REPO`, `SEKAIPOP_REF` or `~/.config/pjsekai/update-repo`.
  - `UpdateService.qml` (`managed`, `unknown`, `canApply`) and `SettingsPageAbout.qml` (texts, the commit chip, the Update now button) follow the new modes. Only the shell's files are updated; Hyprland config and packages still come from `install.sh`.
  - **To check:** Settings -> About / Update on an installed copy: the first time it says there is no version mark yet and offers Update now; press it, the shell reloads, open the page again: "You're up to date" and a `main @ <commit>` chip. Push a commit to GitHub, press Check for updates: "A newer commit is on GitHub". To go back: `~/.config/quickshell/sekaipop.previous` is the copy from before the last update.
- Changed: **Lyrics widget: less work while it is on, nothing looks or moves differently.** Reasoned from the code, **not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt / Quickshell where this was made); the node tests give the same results as before (the same 6 that failed before still fail, none new). Each change is marked **reasoned only**.
  - **The frame clock stops when nothing can move** (`LyricsWidget.qml`: `songEnd`, `animating`; **reasoned only**). The `FrameAnimation` that drives the words ran at the screen's refresh rate for as long as anything was playing, also while the middle only said "no synced lyrics for this one" / "looking for the words..." (a video, a podcast, a track nobody has), and again after the last line had been sung. Now it runs while there are lines and the clock is less than 3 s past the end of the last one (the last two lines and the sing-along voice are counted; 3 s is longer than anything that settles: the held line lets go in 0.7 s, the marker shrinks in 0.26 s, the diamonds fly for 0.6 s). The 250 ms poll still keeps the clock right, so a seek back or the words arriving start the frames again at once. Edit Desktop's made-up verse always runs. **To undo:** `running: win.live && (win.demo || win.playing)` on the `FrameAnimation`.
  - **The four sync keys are built only while pointed at** (`LyricsWidget.qml`: `syncKeys` is a `Loader` now; **reasoned only**). They were 4 stickers = 56 text items kept for the whole song, invisible. They are built when the pointer comes onto the lyrics and let go 180 ms after it leaves (when the fade has ended); same place, same fade. The first pointing at them builds four short words, a few ms inside the fade. **To undo:** put the old `Row { id: syncKeys ... }` back (the keys unchanged inside it).
  - **A word no longer keeps a text item just to measure itself** (`LyricsWord.qml`; **reasoned only**). The hidden `probe` is gone; the colour layer (`ink`) is the same string in the same font and gives the same size. One text item less per word (15 -> 14), and one less layout of the string each time a line is built. **To undo:** put `probe` back and point `implicitWidth` / `implicitHeight` / `box` at it.
  - **The lyrics window is a small box around the card, not a full-screen layer** (`LyricsWidget.qml`: `compact` no longer asks for `EditMode.compactWindows`; **reasoned only**). The other widgets are unchanged (that switch is still off by default for them). Known cost, from the comment in `EditMode.qml`: pressing Done in Edit Desktop may make the lyrics jump a little. **To undo:** put `&& EditMode.compactWindows` back in the `compact` line.
  - **Optional, would change the look or the feel (not done):** (1) draw the sticker of a word that is not being sung once into a texture (`layer.enabled`) instead of 13 text items: the biggest cut of nodes, but the text is then scaled as a picture (softer at the side lines' scale and at 1.5x shell scale unless the texture is made larger); (2) cap the frame clock at 60 fps on a 120 / 144 Hz screen: half the work there, the singing fill moves in steps of 16 ms instead of 7; (3) the same compact window for the other widgets (`ipc call desktopedit compactwindows true`); (4) `gc()` a few seconds after each song's lyrics settle (frees what the providers sent, but a collection can drop a frame while playing).
  - **How to measure:** `sh tests/perf-test/perf-baseline.sh 60` before and after, same conditions, twice: (a) play something with no synced lyrics (a video) with the widget on: `cpu_self` and `gpu_*` should drop to near idle; (b) a song with lyrics, hands off: `cpu_self` while singing is about what it was (the words still move every frame). For memory: `grep -E 'Rss|Pss' /proc/$(pidof quickshell)/smaps_rollup` after the first song, after ten songs, then `ipc call lyrics hide` and again (what drops on hide is the widget's own; what stays is the allocator's and the GPU driver's).
- Added: **the equalizer handheld (Sekai Pad) now wears the shell theme, including Wallpaper (matugen).** It was the one part of the shell still painted in fixed colours (teal shell, indigo screen); pick Leo/need, Vivid BAD SQUAD, Monochrome, a custom palette or the matugen theme and the whole console follows, live (nothing to restart: a theme change, or matugen finishing a new wallpaper, repaints it). **Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Quickshell / Qt here): checked with the new `tests/pad-theme-test/pad_palette_test.js` (runs `PadPalette.qml`'s own expressions for the six units, Monochrome and four matugen-style palettes: valid colours, readable ink and print, distinct lane groups and dome keys, no tint in Monochrome, no hard-coded colour left in `equalizer/`, a repaint connection on every canvas), the bracket balance of every changed file, and the rest of `tests/` (the same 6 tests that failed before still fail, none new).
  - New `modules/common/PadPalette.qml` (a pragma Singleton like `LockPalette.qml`, used as `PadPalette.foo`): every colour of the console is a hue taken from the theme plus a fixed saturation and lightness, so the light and shade stay the same in every theme. The shell, wall, cavity and sockets take the hue of the accent `A`; the screen, bezel, MEDIA key, d-pad, wheel and the pearl parts take the hue of `border`; the lit gradient (AUTO, VOL, the curve, chips, the cartridge label) is `B` -> a softened `A`; bass / mids / treble, the four dome keys and the LED are `A` turned round the colour wheel (mids on `A`, bass 156 degrees, treble 85), so the three groups stay apart in any theme. The numbers were fitted to Virtual Singer: the default theme gives the old colours to within 2/255 (the test checks 51 of them), so the default look is unchanged. A theme with little colour (a nearly grey wallpaper) gives a nearly grey console, and the lane groups keep a floor of colour so they can still be told apart. Monochrome is grey throughout (the three groups differ by lightness).
  - Readability is kept in every theme, not just the default: the dark text on the lit gradient (the AUTO key, chips, the cartridge label) lifts a stop that is too dark for it (a blue is, at the same lightness) to 4.5 : 1; the text printed on the shell (SELECT BAND, the key legend, SEKAI-EQ) is darkened to read, or switched to light print on a shell too dark for dark print (Leo/need, Nightcord), with the embossing in the opposite tone, and printed bolder (90 % instead of 65 %) where it still reads less well.
  - Every canvas (`PadBody`, `PadScreen` x3, `PadWheel`, `PadShoulder`, `PadCartridge` x2, `PadDpad`, `PadDome`) paints again on a theme change (a `Connections` to `PadPalette`); the dome caps already did through `capColor` / `wallColor`. `PadMath.js`: `GROUPS` / `LIGHT` / `DARK` are gone (the colours are in `PadPalette`), new `PM.alpha(color, a)`. Layout, geometry, depth vector and every animation are untouched.
  - Not themed on purpose: the white of the glass, highlights and text on the screen, the black shading of the wheel and the dome caps, the screen's black backing. The console ignores Light / Dark / Darker (it is a physical object) and follows only the theme. This replaces "still coloured on purpose: the Sekai Pad (EQ) artwork" in the Monochrome entry below.
  - **To check:** open the equalizer (`qs -c sekaipop ipc call eq open`) and cycle the theme (`ipc call theme cycle`, or Ctrl+Shift+T) with the card open: (1) Virtual Singer looks as before; (2) each unit recolours the shell, screen, keys, wheel and cartridge at once, nothing stays teal / indigo; (3) Monochrome: no colour anywhere except what the test lists above; (4) Wallpaper (matugen): change the wallpaper with the card open, the console eases to the new colours when matugen finishes (needs `matugen`, see the README); (5) read the printed text on the shell and the AUTO key in Leo/need, Nightcord and Vivid BAD SQUAD; (6) drag a jewel, press the dome keys, the d-pad and the wheel: they still sink and repaint with the new colours; (7) the first open after a theme change is not slower (the canvases are repainted when the theme changes, not on open). To go back to the fixed colours: `git checkout -- modules/sekai/equalizer` and delete `modules/common/PadPalette.qml`.
- Added: **Boidu (Better Lyrics), YouTube Music and SimpMusic as lyrics sources, in the order Boidu, LyricsPlus, LRCLIB, YouTube Music, SimpMusic, NetEase, QQ Music, Kugou; and the source can be chosen from Settings.** **Not run on a live Quickshell or against the live services** (no network where it was written): the requests follow the services' own docs and examples, and `qs -c sekaipop log` shows each as `[lyrics] ...`. Checked with `tests/lyrics-test` (new cases in `lyrics_unit.js` and `lyrics_source_test.js`) and the rest of `tests/` (the same 5 tests that failed before still fail, none new).
  - **Boidu** (`pBoidu`, `LM.ttmlToLrc`): `GET /getLyrics?s=<title>&a=<artist>&al=<album>&d=<seconds>` at `api.betterlyrics.org` (then the old `lyrics-api.boidu.dev`), answer `{ ttml, score }`. The TTML has a start and an end for every syllable, which is read into the word timing the widget already uses (so how long each word is held and how fast it is sung come from the file); the backing voice becomes the line's "sings along" row, translations / romanizations are left out. It is first in the order, so it wins when two sources are as good, and a word-timed answer it is sure of (`score` 0.6 or more, or from its cache, which sends none) is used as it came: it is neither dropped where the synced text of another source reads a little differently nor moved onto that text's timing (other sources' word timing still has to agree with the synced text, and is moved to it). **Its limit:** a song that nobody has played through the Better Lyrics extension is not cached, and a fresh fetch needs an API key, which are not being given out (401): the row says "Not found" and the next source shows.
  - **YouTube Music** (`pYoutubeMusic`): the video's id (out of the player's address when it is a YouTube one, else the first search result that is the song) -> `/next` names the lyrics tab -> `/browse` has the timed lines (line by line). Not given out in every country.
  - **SimpMusic** (`pSimpmusic`): `GET /v1/<videoId>`, else `/v1/search?q=`; takes the entry cut closest to the track's length and its word timing when an entry has it (written from the shape of Musixmatch's rich sync, unconfirmed). 30 requests a minute are allowed from one address.
  - **Choosing the source:** Settings -> Desktop -> under the Lyrics switch, **Lyrics source** (Auto or one provider) and **Word by word**; the picker under the lyrics (the "sources" key) now shows a hint under each name; `ipc lyrics source <name>` takes `boidu`, `lyricsplus`, `lrclib`, `youtubemusic`, `simpmusic`, `netease`, `qq`, `kugou` and also `neteasecloudmusic`, `qqmusic`, `betterlyrics`. Turning Word by word off now also shows a song that was kept with its word timing line by line, and looks for the song again either way.
  - Songs kept as line-synced before this are asked once more (cache `rev` 2), in the background, in case a new source has them word by word. A saved choice (`source`) is still valid.
- Changed: **the desktop sleeps while the screen is locked, and wakes when you unlock; the lockscreen copy in `LockRing` is no longer kept built all the time.** **Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Quickshell / Hyprland here): checked with `qmllint` (Qt 6, no syntax errors in any changed file), the new `tests/lock-sleep-test/lock_sleep_test.js` (68 checks: the phase table, every widget loader's turn, the wiring, brackets) and every other test in `tests/` (same results as before: the 5 that already failed still fail, none new). Nothing was timed, so **no saving is claimed in numbers**; what each change stops holding / doing is below.
  - `services/LockState.qml`: new `desktopAsleep`, true only in the `locked` phase (the session lock is engaged and the compositor draws nothing but the lock surface) and only while `lock.sleepDesktop` is on. It is false while `locking` and `unlocking`, because the real desktop is on screen under the ring then.
  - `shell.qml`: `widgetsAllowed` now also needs `!LockState.desktopAsleep`, so while locked **all eleven desktop-widget loaders let go** (clock, user card, weather card, calendar card, to-do, palette card, lyrics, visualizer, sticky notes (both layers) and custom images): their QML trees, images, models and timers are freed, and the visualizer's and the lyrics' own audio / fetch work goes with them (it lives inside the widgets). On unlock they come back **one after another, 90 ms apart** (`wakeStep`, `woke(n)`: clock first, images last, about 0.8 s in all), so ten widgets are not created in the one frame in which the unlock ring is opening the desktop. A start, Game Mode and the setting being off are unchanged (`wakeStep` is 99: nothing waits). Positions, "shown" switches and contents live in the services and are not touched. About 1.5 s after the lock engages the JS engine is asked to collect what they held (`gc()`; it runs while nothing is on screen).
  - `shell.qml`: **Edit Desktop's guides (`EditGuides`) are built only while Edit Desktop has a monitor** (`LazyLoader` on `EditMode.screenName !== ""`, which stays set until the card has grown back, so the exit animation still plays). They were a screen-sized window with an 833-line tree and ten repeaters per monitor, built and unmapped the whole time you were not editing. Cost: they are created as Edit Desktop starts (it already builds every widget then).
  - `modules/sekai/lock/LockHost.qml` / `LockRing.qml`: **`LockRing` kept one complete lockscreen (the Gacha or Calendar look with its wallpaper decode, models and timers) per monitor for the whole life of the shell** to be able to play the lock ring. `LockHost` has a new `live` switch (default true, so the real lock surface is unchanged) and `LockRing` sets it to `LockState.phase !== "idle"`: the look exists from the start of a lock to the end of an unlock and not otherwise. The ring still waits for `wallpaperReady` before it starts; its fallback timer is 900 ms (was 400) because the first lock after a start now decodes the wallpaper then. The Calendar look used to ask `LockCropService` for the picture map when it was built (at start); `LockHost` now asks for it itself when the wallpaper, the style or `lock.crop` changes, so the first lock is not slower for that.
  - `services/PcCavaService.qml`: the bar's cava tap is not run while asleep (it starts again at unlock if a player is playing).
  - `modules/sekai/background/WallpaperLayer.qml`: with mpvpaper, **the process is stopped while asleep, not only paused** (`restartOnPause` is also true then). A paused mpvpaper still holds the video in RAM and VRAM. The still frame under it shows meanwhile; the process starts again when the unlock begins (paused on its first frame) and plays, from the start, when the ring is done. The built-in player is only paused as before.
  - New setting `lock.sleepDesktop` (default **on**; a desktop preset leaves it alone): Settings -> General -> Launcher & lock -> **Sleep the desktop while locked**. `ipc call options set lock.sleepDesktop false` turns it off.
  - **Not changed on purpose:** the bars and docks keep their windows while locked: they reserve space (`exclusionMode`), so destroying them makes every tiled window grow and then shrink again around the unlock, and the dock has to be built after the bars (see `docks` in `shell.qml`). Their timers are already gated. The Quick Panel / Settings phone, the Overview, the launcher phone, the Palette tool, the cheat sheet and the on-screen keyboard are still built at start: their state and `ipc` handlers live inside them, so making them lazy needs those split from their windows, and the first open of each would then pay for building them (the open animations would start on a busy frame). That needs a live Quickshell to look at; see the report.
  - **To check:** (1) lock (`ipc call lock lock`): the ring plays over the full desktop as before, and `qs -c sekaipop log` shows no QML error; (2) with a widget (clock, to-do, visualizer) on, unlock: the desktop opens with the wallpaper and bar, the widgets appear one after another within about a second, none is missing or in the wrong place; (3) with music playing and the visualizer / lyrics on, lock for a minute: `ps` shows no `cava` / analyzer while locked, they are back after unlocking; (4) with a video wallpaper (mpvpaper): `pgrep mpvpaper` is empty while locked and one per monitor again after unlocking; (5) Settings -> General: the new switch; off = nothing stops while locked; (6) lock, unlock, lock again quickly (`relockPending`); (7) both lockscreen styles, the very first lock after a start and after changing the wallpaper (the picture is there when the ring ends); (8) Edit Desktop: open it, drag a widget (grid and guides show), close it (the guides fade as before); (9) compare `grep -E 'Rss|Pss' /proc/$(pidof quickshell)/smaps_rollup` idle, and while locked, before and after (`tests/perf-test/perf-baseline.sh`). To go back: `lock.sleepDesktop` off covers everything except the `LockRing` copy (`live: true` in `LockRing.qml` restores that) and `EditGuides` (the old `Variants` block).
- Added: **you can choose the folder the wallpapers come from.** A new folder button in the Wallpapers toolbar (left of "Jump to applied"; lit while a folder other than the default is in use) and Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> **Wallpaper folder** (with **Use the default folder** while a custom one is set) open a normal folder chooser (`scripts/wallpapers/pick-folder.sh`: kdialog, zenity or yad, the same order and the same Settings -> General picker choice as the avatar picker). New option `wallpaper.folder` (empty = `~/Pictures/Wallpapers`, `~` allowed); `WallpaperService.directory` is now that folder, and `directoryUrl` is its url for the three `FolderListModel`s (picker, service, first-run fallback), so spaces, `#` and `%` in the path work. Changing it re-lists the gallery from scratch, makes thumbnails for the new folder (a scan asked for while another is running is queued, not dropped) and Random draws from it; wallpapers that are already applied stay applied. The folder is a path on this machine, so desktop presets leave `wallpaper.folder` alone (`keepExact`). The phone steps aside while the chooser is open (so the dialog is not under it and gets the keyboard) and comes back on the same side / page afterwards (`WallpaperService.folderPicking`, a `Connections` in `LauncherPhone.qml` and `QuickPanel.qml`). The first-run wallpaper seed now waits for the saved settings (`WallpaperService.ready`), so a chosen folder is never ignored at start-up. IPC: `ipc call wallpaper getfolder|setfolder <path>|resetfolder|pickfolder`. **Not run on a live Quickshell** (only the chooser script was run, with a stub zenity). To check: open the Wallpapers, press the folder button: the phone closes, the chooser opens, pick a folder, the phone comes back showing it (subtitle ends with the folder, the button is lit); cancel the chooser: same phone, same folder; Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper folder: the Settings come back on the Desktop page; restart the shell: the folder is kept.
- Changed: **the Wi-Fi / Bluetooth look behind the status pill no longer runs when no bar shows the pill.** `StatusIconsService.qml` looked every 8 seconds from the moment the shell started (several child processes each time), also in Game Mode, with the pill switched off, or with the bar hidden. It now keeps a count of the bars that show the pill (`watchers`, `wanted`, `watch(delta)`), and its timer runs only while that count is above 0; the first bar to show the pill triggers a look at once. `Bar.qml`: a small `statusDemand` object adds this bar to the count while `statusBar.shows("status")` is true and takes it back when the pill goes away or the bar is destroyed (a `counted` flag keeps that safe in any order of signals). `ipc call statusicons get` now asks for a fresh look when nobody is watching, so the next call is right (the first call returns the old text). No change with the default setup (bar visible, pill on). To go back: `running: root.wanted` -> `running: true` on the timer in `StatusIconsService.qml`. Checked with `tests/perf-test/demand_unit.js` (the counting logic, 2000 random runs) and `qmllint` (no errors); not run on a live Quickshell.
- Changed: **a player that is "playing" silence no longer makes the bar's media wave repaint.** `PcCavaService.qml` assigned every cava frame to `points`, so a silent stream (all-zero frames, up to 60 a second) still told every media pill to repaint and re-blur its wave and to commit a bar frame. A frame equal to the last one is now skipped; what is on screen is the same. `demand_unit.js` checks that a reader sees the same sequence of states with and without the skip. Not run on a live Quickshell.
- Fixed: **the 2D Quick Panel phone showed a second side button while it turned to the Settings and back.** The 2D phone has one side button (the flat Power tab); while it turns, the side wall comes back and with it the 2.5D phone's lower, broad button, which popped up on the right edge. `PhoneBody.qml`: the lower button (its base and its stack of copies) is not drawn when `plain` is on (the 2D design: the Quick Panel's and the Settings phone's). The 2.5D phone is unchanged. The Power button itself still changes from the flat tab to the slim one on the wall for the turn (unchanged). `tests/phone-test/power_press_test.js` already failed before this (it expects no `powerStickNow` before the power copies, but the property is declared above them); not touched. **Not run on a live Quickshell.** To check: 2D style, open the phone, open Settings and go back: no lower side button should show at any point.
- Changed: **the dock's media card (the pop-up over the dock's media key) is redesigned, with seeking.** `DockMediaCard.qml`: a bigger cover (dims when paused), a two-line title, the artist, a chip with the player's name, a seek lane under them (click or drag to jump, the song is only told when you let go; a time chip over the playhead while dragging; the left time shows the time under the pointer while you hover; the wheel seeks 5 s), elapsed and total time, and five keys: back 10 s, previous, play / pause, next, forward 10 s. Keys the player cannot do are dimmed. A song with no known length hides the lane and the card gets shorter. **Not run on a live Quickshell.**
- Fixed: **the dock's name flag ("Search / click to open", "release to remove") had a much thinner white outline than the other blocks.** Its ring was `3.5` with a 3 px outline, so the white line was half a pixel; it is now `Theme.borderWidth + Theme.whiteLine` (3 + 2), the same outline + 2 px white line as the calendar's prev / next keys. `Dock.qml`, the flag's Pill. The flag's text and size are unchanged. **Not run on a live Quickshell.** To check: hover a dock key and compare the flag's white line with the calendar's prev / next keys in Dark, Darker and Light.
- Added: **a Monochrome (black & white) colour theme.** A seventh theme after the six units in the picker (Ctrl+Shift+T / Edit Desktop -> Theme, or `qs -c sekaipop ipc call theme set mono`), working in Light, Dark and Darker. `ThemeService.qml`: new `extraGroups` list with the `mono` palette (every colour a neutral grey: outline `#444444`, lit gradient white `#f4f4f4` -> silver `#bdbdbd`, dark surface `#1a1a1a`); it is kept out of `baseGroups` so the welcome screen's six-unit row and the transition colours' Virtual Singer reference are untouched. The palette carries `mono: true`, which the few places that work colours out from a hue read: `LockPalette.qml` (`hsl()` drops its saturation, or every hue-less grey would come out pink), `Theme.qml` (`Theme.mono`; `attributeColors` become five greys, so the settings cards, lock dots, notification icons, workspace badges and visualizer follow; `capWsColors` too), `WorkspaceBadges.qml` (reads `Theme.attributeColors` instead of its own copy of the same five pastels, so no other theme changes), `ShellBadge.qml` (outline = border colour in Monochrome, `#555473` otherwise), `AppThemeService.qml` (terminal magenta / cyan stay grey), `ThemeService.systemArgs()` (GTK / Qt / KDE use matugen's `scheme-monochrome` for it; a grey seed under tonal-spot still comes out faintly coloured), `discord-theme.sh` (no minimum tint when the palette has no hue). Every other theme produces exactly the same values as before (checked for the Discord CSS). `tests/capsule-test/capsule_source_test.js` updated for the new `capWsColors` line. **Not run on a live Quickshell.** Still coloured on purpose: warning / error reds, weather glyphs, the Sekai Pad (EQ) artwork, Discord and other brand colours, the classic wallpaper-transition ring (Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> Transition colours = Theme makes it grey). To check: pick Monochrome in each of Light / Dark / Darker and look at the bar, the Quick Panel phone, a bar popup, the lockscreen (Gacha and Calendar), the launcher, and `Settings`; nothing should show a colour cast.
- Changed: **the media card (the pop-up under the media pill) is redesigned, and you can now change the time in it.** `MediaPopup.qml`: the cover and the song sit on a header plate (`SekaiPlate`, the same header the Weather / Calendar / Battery cards use) in the cover's own two colours (the theme's accents when there is no cover), with a tilted cover keycap, the app's name, a tilted PLAYING / PAUSED / IDLE chip and the title and artist in the plate's dark ink. New **seek lane** under it: elapsed-time chip, a track with a diamond playhead in the cover's colours, and a length chip (click it to flip to the time left, like `-1:31`). Press on the track and drag, or just click, to jump: the playhead follows the pointer and the player is only told when you let go; the wheel over the track seeks 5 s; Left / Right keep doing 5 s. A song with no known length (a live stream) shows the lane dimmed and does nothing. Every block on the card (cover, state chip, time chips, the two small tool keys) wears the same outline + white ring as the prev / play / next keys (`ringWidth` 5); the small keys are a little bigger (38 x 32) so the ring fits. The position is asked for once a second, only while the card is open and the song is playing (and once on open, on a new song and when another player is picked, so a paused player shows the right time). The key row, tabs, Escape / Space / N / P and the focus grab are untouched. `tests/media-test/media_source_test.js` is updated: the old "no plate, no progress bars" rule is dropped on purpose and replaced by checks for the lane (only seeks when the player can, only sends on release, only polls while open and playing). **Not run on a live Quickshell.** To check: click the media pill. (1) The plate takes the cover's colours and eases to the next song's; (2) drag the playhead, then let go: the song jumps there and the playhead does not flick back; (3) click the track, scroll over it, click the length chip; (4) pause: PAUSED chip, cover pressed flush, lane stays; (5) a player with no length (a radio stream): lane dimmed; (6) two players: tabs above the plate, app name hidden on the plate; (7) Light, Dark, Darker and a group theme; (8) a bar on the bottom / side edge: the card still grows out of its pill (it is about 80 px taller than before).
# Changelog
- Changed: **the emoji picker is a phone card now, like Calc, Clip and Web, and its keys open it directly.** **Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Quickshell / Hyprland here): the changed files were only read through against the Calc / Clip cards they copy.
  - `modules/sekai/launcher/EmojiCard.qml`: rebuilt on `PhoneCard` instead of the white paper `ExtraFrame` with tape. A head (the category name, or `Matching "..."` while searching, with a count chip), a row of round category keys (`PhonePlate`, the picked one lit cyan with the thick outline), the grid in a `PhonePlate` tray (picked cell mint, hover a soft fill), and a sheet at the bottom with the big emoji, its name and two keys: a keyboard key (copy and type it, needs `wtype`) and **Copy**. It is handed the page's size and laid out for it, so it is no longer shrunk by the loader. Click, Shift+click, Enter, Shift+Enter, the arrows, Recent, `:smile` and `emoji red heart` search all work as before; the grid is 8 whole cells wide and has the Clip card's scroll cue in the phone's margin.
  - `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`: `ipc call launcherphone mode <calc|clip|color|emoji|web>` opens Apps with that card already up, and `toggleMode <name>` does the same but puts the phone away when that card is already showing. (`show`, `hide` and `toggle` are unchanged.)
  - `hypr/hyprland/keybinds.lua`: **SUPER+V** (Clip) and **SUPER+.** (Emoji) now call `toggleMode` instead of `show && sleep 0.4 && wtype '...'`, so there is no typing and no timing. The bind descriptions are unchanged, so the cheatsheet lists them as before. `hypr/README.md` test step 5 updated.
  - Not changed: `Emoji.js`, `ExtrasSource.qml` (copy, type, recents), `ColorCard.qml` (still the paper card), the Apps search triggers.
  - **To check:** SUPER+. opens Apps on the emoji picker; again closes it; Esc closes it. Open at 100% and a smaller monitor scale: the sheet is under the grid and not cut off. Pick one: it is copied (and typed with Shift / the keyboard key). Search `fire` and use the arrows. Light / Dark / Darker. SUPER+V the same for Clip.
- Added: **a source picker for the lyrics** (`LyricsSources.qml`). Point at the lyrics and press the new "sources" key (it also shows when no lyrics were found), or `ipc lyrics picker`: a card lists Auto and each provider with what it found for the song on now (WORD BY WORD, SYNCED, Not found, Unreachable + Try again); click a row to use that provider whenever it has the song, Auto for the best of each song. A click elsewhere or Esc closes it. **Not run on a live Quickshell** (the layout, the colours of the badges and where it opens under the lyrics have not been seen).
- Added: **the lyrics widget asks several providers and shows the best one for each song** (`LyricsService.qml`, `LyricsMath.js`). LyricsPlus, NetEase Cloud Music, LRCLIB, QQ Music and Kugou are asked at once; the first usable answer is shown right away, then the best is chosen: word by word over synced, then the one cut to the track's length, then the provider liked best. A word-timed text is only used when it is the same words as the synced one and agrees with its timing (it is shifted to match), so another song or another cut is never shown. What was chosen is cached with the provider's name. `ipc lyrics sources` lists each provider and what it found for the song on now; `ipc lyrics source <name|auto>` uses one provider whenever it has the song; `ipc lyrics words false` keeps line timing only. The widget's clock also shows the words 0.2 s early (`playerLead`). **Not run on a live Quickshell or against the live services** (no network where it was written; the NetEase, QQ, Kugou and LyricsPlus requests are written from memory): `qs -c sekaipop log` shows each request as `[lyrics] ...`.
- Fixed: **the pinned dock left a big gap (about 15 px) to the windows, and it differed with the dock style.** The zone the dock reserves (`Dock.qml`, the `exclusiveZone` Binding) included the dock's 14 px edge margin, but the compositor adds the layer's own margin to a zone, so it was counted twice. Checked against a screenshot (standing dock on the right, 69 px, gaps_out 14: windows stopped 98 px from the edge, 15 px from the dock; it was meant to be 1). The zone is now the pill's thickness less `gaps_out` plus `pinnedWindowGap`, which is now **8** (the Capsule bar's own gap) and is measured from what is visible: Float and Capsule from the pill's edge, Transparent from the keys (its empty padding on the windows' side, `pillPaddingV`, is taken off too), so the gap is the same in all three dock styles, with or without the running dots, on any edge, at any scale. Change `pinnedWindowGap` for a different gap (bigger = more room, 0 = touching). Test: `tests/dock-gap-test/dock_gap_test.js`. Not run on a live Quickshell: pin the dock and look at the space between a window and the dock in each style (Settings -> Desktop -> Dock -> Style).
- Added: **the lockscreen style is saved in a desktop preset** (Gacha / Calendar). A new `lock` part (`lockPart()` / `wearLock()` in `DesktopPresetService.qml`), the same way the phone and Overview parts work: `lock.style` stays in `keepExact` so a preset saved before this has no `lock` part and leaves your lockscreen alone, and the "you are wearing this one" tick ignores the part for those presets. The Calendar's other options (`lock.tone`, `lock.crop`, `lock.powerButtons`) are still yours under every preset. Test: `tests/preset-frames-test/frames_unit.js`. Not run on a live Quickshell: save a preset on Calendar, switch to Gacha, wear the preset again.
- Added: **"Advanced" on the Quick Panel's Wi-Fi and Bluetooth popups** (right-click the tile). A pill beside Scan / Refresh opens the full settings app and puts the phone away: Wi-Fi -> KDE's Wi-Fi & Networking page (`kcmshell6 kcm_networkmanagement`, from `plasma-nm`; then `nm-connection-editor`, GNOME Settings), Bluetooth -> `blueman-manager` (then `blueberry`, KDE's `kcmshell6` page, GNOME Settings, and last a terminal tool: `nmtui` / `bluetui` / `bluetoothctl`; if nothing is installed a notification names the package). The work is `scripts/system/open-settings-app.sh`, called by `QuickSource.openNetworkSettings()` / `openBluetoothSettings()`. The installer now installs `plasma-nm`, `kde-cli-tools` and `blueman`, and `check-deps.sh` lists them. Test: `tests/theme-default-test/advanced_buttons_test.js`. Not run on a live Quickshell: look at the pill's place next to Scan / Refresh (the popup is 575 px wide) and that the phone closes after the app opens.
- Changed: **a fresh install starts with only the clock on the desktop.** To-Do, User card, Weather card and Visualizer now start off (`shown: false` in their services; Edit Desktop or Settings -> Desktop switches them on). Anyone with a saved state file keeps what they had. **Window borders now follow the theme by default** (`appTheme.hyprland` is `true`; with the Wallpaper (matugen) theme the borders take matugen's colours). Not run on a live Quickshell.
- Fixed: **Log out did nothing.** On Hyprland 0.55+ (Lua config) `hyprctl dispatch exit` is not understood; the dispatcher is `hl.dsp.exit()`. `scripts/system/session-logout.sh` (Quick Panel power menu) and the launcher's Log out (`Extras.js`) now try the Lua form, then the old form, then `loginctl terminate-session`. Test: `tests/theme-default-test/first_run_defaults_test.js`.
- Changed: **a first run starts in Dark mode** (it started light). `ThemeService.dark` now defaults to `true`; when no `dark` state file exists yet, `bootSaved()` calls `firstRunDark()`, which saves dark = on and asks for a dark system colour scheme once (`syncSystemScheme(false)`). `UtilButtons.followScheme()` ignores a light scheme while `firstDarkPending` is true (until the scheme turns dark, or 8 s), so the bar cannot pull the shell back to light before the system has changed. Saved choices are untouched: anyone with a `dark` file keeps what they picked. New test: `tests/theme-default-test`. **Not run on a live Quickshell.** To check: `rm ~/.local/state/pjsekai/dark ~/.local/state/pjsekai/darker`, set `gsettings set org.gnome.desktop.interface color-scheme prefer-light`, restart the shell: it should come up dark with no light flash, and the file `dark` should now say `on`.
- Fixed: **the shell did not start** ("Type Lockscreen unavailable ... LockCalendar.qml[634:9]: Cannot override FINAL property"). `clockKeys` in `LockCalendar.qml` declared `readonly property real left`, and `left` (like `right`, `top`, `bottom`, `x`, `y`, `width`, ...) is a FINAL property of every Item, which QML refuses to redeclare; it is `startX` now. Found by loading the file for real (the old checks only read the source); `tests/qml-names-test/final_names_test.js` now fails on any such declaration in any .qml file.

## Unreleased — Calendar lockscreen: the shell's own blocks (straight frames, straight shadows, clock as four keys)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (a flat mock-up with the default theme's colours was the only visual check). Look at: lock with Settings -> General -> Launcher & lock -> Lockscreen style -> Calendar. Check: (1) the three frames are plain rectangles with softly rounded corners (no cut corners anywhere), each in an outline + white ring, and each on a hard shadow that drops STRAIGHT DOWN (pink under the wide one, mint under the tall one, lilac under the small one; border colour in Black & white); the picture inside follows the rounded corners; (2) the clock is four keycaps in a shallow V (hours dark with white digits, minutes mint fading to cyan), two diamonds between them, a date sticker above (`FRI  16  OCT`, with a pink AM / PM chip on 12-hour clocks), and a mint LOCKED chip and a NAME chip with your user name on top; (3) along the bottom: a white card with every day of the month in two rows (weekends pink, today a mint key with an outline), the password plate (dots in the five card-attribute colours, in a pale well; a wrong password turns its outline and shadow pink and shakes it), a mint ENTER key, and two round keys (Restart, Shut down); (4) hold Ctrl: the ENTER key turns pink with a coffee cup and says SLEEP (Ctrl+Enter, a click while Ctrl is held, or a right click sleeps); (5) click Restart once: the round key turns pink with an exclamation mark and the line under the keys says PRESS AGAIN TO RESTART (naming a running install / download if there is one); the second click restarts; 4 s of nothing disarms; same for Shut down; hover a round key and the line names it; (6) keys sink onto their shadow when pressed; (7) the transition ring into the lock and out of it; (8) other themes and Black & white (Settings -> Calendar colours).

- Changed: everything in the Calendar look is now built from one block, `LockPlate.qml` (new): `Pill.qml`'s three layers (outline, thin white ring, colour) on a hard shadow with NO sideways offset, in the lockscreen's palette (`LockPalette`), with `pressed` sinking it like a keycap. The outline, shadows and text are the theme's border colour (`LockPalette.cBorder`), the plates are the lockscreen's white (`LockPalette.paper`), and the colours are the theme's accents (`calAccent`, `calMint`, `calLilac`) on the pale themed sheet (`calPaper`). `calInk` and `calBarDim` are gone (the dark bar and the big ink numerals with them).
- Changed: `LockPanel.qml` is a LockPlate with the picture inside it: straight sides, corners rounded by a mask (`MultiEffect` `maskEnabled`, the same way the dock rounds its icons), no cut corners (`cuts`, the corner triangles and the outline polygon are removed). Colour pass and halftone unchanged; `plate` / `plateOffset` became `shadowColor` / `shadowDy`.
- Changed: the clock and date. The big ink numerals, the pink print shadow, the scribbled month and the star badge are gone. Four keys in a V like the desktop clock widget's Keys design, a date sticker, an AM / PM chip, LOCKED and NAME chips, in the left column (which now starts under the wide frame, so it is a little taller).
- Changed: the bottom. The dark bar and its three-ink strip are gone; the month is a card of day cells, the password is a plate, Enter is a plate key, Restart and Shut down are round `LockPowerKey`s (now on a LockPlate; icon-only, `priority_high` when armed, `hovered` for the caption line). The small print ("locked since", battery) and the now-playing caption are small plates too.
- Not changed: crops, `lock.tone` / `lock.crop` / `lock.powerButtons`, Ctrl / sleep / restart / shutdown behaviour and their safeguards, anything outside the Calendar look.
- Known: the frames' corner radius is `frameRadius` in `LockCalendar.qml` (`root.px(18)`; set it to 0 for perfectly square corners, the picture mask follows). Like the rest of the Calendar look it ignores the shell scale and sizes from the screen's height. Round keys are icon-only; their names show in the line under the keys while hovered.

## Unreleased — Calendar lockscreen: in colour, in your theme's colours

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (a flat mock-up with the default theme's colours was the only visual check). Look at: lock with Settings -> General -> Launcher & lock -> Lockscreen style -> Calendar. The pictures should now keep your wallpaper's colours (a little more saturated), each on a flat plate of an ink peeking out down and to the right of it (pink under the wide one, mint under the tall one, lilac under the small one); the paper is a pale tint of the theme, the ink and the bottom bar a deep tint of it; the time has a pink print shadow; the date star is pink; today in the month strip is a mint block, weekends pink; the coffee key is mint; the very bottom edge is three bands of ink. Check: (1) change the theme (Settings -> theme, try Vivid BAD SQUAD, MORE MORE JUMP!, and Wallpaper (matugen)): paper, ink, plates and accents should all follow; (2) a pale wallpaper (like the default one) still reads, and a dark or very colourful one is not drowned by the plates; (3) text on every ink stays readable (pink weekend numbers on the bar, ink on the pink star, ink on the mint today block); (4) Settings -> Calendar colours -> Black & white gives the earlier monochrome pictures (no plates, no print shadow); (5) the Enter key, the coffee key, Restart / Shut down keys, and the transition ring behave as in the entry below.

- Changed: `LockPanel.qml` shows the picture in colour by default (`MultiEffect` saturation +0.25, contrast +0.12, no colorize) and draws a flat colour plate behind the frame (its own shape moved by `plateOffset`, so cut corners follow). `mono` brings back the earlier black-and-white / tinted treatment. The halftone screen is lighter in colour (0.2 vs 0.55) so it does not dull the colours.
- Changed: `LockPalette.qml` calendar colours are now all tints of the THEME, none fixed: `calInk` (deep border tint), `calPaper` (pale glint tint, was warm grey), `calAccent` (pink), and new `calMint` (from the mint), `calLilac` (from the glint), used by `LockCalendar.qml` for the plates, the star, today, the slashes, the coffee key and the bottom bands.
- Added: setting `lock.tone` = `color` (default) | `mono`, a choice under the Calendar settings (kept across presets, like the other lock keys). Test updated: no fixed hex colours in the Calendar look, palette colours come from theme colours, `lock.tone` is wired.
- Not changed: crops, Ctrl / coffee / restart / shutdown behaviour, anything outside the Calendar look.
- Known: colours depend on the wallpaper: a very pale one (like the default) gives pale pictures, which is the wallpaper's doing; the plates keep the poster colourful regardless.

## Unreleased — Lockscreen: a Calendar style, with Restart / Shut down and a coffee (sleep) key

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (and the pictures were checked only as a flat mock-up, not a real render). Look at: Settings -> General -> Launcher & lock -> Lockscreen style -> Calendar, then lock (`qs -c sekaipop ipc call lock lock`, or your usual key). You should get a poster in your theme's colours (see the next entry up): three framed pictures cut out of your wallpaper (a wide one top left, a tall one on the right, a small one under the wide one) under a fine dot screen, the time as huge ink numerals with the month scribbled across it and the date as a tilted star badge, your user name on a NAME tab, and a black bar along the bottom with every day of the month in two rows (weekends in the accent, today in a block), the password field, an Enter key, and Restart / Shutdown keys. Check, in this order: (1) that the three pictures show different, sensible parts of the wallpaper and the first one shows the subject (try two or three wallpapers: a character, a landscape, a flat colour, a portrait one); (2) hold Ctrl: the Enter key turns into a mint coffee key that says SLEEP, and Ctrl+Enter (or a click on it while Ctrl is held, or a right click on it) suspends and wakes on the same locked screen; release Ctrl and it is Enter again (and plain Enter unlocks); (3) click Restart once: it turns into SURE? and the line under the keys says so (and names a running install or download, if there is one); a second click restarts; do nothing for 4 s and it disarms; same for Shut down; (4) a wrong password: the field shakes and its label turns into the error; (5) with two monitors, holding Ctrl on one makes the coffee key show on both; (6) the transition ring into the lock and out of it, which now runs over this picture; (7) 75 / 125 / 150% shell scale (it should look the same: see Known); (8) switching the style back to Gacha, which should look and behave exactly as before.

- Added: the Calendar look (`LockCalendar.qml`) and `LockPanel.qml` (one frame: the picture flat in a texture the size of the frame, then `MultiEffect` for black and white / tint, a tiled halftone, thick outline with cut corners). `LockPowerKey.qml` (the outlined key; its own file because inline components cannot see the ids of the file they are in). `LockHost.qml` picks the look from `lock.style` and gives both the same interface; `Lockscreen.qml` and `LockRing.qml` now use it instead of `LockSurface` (a one-word change each), so the ring and the real lock always show the same look and only the chosen look is built.
- Added: where each frame's picture comes from. The frames sit in fixed places (fractions of the screen, so it fills any monitor); what they show is chosen from a tiny copy of the wallpaper: `scripts/wallpapers/lock-crop.sh` (ImageMagick, 96 px, plain PPM on stdout, nothing written to disk) -> `LockCrop.js` (edges + colours that stand out + a little pull to the middle; the wide frame takes the most interesting window, the tall one the best that is left, the close-up another; each later frame is penalised for overlapping the earlier ones) -> `services/LockCropService.qml` (one read at a time at low priority, keeps the last 8 pictures' maps, 8 s timeout). It is requested as soon as the look exists, long before you lock, and everything downstream is deterministic, so the ring's copy and the real surface cut the same pictures. `lock.crop` = `fixed` (or no ImageMagick, or a picture it cannot read) uses fixed spots instead (`LockCrop.fixedPlan`).
- Added: Ctrl state and power actions in `LockContext.qml`: `ctrlHeld` (shared by every monitor), `powerPress("reboot" | "poweroff")` (first press arms for 4 s, the second does it; looks for a running install / download while armed through `PowerBusyService`), `sleep()` (`systemctl suspend || loginctl suspend`; wipes what was typed). All of them do nothing unless the session is really locked (`LockState.phase === "locked"`), so LockRing's copy of the picture can never act. A fresh lock starts disarmed and with Ctrl released.
- Added: settings `lock.style` (gacha | calendar, default gacha), `lock.crop` (auto | fixed), `lock.powerButtons` (default on; off hides Restart / Shut down, Ctrl+Enter still sleeps), all in `keepExact` so a desktop preset does not change them. `qs -c sekaipop ipc call options set lock.style calendar` works. New palette colours `calInk`, `calPaper`, `calAccent`, `calMuted`, `calBarDim` in `LockPalette.qml` (follow the theme like the rest of that file). New asset `assets/images/lock-dots.png` (a 6 px halftone tile).
- Cost: the Calendar look is drawn once a minute and runs no animation of its own (the only loop is the spinner on the Enter key while a password is being checked), so a locked screen is idle between minutes. The wallpaper is decoded once at up to 1.5x the screen (max 3200 px on the long side) and shared by the three frames; the always-built copy in LockRing therefore holds one such decode (about 19 MB at 1080p) while this style is selected, and nothing when Gacha is.
- Not changed: the Gacha look (`LockSurface.qml` is untouched; a test checks it does not mention Ctrl), the password / PAM logic, the lock transition, the wallpaper choice (`WallpaperService.lockImagePathFor`). "Blur lock wallpaper" only affects Gacha.
- Known: the Calendar look ignores the shell scale (`uiScale`) on purpose: it is laid out in fractions of the screen and sized from the screen's height, like a poster; scaling it only makes the text overflow its frames. Needs Material Symbols Rounded (like the rest of the shell) for the icons. A wallpaper replaced in place (same file name) keeps its old crops until the shell restarts. The star badge and the month overlap the numerals a little on purpose (as in the poster it copies); at very narrow screens (4:3 and narrower) the time shrinks to fit and the month strip is left out when its cells would be under 30 px. `Fixed` crops are three fixed spots, they cannot know where the subject is. The battery and "locked since" are small print above the bar, and what is playing is a caption tab on the wide frame (only while something plays).
- Files: `modules/sekai/lock/LockCalendar.qml`, `LockPanel.qml`, `LockPowerKey.qml`, `LockHost.qml`, `LockContext.qml`, `Lockscreen.qml`, `LockRing.qml`, `modules/common/LockPalette.qml`, `modules/common/functions/LockCrop.js`, `services/LockCropService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, `scripts/wallpapers/lock-crop.sh`, `assets/images/lock-dots.png`, `tests/lock-calendar-test/crop_unit.js`, `tests/lock-calendar-test/calendar_source_test.js`.

## Unreleased — Displays: drag a screen to move it

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt where this was built). What was checked: the placement maths and the draft logic by node (`tests/display-test/display_unit.js`, `service_sim.js`; the latter runs the real function bodies of `DisplayService.qml`), and the three changed QML files for balanced braces. NOT checked: the new card drawn and the drag itself, and Hyprland accepting the positions (the same `hl.monitor({ position = "XxY" })` call as before, only the numbers now come from where you dropped the screen). Look at: Settings -> Hyprland -> "Displays" with two screens on. A drawing of the screens to scale appears at the top of the card; drag one and it should follow the pointer, snap to the other's edges (flush, or lined up top / bottom / left / right), and when you let go land where it was snapped (or slide off the other screen if it was dropped on it). Press Apply: the screens move on the desktop, and Keep writes `monitors.lua` with those positions. Check: (1) the "Sits at x, y px" line under each screen follows the drag; (2) the Left / Right / Above / Below keys still work and the drawing follows them; (3) change a screen's resolution or scale: the screens past it move over, so neighbours stay neighbours; (4) drag a screen away from the others: a line names it as unreachable by the pointer; (5) the drawing does nothing during the "Keep these?" countdown; (6) turn a second screen on: it appears on the right of the others.

- Added: `modules/sekai/settings/DisplayLayout.qml`, the drawing. The main screen is the coloured one. Screens snap within 14 on-screen px.
- Changed: `DisplayService.qml` keeps an x, y for every screen in the draft (read from Hyprland) instead of working positions out from "which side of main". New `setPosition`, `settle` (shifts the layout so the smallest x and y are 0 and re-reads each screen's side), `resized` (a screen changes size: the ones past it move by the difference), `unlinkedNames`. `setPlace` now puts the screen flush against the main screen, past any screen already there.
- Changed: `DisplayMath.js` gets `snap`, `separate` (nearest clear spot), `dock`, `reflow`, `normalize`, `touches`, `unlinked`, `view`. `positions` is no longer used by the service but is kept, with its tests.
- Changed: the Hyprland page's Displays card: the drawing and a one-line hint above "Main screen"; a "Sits at x, y px" line per screen; the nwg-displays key now says "Rotate, mirror and more" (dragging is done here).
- Fixed: screen sizes in the layout round up to whole pixels (at 175 % a 1920 px screen is 1097.14 wide, so a neighbour at 1097 sat on top of it and Hyprland said "monitor layout is set up incorrectly").
- Fixed: a screen turned off is now parked past the right edge of the others (`name|off|x|y`; `displays.sh apply` first turns it off, then sets that position as a best effort), because Hyprland counted it at 0, 0 and said "monitor layout is set up incorrectly" when it was switched off. NOT checked on Hyprland: whether it takes a position for an off screen; if not, nothing changes (the screen is already off). `monitors.lua` and "Go back" are unchanged (no position for off screens), so the warning can still show after a reload.
- Not changed: `displays.sh` for screens that are on (it already took any x and y), the 15 s keep / go-back, mirroring, rotation, VRR.
- Undo: delete `DisplayLayout.qml` and put back `DisplayService.qml`, `DisplayMath.js` and `SettingsPageHyprland.qml` from the previous zip.
- Files: `modules/sekai/settings/DisplayLayout.qml` (new), `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageHyprland.qml`, `services/DisplayService.qml`, `modules/common/functions/DisplayMath.js`, `tests/display-test/`.
- Unrelated: three test files already fail in this tree and were not touched: `analyzer-test/chart_test.js`, `phone-test/power_press_test.js`, `wheel-test/wheel_source_test.js` (a dock shelf wants a `WheelScroll`).

## Unreleased — Settings: change the screens inside the shell (Displays)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt where this was built). What was checked: the maths and the whole draft / apply / keep / go-back logic by node (`tests/display-test/display_unit.js`, `service_sim.js`, the latter running the real function bodies of `DisplayService.qml` against hyprctl-shaped data for a laptop panel that is off and an HDMI screen that is on), and `displays.sh` against a fake `hyprctl` and a copy of a real `monitors.lua` (values checked, extra lines kept, bad input refused). NOT checked: Hyprland itself accepting `hyprctl eval "hl.monitor({ ... })"` with these fields (the same call shape `hyprland/general.lua` uses), and the card drawn. Look at: Settings -> Hyprland -> "Displays". Each screen should have its own block: Screen on/off, Resolution, Refresh rate, Scale, and (for every screen but the main one) Placed. Change the scale, press Apply: the screen changes and the card says "Keep these display settings?" with a countdown. Keep: stays, and `~/.config/hypr/monitors.lua` is written. Do nothing for 15 s (or press Go back): it goes back, and nothing is written. Turn the second screen on and put it Left of the main one: it should land where you said. Check: the last screen on cannot be turned off; the controls do not flicker while the page sits open; a change made in nwg-displays shows up here within a few seconds.

- Added: `services/DisplayService.qml` (reads `hyprctl -j monitors all`, keeps a draft, applies it live, asks "keep these?" with a 15 s countdown, saves only on Keep), `scripts/hyprland/displays.sh` (`apply` and `save`), `modules/common/functions/DisplayMath.js` (modes, scale, where each screen sits), and the card on the Hyprland page.
- The layout is "main screen at 0,0, the others on its Right / Left / Above / Below"; screens on the same side go one after the other; all positions are then shifted so the smallest x and y are 0. Rotated screens (transform 1, 3, 5, 7) count with width and height swapped.
- `monitors.lua` is written in the format nwg-displays uses, so nwg-displays still works on the same file (an "nwg-displays" key shows on the card when it is installed). Lines in an existing block that are not ours (`vrr`, `transform`, `bitdepth`, `mirror` ...) are carried over, and the old file is kept as `monitors.lua.bak`.
- Not changed: free-form dragging of screens (that is what nwg-displays is for), mirroring, per-screen rotation, VRR. They are kept if they are already in `monitors.lua`, just not editable here.
- Undo: delete `DisplayService.qml`, `displays.sh`, `DisplayMath.js` and put back `SettingsPageHyprland.qml`; `monitors.lua.bak` is the file as it was before the last Keep.
- Files: `services/DisplayService.qml`, `scripts/hyprland/displays.sh`, `modules/common/functions/DisplayMath.js`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageHyprland.qml`, `tests/display-test/`.

## Unreleased — Keyboard layouts: pick them in Settings, and the bar key switches them

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Look at: Settings -> Hyprland -> "Keyboard layouts". Turn on a second layout (say Russian next to English): a layout code (EN / RU) should appear on the bar within a moment, without a shell restart; click it to switch, and the code should change. Turn the second one off again: the key should leave the bar. Type a code like `pl` in "Another layout" and press Enter: it should join the list and the field should empty. Pick "Alt + Shift" under "Switch with" and press it: the layout should switch. Check: you can't turn off the last layout; a reload (`hyprctl reload`) keeps the choices.

- Added: a "Keyboard layouts" card on the Hyprland page: a chip per common layout (plus any other layout already on), a field for any other xkb code, and the key combo that switches (only the `grp:` option in `kb_options` is replaced; others such as `caps:escape` are kept). It writes `input.kb_layout` / `input.kb_options` through the same override file and live-apply as every other option on that page, so nothing in `hyprland.lua` has to be edited.
- Changed: changing the layout list clears `input.kb_variant` (variants are matched to layouts by position and would point at the wrong layout).
- Fixed: the bar's layout key ran `hl.dsp.switchxkblayout(...)`, a guess that was never checked against Hyprland. It runs `hyprctl switchxkblayout all next` now, which is the same on a Lua and a hyprlang config.
- Changed: the bar re-reads its layouts when Hyprland reloads and 0.4 s after the panel changes them (new `applied` signal on `HyprlandSettings`), so the key appears and disappears by itself.
- Fixed: turning a layout off while you were typing in it crashed Hyprland (and Quickshell with it). `hyprland-settings.sh` now switches every keyboard back to the first layout and waits a quarter of a second before it changes `input.kb_layout` or `input.kb_variant`. This is the likely cause, worked out without a crash log: if it still crashes, the report is `~/.cache/hypr/hyprlandCrashReport*.txt`.
- Not changed: when the layout key shows (still only with two or more layouts), its two-letter code, the OSD, the on-screen keyboard.
- Undo: put back `Bar.qml`, `HyprlandSettings.qml` and `SettingsPageHyprland.qml`. "Drop my changes" on the Hyprland page also removes the layouts chosen here.
- Files: `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageHyprland.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/Bar.qml`, `services/HyprlandSettings.qml`.

The key is 3 px taller (31 instead of 28) so the icon still fits inside the ring; it keeps its right edge and its bottom, so its top goes up 3 px (`y` 74 -> 71, 15 -> 12, `mini.hangY` -> `mini.hangY - 3`) and nothing else moves.
