## Unreleased — OSD: the volume / mic / brightness card is a Sekai plate

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt where this was built), so the layout was worked out by hand from the same parts the Battery popup and the resource plates use. Look at: press the volume keys, mute / unmute, the mic mute key, the brightness keys (and gamma, if you use it). The card should be a slab in the bar's white outline, with its icon on a keycap at the left, the name beside it, the level as big sticker numerals at the right, and ten slanted segments along the bottom. Check: (1) hold a volume key: the numerals hop on every step, the front segment lifts, and a 5 % step lights half a segment; (2) mute: the plate turns lavender, the numerals become a tilted MUTE stamp, the gauge keeps the level dimmed, the keycap dips and springs back; (3) a boosted volume above 100 %: the plate turns pink, the gauge is full, the number says the real figure; (4) all three modes (Light, Dark, Darker) and a bar on each edge: the card grows out of the bar's edge; (5) 75 / 100 / 150 % scale.

- Changed: `OSDIndicator.qml` is now a `SekaiPlate` (the header slab of every bar popup: outline, white line, hard shadow straight down, halftone, two bands of stage light) inside the housing the bar, the dock and the keyboard have (navy outline, the wide pale ring `Theme.surfaceRing` = `borderWidth + outerRingWidth` thick, then the surface), instead of a plain surface `Pill`; so the OSD keeps the same white outline as the rest. The icon sits on a chip-coloured keycap, the name is the plate's small letter-spaced caps, the level is `StickerText` numerals (the plate's own `numberFill` / `numberRing`), and the bar underneath is ten slanted segments like the Battery popup's life gauge (lit white on a dim slot; a part-full segment fills from the left).
- Changed: the plate wears the colour of the state, like the Battery plate wears the judgement: the accent gradient normally, lavender while muted (the number is replaced by a tilted MUTE stamp), pink above 100 %.
- Added: the card reacts like the rest of the shell. The numerals hop and the front segment lifts on every change of the level; the whole plate and the keycap dip onto their shadow and spring back (Pill's press) when the kind changes or the mute flips.
- Fixed: the old card laid its content out twice inside its ring (a `Pill` already insets what is declared in it, and the content then added the ring again), so the percentage ran off the right edge and the track poked out under the card. Everything is now placed in the plate's own coordinates.
- Changed: `OSDPopup.qml` grows the card out of the edge the bar is on (and back into it) instead of from its middle, and from a little smaller (88 % instead of 92 %).
- Not changed: `OSDSource.qml`, what shows when, the hover-to-dismiss, the window per screen, the scale handling. The card is 314 x 122 design px (it was 280 x 86).
- Files: `modules/sekai/onScreenDisplay/OSDIndicator.qml`, `OSDPopup.qml`.

## Unreleased — Lyrics: a fast line no longer makes the one before it finish at once

**Checked:** the maths and wiring by node (`tests/lyrics-test/`), and `LyricsLine.qml` was drawn for real in Qt (offscreen, stand-in theme) with the old and the new code side by side on the same made-up song, and with an echo folded into its line. **Not checked:** a live Quickshell, or real LRCLIB files.

- Fixed: **the line that is on finishing itself the moment a fast line comes in.** When the next line was stamped soon after the current one began (an echo, a quick answer, voices that overlap), the current line was squeezed into the room before that stamp: its words rushed through and it was complete almost at once, well ahead of the voice. A line whose room is under 65 % of what it takes at the song's pace is now a *fast entry*: it takes most of its own time (never more than 0.9 of it) and is let run on up to 1.5 s under the next line (`sungSpan`, `OVERLAP_BELOW`, `MAX_OVERLAP`). In the test song a line that takes 4.5 s with the next stamp 1.2 s later is sung over 2.7 s instead of 1.1 s. Lines that fit their room are exactly as before: on made-up songs at a steady pace 0 of 59 lines change, with a ±35 % uneven pace 1 of 59, in a ballad with pauses 0 of 59. An end stamp that falls after the next line's start (overlapping voices) is believed, up to 2 s into it.
- Fixed: **that line leaving the middle at once.** The next line began climbing 0.45 s before its stamp whatever the room, so a line that was on for half a second spent most of it already leaving. The climb now takes at most the last 40 % of the time the line is on (`cursor`); a line that is on for a long time has the whole 0.45 s as before.
- Fixed: **it then shrinking to a faint small line with its words still filling.** A line that is leaving or has just left, while it is still being sung, keeps 78 % of its size and 80 % of its strength, sits a little higher so it is clear of the new line, and eases back to the usual look over the last 0.4 s of its singing (`busy` in `LyricsLine.qml`).
- Added: **an echo that comes in during a line sings along instead of taking the stage.** A line of only ad libs, "(about you)", stamped while the line before it is still being sung (before that line's end stamp, or in the first 60 % of the time it takes at the song's pace) is folded into that line as the small "sings along" row, starting at its own stamp (`foldEchoes`, applied as the lyrics are loaded). The row can outlast its line (`bgEnd`). Lines not in brackets are never folded: they are fast entries and keep the stage. This is the case the previous entry listed as not done.
- Files: `modules/common/functions/LyricsMath.js` (`sungSpan`, `cursor`, `foldEchoes`), `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/LyricsLine.qml` (`busy`, `bgEnd`), `LyricsWidget.qml` (`bgEndOf`), `services/LyricsService.qml` (folds on load), tests `tests/lyrics-test/lyrics_unit.js`, `lyrics_source_test.js`.

## Unreleased — Lyrics: steadier timing, and ad libs

**Checked:** the maths and the wiring by node (`tests/lyrics-test/`, `tests/preset-widgets-test/`), and `LyricsLine.qml` was drawn for real in Qt (offscreen, a stand-in theme and the real `DiamondDot` / `Burst`) at several moments of made-up songs: an inline ad lib, a second line at the same moment, a line of only ad libs, a line with word timing, a Japanese line with full-width brackets, and a very long line. **Not checked:** a live Quickshell, a real player's readings, or real LRCLIB files (no network where this was built). The clock numbers below are from a simulation of players that report badly, not from your player; `ipc lyrics debug` is there so you can see your own.

- Fixed: **the words being early, then late, then early.** The clock averaged the player's readings in, but a reading can only ever be OLDER than the truth (a player that moves its position once a second, a busy bus, a slow reply), never newer, so an average sags behind by half the lateness and jumps about when the lateness does. The clock now follows the best (least late) of the last ten readings (`LyricsMath.observe`). In the simulation, against the old clock: a player that moves its position once a second, rms error 480 ms -> 120 ms (worst 690 -> 120); a slow-to-answer one 240 -> 40 (worst 440 -> 180); one with now and then a very late reply 190 -> 2 (worst 800 -> 7); an exact one 21 -> 6. What is left of the 120 ms is a steady lag, not a wobble, so a song's nudge can take it out. A seek of 1.5 s or more is still followed at once; a jump back of under 0.8 s is taken for a late reading and believed only after two seconds of saying the same.
- Fixed: **words that drift inside a line.** How long a line is sung was a guess from the room before the next line (88 % of it up to 7 s), so a line followed by a short break was filled slowly over the break and its words were late. It is now the amount of singing in the line at THIS song's rate (`songRate`: the middle of the rates of the lines that say how long they are, so one slow line does not decide it), a fifth longer for the held note, never more than 92 % of the room. A line that is followed at once is still sung right up to the next one. A stamp with no words right after a line ("[00:14.20]") is now read as where that line ENDS (before, it was a gap of three diamonds even when it was a breath); it is shown as a gap only when the next line is 3.5 s or more away.
- Fixed: **a word's share of the line.** The time was shared out by letters; it is now by syllables (English), morae (a kana is one, a kanji about two, a small ya / yu / yo and the like none, a long-vowel mark one) and one per Hangul block, with the last word of a line held a bit longer.
- Added: **word timing from the file.** A lyrics file with "<00:12.40>want" tags (enhanced LRC; from a `.lrc` beside the music file or LRCLIB) is used as it is: each word starts when its tag says, and a closing tag is where the line ends. Before, the tags were thrown away.
- Added: **ad libs.** Words in brackets, "hold me (oh oh)", are drawn smaller, raised a little, tilted and in the second accent colour; they fill when they are sung, take less of the line's time than the same number of words (they are sung over it), and the bouncing marker and the diamonds skip them (a line of only ad libs is hopped over whole). The brackets themselves are not drawn. ASCII and full-width brackets both work.
- Added: **a voice that sings along.** A second line stamped at the same moment (within 0.15 s) used to make the first line flash past; it is now a small row above the line, filling over the same time, there while the line is on. Which line is the main one: the one without brackets. A "[bg: ...]" tag (on a stamp, after the words, or on a row of its own after a line) is the same thing; a stamp with only a [bg:] tag is a line of ad libs, not a gap (it used to blank the line).
- Added: `ipc lyrics debug`: the tag under the lyrics shows the clock's numbers instead of the title (how far apart the player's last ten readings were, which line is on, how fast the song is sung, the nudge); again to hide. The shell's log has a `[lyrics] clock moved ...` line whenever the clock jumps by more than half a second (a seek, or the player stalled), so an early or late moment can be matched to what the clock did.
- A backing line stamped at its own time while the line before it is still being sung (a call and response): see the entry above.
- Files: `modules/common/functions/LyricsMath.js` (`parseLrc`, `tokens`, `lineUnits`, `songRate`, `sungSpan`, `spansFor`, `mainIndexes`, `clockReset`, `observe`, `clockSpread`; `retune` is gone), `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/LyricsWidget.qml`, `LyricsLine.qml`, `services/LyricsService.qml` (`debug`), `shell.qml` (`ipc lyrics debug`), `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js`; tests: `tests/lyrics-test/lyrics_unit.js` (now includes the clock simulation), `lyrics_source_test.js`.

## Unreleased — Lyrics: a transparent desktop widget that sings along

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** What was checked: the maths, the scripts and the wiring by node (`tests/lyrics-test/`, `tests/preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js`), the line and word components were drawn for real in Qt (offscreen, a stand-in theme) at several moments of a made-up song, and `LyricsService.qml` itself was run in Qt against a stand-in `Process` and a fake `curl` (first play, the cache on the second play, no network, a track nobody has). NOT checked: the real lrclib.net (no network where this was built), so its answers in the test were written by hand from its documented shape. Drawn: the intro diamonds, a line climbing into the middle, the sung colour and the bouncing marker, a gap, a Japanese line. Look at, on the real shell: Settings -> Desktop -> "Lyrics" (or Edit Desktop's shelf, or `qs -c sekaipop ipc call lyrics toggle`), then play something in Spotify / mpv / a browser tab: the words should appear on the desktop, under your windows, with no card behind them. Pause: they dim and stop. Seek in the player: they follow. Open a window on that workspace: Focus Mode hides it. Edit Desktop with nothing playing: a made-up verse shows so you can place it.

- Added: **Lyrics**, a desktop widget (off until you add it). The words of the song that is playing are drawn as stickers (a navy outline and a hard shadow) straight on the wallpaper, so it is transparent everywhere except the letters. The line being sung is big in the middle; the one before it has slid up and faded, the one to come waits below, small and tilted. A moment before a line starts it climbs into the middle and its words hop up one after another. While it is sung the words fill with the theme's colour (a lyrics file only says when a LINE starts, so the line's time is shared out over its words by length), and a diamond bounces from word to word, landing on each as it starts: the word squashes and a few diamonds fly off. An intro or an instrumental part is three diamonds that light one by one. Text with no spaces (Japanese, Chinese, Korean) is cut into groups of three characters. A line that is too wide is shrunk to fit. It follows the theme (its accent colours), dims while paused, and goes away when no player is around.
- Added: where the words come from (`services/LyricsService.qml`): the cache (`~/.cache/pjsekai/lyrics/`), then a `.lrc` file beside the music file (same name), then **LRCLIB** (lrclib.net, a free lyrics database; only synced lyrics are used), **fetched online with `curl`** like the weather is (so `curl` and a network connection are needed). Per title it asks the exact lookup, then a search by title and artist, then a free-text search, and a result of the wrong length (more than 4 s off) is skipped. If the service never answers (no curl, no network) the middle says "can't reach the lyrics service" and the track is asked again next time (it is not remembered as having no lyrics); the shell's log has a `[lyrics]` line for every request. **Only the artist, the title, the album and the length of the track are sent to it** (never the file path or anything else), and only while the widget is on. What is found is cached, so a track is asked for once; a track nobody has is not asked for again for ten minutes. Titles are cleaned first ("(Official Video)", "- Topic", "feat. ...") and an "Artist - Title" title is tried as well.
- Added: the time comes from the player (MPRIS). Its position is read four times a second and a clock runs smoothly in between; a small error is eased out and a seek is taken at once.
- Added: **keeping in sync.** (1) The clock is sturdier: a player whose position does not move while it plays (a browser tab, mostly) is no longer trusted, so the clock is not pulled back to the same spot at every reading; the clock restarts for a new song but not when the length arrives a moment after the title; and pause / resume keeps its place even for a player that gives no position at all. (2) The words of a line are sung over most of the room before the next line (88 %) instead of a guess from the line's length, so the fill and the bouncing marker keep up with the singing in a song where line follows line; only before an instrumental part (7 s or more to the next line) does the length of the line decide. (3) **Point at the lyrics and "earlier" / "later" keys appear**, a tenth of a second a click, with this song's nudge between them (click it to clear it). It is remembered per song, because a lyrics file is made for one cut of a song and the next song is off by a different amount. (4) When the lyrics were made for a cut of a different length than the track (1.5 s or more), the small tag under the lyrics says so ("lyrics for a 3.0 s shorter cut"): that is the song that needs a nudge. (5) `ipc lyrics nudge <ms>` now changes THIS song (remembered); `ipc lyrics nudgeAll <ms>` changes every song (a speaker or a player that is always a little behind); `ipc lyrics resetSync` clears both. Neither nudge is part of a desktop preset.
- Added: `ipc lyrics toggle | show | hide | resetPosition | nudge <ms> | nudgeAll <ms> | resetSync | refresh`. `refresh` looks for the playing track's lyrics again, skipping the cache and the "nobody has them" memory (after a wrong match, or once the network is back). `nudge 250` shows the lyrics 250 ms earlier (negative: later) for a player whose clock is a little off; it is remembered (and is not part of a desktop preset: it belongs to your player).
- Added: the widget has its switch in Settings -> Desktop and Settings -> Quick, a tile in Edit Desktop, a Focus Mode slot, and an entry in the desktop presets (place and on / off). A preset saved before this existed switches it off. While Lock all is on, the widget does not take clicks at all.
- Files: `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/LyricsWidget.qml`, `LyricsLine.qml`, `LyricsWord.qml`, `LyricsKey.qml` (new); `services/LyricsService.qml` (new); `modules/common/functions/LyricsMath.js` (new); `scripts/state/lyrics-state.sh`, `lyrics-cache.sh` (new); `shell.qml`, `services/EditMode.qml`, `services/PresetWidgets.qml`, `modules/common/functions/FocusMath.js`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/FocusGate.qml`, `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `SettingsPageQuick.qml`; tests: `tests/lyrics-test/lyrics_unit.js`, `cache_unit.js`, `lyrics_source_test.js` (new), `tests/preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js`.

## Unreleased — Weather: Open-Meteo, and you just type your city

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and not run against the live Open-Meteo / ipwho.is services** (no network where this was built). `tests/weather-api-test/open_meteo_unit.js` passes, but its sample answers were written by hand from Open-Meteo's documented response shape, so it checks our parsing, not the service. Look at: Settings -> General -> Weather -> Location. Type `Majayjay` and press Enter (or click away): the pill, the popup and the desktop card should go blank for a moment and then show that town's weather, with "Majayjay, Philippines" in the header. Then try `Paris, France`, `Springfield, Illinois` (not the first Springfield), `14.15, 121.47`, a misspelling (the label should read "Couldn't find ..." and no old numbers should stay on show), and the X button (back to a guess from your IP). Flip Fahrenheit: the numbers follow at once. Unplug the network: "Couldn't reach Open-Meteo", and the last readings stay on show if there were any. Check the sunrise / hourly / 3-day row still look right, and that the first request after typing finishes in a second or two.

- Changed: `WeatherSource.qml` no longer asks wttr.in. It searches the typed city on Open-Meteo's geocoding API, then asks the forecast API for that spot (two `curl` steps, each with its URL as a single argument, never in a shell string). The place is looked up once per change of the Location; the 15-minute poll only asks for the weather. Typing `city, country-or-region` picks between same-named places; `lat, lon` skips the search. An empty Location still means "guess": it now uses ipwho.is (looked up again about once an hour). Every property the bar pill, popup and card read is unchanged, so none of their layouts moved.
- Added: `modules/common/functions/OpenMeteo.js` (all the parsing: what was typed, picking the place, WMO weather codes -> text and icon, one forecast answer -> the strings the shell shows, in C or F). `WeatherSource.problemText`: why there is no reading ("Couldn't find ...", "Couldn't reach Open-Meteo", "Type a city in Settings"), which the popup and both card faces show instead of the fixed "Couldn't reach wttr.in".
- Changed (small differences from wttr.in): hourly notes are now exact readings for the hour (still three hours apart, eight of them) instead of wttr.in's three-hour summaries; "now" is found with the place's own clock, so it no longer depends on this machine's time zone; day / night icons follow Open-Meteo's `is_day` for the place. Weather descriptions are Open-Meteo's WMO wording (e.g. "Light rain", "Overcast"), not wttr.in's.
- Not changed: `weather.location`, `weather.fahrenheit`, `weather.view` (same keys, same preset handling), the popup and card drawing, `WeatherMath.js`.
- Removed: wttr.in's `~Place` marker (a leading `~` is ignored) and searching by landmark ("Eiffel Tower"): Open-Meteo's search finds cities and towns, not points of interest.
- Files: `services/WeatherSource.qml`, `modules/common/functions/OpenMeteo.js`, `modules/sekai/bar/WeatherPopup.qml`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/WeatherCardWidget.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingText.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/WeatherCardService.qml`, `shell.qml`, `modules/common/widgets/WeatherGlyph.qml`, `modules/common/functions/WeatherMath.js`, `tests/weather-api-test/open_meteo_unit.js`.
- Known: the typed city is only searched when you press Enter or leave the field (no suggestions list while typing). The first city with that name wins unless you add a country or region. Open-Meteo's free service is for non-commercial use; ipwho.is is a third-party service for the automatic guess.

## Unreleased — Dock (Capsule): the media cover is a circle

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Look at: the dock in the Capsule style, on a bottom / top dock (the long media pill) and on a left / right dock (the small media key). Play something with cover art, then pause it, and magnify the key. The cover should be a circle: on the long pill the whole left end of the capsule is the cover, the blurred cover runs behind the title and buttons, and the pill is fully rounded with the thin capsule ring (lit when playing). On the small key the cover is a circle inside its ring and the play / pause mark sits on the circle's lower right. Float and Transparent should look as they did. Check the previous / play / next buttons on the long pill still light and click where they are drawn (the ring is thinner in Capsule, which the hit zones follow).

- Fixed: the small media key (`mediaFace`) cut its cover with a Rectangle's `clip`, which only clips to the box, so the square cover's corners stuck out of the round key. The cover is now cut with a layer + mask like the app keys (the key's own radius: a circle in Capsule, the rounded square elsewhere). The play / pause mark moved onto the circle's edge in Capsule (the square's corner is empty there).
- Fixed: the long media pill (`mediaWideFace`) did not know about Capsule: a rounded rectangle with a square cover. In Capsule it is a flat, fully rounded capsule in the capsule keys' ring (`mediaRing` is now `Theme.capDotRing` there), the cover is a circle (as wide as the pill is tall, masked) and the blur runs under it the whole length, masked to the capsule's shape.
- Not changed: Float and Transparent (still the square cover with `DockArtFrame` on its corners); the media card; what the buttons do.
- Files: `modules/sekai/dock/Dock.qml`, `tests/dock-test/media_capsule_test.js`.

## Unreleased — Dock: the Files key opens a folder stack

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Look at: click the Files key on the dock. A card opens over it with what is in ~/Downloads (grid of real file icons, pictures as small outlined thumbnails). Click a folder: it opens in place and the arrow in the header goes back. Click a file: it opens in its default application and the card closes. Esc, a click outside the dock, or the key again closes it. Try: the grid / list buttons, the sort button (name, date, type) and the arrow beside it, the eye (hidden files), refresh; right-click a file (Open, Show in file manager, Copy path, Copy file, Add to Shelf) and a folder (Use as the stack's folder); right-click the empty part; right-click the Files key and pick Documents (opens the stack there); middle-click the key (file manager). Dragging a file out of the card into a browser or a terminal is the part most worth a hands-on look. Also an empty folder, a folder with no permission (`chmod 000`), a folder removed while the card is open, a name with spaces, `#` or `<b>`, a large folder, a standing dock (left / right) and a second monitor.

- Added: `DockFilesCard.qml` (the card), `DockFilesIcon.qml` (a picture's thumbnail, else its icon-theme type icon), `FilesStackService.qml` (where it starts, how files are described, open / reveal / copy). The listing is a `FolderListModel` that only exists while the card is shown plus 20 s after (a quick reopen has the list ready); then the model and its watcher are gone. No polling, no process in the background; an empty list runs one small `sh` check to tell "empty" from "gone" / "no permission". Every path goes to a process as its own argument (`xdg-open`, `wl-copy --`, or `$1` of a fixed script), never into a shell string.
- Added: Settings -> Desktop -> Files stack: Files key (Folder stack | File manager), Folder (Downloads, Documents, Pictures, Videos, Music, Desktop or a path), View, Sort by, Reverse order, Show hidden files. Keys `dock.filesMode`, `dock.files.folder|view|sort|reverse|hidden`; all in `keepExact` so a desktop preset does not change them. `qs -c sekaipop ipc call dock files` opens / closes it from a keybind.
- Changed: `Dock.qml` opens the stack from the Files key (the key was only a launcher), puts the dock on the Top layer and gives it keyboard focus (on demand) while the stack is open, and adds the card to the input mask. The key's menu gets "Open file manager"; Downloads / Documents / ... open the stack on that folder this once (File manager mode: as before).
- Not changed: `dock.filesCommand` and what it runs (File manager mode, and middle-click on the key); the Shelf, Clipboard and Timer cards.
- Files: `modules/sekai/dock/DockFilesCard.qml`, `modules/sekai/dock/DockFilesIcon.qml`, `services/FilesStackService.qml`, `modules/sekai/dock/Dock.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `shell.qml`, `tests/files-stack-test/files_stack_test.js`.
- Known: videos and PDFs show their type icon, not a rendered thumbnail (that needs ffmpeg / a thumbnailer); pictures over 24 MB show their type icon too. No arrow-key / Enter navigation (Esc, Backspace, Alt+Left and F5 work). No recent-files list.

## Unreleased — Quick Panel: the Power button is the phone's side button

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Look at: Quick Panel (2.5D and 2D). The header row is Settings, Refresh, Edit (three buttons, same right end as before) and the user@hostname text beside the avatar has room to show in full. 2.5D: the upper, slim button on the phone's right edge is the Power button: hover lights it mint, click opens the Power rail beside the phone, click again (or the scrim) closes it, and it stays lit while the rail is open. 2D: there is no side wall, so a small lavender button stands out of the right edge at the same height; same hover / click / lit. Check the click lands on it in both (this is the part most worth a hands-on look: the 3D one is a hit area on the tilted wall), that the Settings turn (2D -> flip) still looks right, and that a very long user@hostname still ends in "...".

- Changed: the Power key is gone from the header row. Settings / Refresh / Edit moved over by one pitch (`headerX` 240 -> 329) so the row keeps its right end, and the user@hostname text is 254 wide instead of 165 (stops at x 321).
- Added: `PhoneBody.powerButton` / `powerOn` / `powerClicked()`. On, the upper slim side button is live (`powerWall`), and with the phone facing straight on (`flat` / `faceDirect`) a flat button (`powerTab`) stands out of the right edge instead, moved with the front's own matrix (new `PhoneBody.frontMatrix`, the same expression the front used). Off by default: the Settings, Wallpapers and Overview phones are unchanged.
- Added: `QuickPanelFace.togglePowerMenu()` (the old key's logic: toggle the rail, closing any open dialog when it opens); `QuickPanel.qml` turns the button on and wires it.
- Changed: the side buttons stand out of the body more. 2.5D: both buttons are a stack of 7 copies moved out of the wall by up to 14 units (`PhoneBody.buttonStick`, `buttonSlices`), the last copy is the light, outlined face and takes the click; the ones under it are its side. 2D: the flat Power button stands out 18 units (`powerStick`, was 8) with a dark underside. Raise `buttonStick` / `powerStick` for more.
- Changed: the phone's picture has room for that on the right (`scene.padR`), and the mouse box (`hitRect`) is as much wider on the right (14 units on 2.5D, 18 on 2D) so the buttons take clicks.
- Not changed: the Power rail's look and place, what its pills do, the scrim.
- Files: `modules/sekai/phone/PhoneBody.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanelFace.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/PowerMenu.qml` (comment).
- Known: the raised 2.5D buttons are copies drawn without their own layer, so their edges rely on the scene layer's smoothing; if they look jagged while the phone moves, say so.

## Unreleased — Apps grid and Add-buttons list run down to the monitor edge

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Look at: open the phone on Apps and on Quick Panel -> Edit (2.5D, 2D, a smaller monitor scale). The apps and the Add buttons list should run right down to the bottom of the monitor and scroll under the edge, with nothing sitting over the last row and no wallpaper / blank band under a cut line. Scroll to the very end of each: the last row should come up fully clear of the edge. Also try the pull-out bounce (open the phone) and the search results / cards (calculator, emoji): those keep their old size.

- Fixed: the apps grid (`LauncherFace.qml`) stopped 58 design units above the monitor's edge, so the bottom row was cut by an invisible line with the wallpaper showing under it. It now runs to the edge (`visibleReach`, from `QuickPanel.qml`: the lower end of the slanted cutoff plus the pull-out bounce), with a scroll margin so the last row can be brought up clear of the edge.
- Fixed: the Add buttons list in Edit mode (`QuickPanelFace.qml`, `trayScroll`) did the same (it stopped at the cutoff's higher end, with 14 more above). It now runs to the edge like the notification list, with the same kind of scroll margin; with the "panel is full" note showing it still stops above the note.
- Not changed: the page height the launcher's extras (cards, rows) use, the tiles, the search pill, the notification list.
- Files: `modules/sekai/launcher/LauncherFace.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanelFace.qml`.

## Unreleased — Launcher: app tiles are just the icon and the name

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source check passes (`tests/launcher-test/lcd_source_test.js`). Look at: open the phone on Apps. Point at one (or walk with the arrow keys): the icon grows a little; click: it shrinks and springs back. ## Unreleased — Launcher: your wallpaper on the Apps screen, seen through an LCD

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source check passes (`tests/launcher-test/lcd_source_test.js`); the look was only mocked up on a still image. Look at: open the phone on Apps (Quick Panel, then the Apps screen). Your wallpaper should be behind the apps at close to full strength, under a fine pixel mesh (thin red / green / blue stripes with dark lines between), a touch darker at the very edge and with one soft reflection across the upper part. The mesh should read as a mesh, not as noise or moire, while the phone settles, when it is tilted (Quick Panel -> Phone -> 2.5D) and when flat (2D). Each app name sits on a small white chip: check a long name's chip stays inside its cell. The header is the Quick screen's: clock and date top-left, download and upload speed top-right, no chip behind them. Try a light and a dark wallpaper: the header's ink should be navy over a light picture and white over a dark one, like the Quick screen's banner (this is the part most worth checking, see Known), and the speed should move like the Quick screen's. Change the wallpaper (the Wallpapers key): the Apps screen follows. With a video wallpaper it shows the video's first frame. Settings -> General -> Launcher & lock -> Launcher wallpaper: off gives the plain white screen (and no chips) back. With \"One wallpaper per monitor\" on, each monitor's phone shows its own. Open and close the phone a few times and watch for the picture arriving late on the first open.

- Added: **LauncherLcd.qml**, the LCD wallpaper: the monitor's wallpaper nearly as it is (a 0.04 to 0.10 white wash, `washBody` / `washTop`), four faint edge fades like a backlight that does not reach the corners, a tiled pixel mesh (`assets/images/lcd-mesh.png`, 24x24 texels = one LCD pixel, drawn 8 design units wide) and one soft diagonal reflection. Cut to the phone's glass (`Theme.phoneScreenInset`, `Theme.phoneCornerRadius`), under everything else on the screen, takes no clicks. The mesh and edges take about a tenth off the picture's brightness; `meshStrength` and `edgeDark` turn that down.
- Added: the switch **Launcher wallpaper** (`launcher.lcdWallpaper`, on by default) in Settings -> General -> Launcher & lock.
- Added: while the LCD is showing, each app name and \"No apps match\" ride on a frosted chip (the screen's white at 0.86), so they stay readable over any picture now that the wallpaper is not washed out. Not drawn with the switch off or with no wallpaper yet.
- Added: the **internet speed** (download and up) top-right on the Apps screen, the same readout as the Quick screen's (same place, size, arrows and `formatRate`). `QuickPanel` feeds it (`downloadBps`, `uploadBps`).
- Added: the header's ink follows the picture. `LauncherLcd` reads a thumbnail of the wallpaper under the clock + date and under the speed readout (the Quick banner's measuring and its 0.30 rule); dark gives white ink, light the navy one. Without the LCD wallpaper it is the plain panel ink.
- Changed: the clock and date are restyled to the Quick screen's (24px extra bold over 20px, same place, tight spacing, one ink for both) and have no chip behind them.
- Changed: `LauncherFace` has a `screenName`, which `QuickPanel` fills from the monitor the phone is up on.
- Not changed: the tiles, the search pill, the colours of the phone, the Quick screen and the Wallpapers screen. The picture is static (no shader, no animation), so the Apps screen's layer is still only moved while nothing in it changes. It is only decoded while the Apps screen shows.
- Known: the ink measuring reads the picture the screen has already decoded (`ctx.drawImage(wall, ...)`) instead of loading it a second time as the banner does; if that ever draws nothing, the header simply keeps the navy ink.
- Files: `modules/sekai/launcher/LauncherLcd.qml` (new), `LauncherFace.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, `assets/images/lcd-mesh.png` (new), `tests/launcher-test/lcd_source_test.js` (new).

## Unreleased — Dock: closing a window works again

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source check passes (`tests/dock-test/close_window_test.js`). Look at: point at a dock key with a window open, press the X on its preview tile; open the key's right-click menu and press "Close window" (one window) / "Close all windows" (several); middle-click a tile. The windows should close. Also check a key's click-to-focus, minimize and restore still work, and the wheel over a key.

- Fixed: closing a window from the dock did nothing. The dock handed Hyprland the address exactly as Quickshell reports it, and that can lack the `0x` that `hyprctl` and the dispatchers want, so the close found no window and failed silently. The address now gets its `0x` first (`DockService.hexAddr`).
- Changed: the dock's focus and move-to-workspace (minimize / restore) dispatches take their address through the same helper, so they can't hit the same problem.
- Not changed: what the X, the menu rows and the middle click do, the Overview's own close.
- Files: `DockService.qml`, `Dock.qml`, `tests/dock-test/close_window_test.js` (new).

## Unreleased — Adjustments and presets live in ~/.config; Capsule workspace dots follow the theme

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source and script checks pass (`tests/config-dir-test/config_dir_test.js`, `tests/capsule-test/capsule_source_test.js`). Look at: restart the shell with your old files in place: Settings, your presets (with their avatar / banner / frame pictures) and your per-monitor scale should all still be there, now under `~/.config/pjsekai/` (`settings.json`, `scale.json`, `presets/`). Save a preset and change a setting: the files in `~/.config/pjsekai/` change, nothing new appears in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/`. Edit `~/.config/pjsekai/scale.json` by hand: the shell still resizes live. Capsule bar and dock: change the group theme (Ctrl+Shift+T), switch to the wallpaper (matugen) theme, and flip light / dark: the workspace dots, the dock's lit keys and its running dots take the new theme's colours each time.

- Changed: **settings.json** (the Settings Panel's switches), **presets/** (each preset's JSON plus its `.images` / `.frames` folders) and **scale.json** (the per-monitor scale) are kept in `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/pjsekai/`. Before: settings and presets in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/`, scale.json next to `shell.qml` (a fresh copy of the shell wrote over it).
- Added: a one-time move, so nothing is lost: an old `settings.json` and the old `presets/` folder are moved over; the old `scale.json` next to `shell.qml` is copied over (and left where it was). A file or folder that is already in `~/.config/pjsekai/` is never overwritten or merged into. New helper `scripts/state/scale-state.sh`.
- Removed: the `scale.json` shipped in the shell folder (it would have replaced yours when unzipped over an old copy). `ShellScale` makes the config file with the defaults when there is none.
- Changed: the Capsule bar's **workspace dots** take their colours from the active theme (`Theme.capWsColors`: the two accent stops, then the palette's glint, light wall and pale divider tones) instead of a fixed pink / lavender set. They re-colour with a group theme, matugen and light / dark.
- Changed: the Capsule **dock** follows the theme the same way: its lit keys (and the timer fill) use `Theme.capLit` (the theme's first accent), the focused app's dot too, a running app's dot `Theme.capLitSoft` (the palette's glint). The fixed pastel set `Theme.capDotColors` is gone.
- Not changed: everything else in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/` (bar layout, dock layout, theme choice, notifications, tray, EQ, welcome flag ...).
- Files: `scripts/state/settings-state.sh`, `scripts/state/preset-state.sh`, `scripts/state/scale-state.sh` (new), `services/ShellScale.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml` and `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (comments), `modules/common/Theme.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/WorkspaceBadges.qml`, `modules/sekai/dock/Dock.qml`, `modules/sekai/dock/DockRunDot.qml`, `scale.json` (removed), `tests/config-dir-test/config_dir_test.js` (new), `tests/capsule-test/capsule_source_test.js`, `tests/preset-favorite-test/favorite_unit.js`, `tests/preset-rename-test/rename_unit.js`.

## Unreleased — Dock Shelf: what you put down looks like what it is

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source check passes (`tests/dock-test/shelf_look_test.js`). Look at: drop on the Shelf key (and on the open card) a folder, a few pictures (png, jpg, webp), a video (mp4, mkv), a song (mp3, flac), a zip / tar.zst, a PDF, a text file and a code file, then paste some text and a web link. Check: the folder is a folder (shows as a plain book for a moment, then turns into a folder: it is asked once); a picture is a polaroid with the picture in its window and the name on the strip, leaning a little; a video is a video tape standing spine-out; a song is a record in its sleeve with the disc showing over the top; an archive is a box with a lid, tape and a label; text and links are sticky notes with the first lines on them; the PDF and the code file are books (PDF / code glyph on the label). Pointing at each: it slides up and tilts, its name shows above the row, its buttons come up (a column up the folder, tape and book; a row along the foot of the polaroid, record, box and note); the three buttons and a click still work on every kind. Shelves: things of different widths fill a shelf (a row of polaroids holds fewer than nine, a row of tapes more); the 2 / 3 tag and the wheel still work. Restart the shell: the folder is still a folder, and a shelf kept by the older version gets its folders found on the first load. Both light and dark mode (the polaroid, box, note and tape keep their own paper colours); a picture that can't be read shows the tinted window with an image glyph.

- Changed: each thing on the Shelf is drawn by what it is (`ShelfMath.js` decides): **folder** -> a file folder with a tab; **picture** -> a polaroid (white card, the picture itself in the window, name on the strip, a bit of tape at the top, leaning a little either way); **video** -> a video tape standing spine-out (dark case, coloured bands, "VHS", a white label with the name up it); **sound** -> a record in its sleeve (the disc over the top, a circle and a note on the sleeve); **archive** -> a cardboard box with a lid, tape and a label; **text or link** -> a sticky note (first lines of the text, a folded corner); **any other file** -> the book it was, with a glyph for the kind (PDF, code, document).
- Changed: things are different widths, so a shelf holds as many as fit its 360 px (nine books, fewer polaroids, more tapes), and the next one stands on the next shelf, as before. Colour and height still come from the item, so a thing keeps its look (the same seed as before: existing books keep their colour).
- Changed: the buttons come up as a column on tall thin things (book, folder, tape) and as a row along the foot of wide ones (polaroid, record, box, note); the line above the things reads "Pick one to open it" (was "Pick a book...").
- Added: `ShelfService` asks once whether a file is a directory (one `sh` over the paths that have no `dir` yet) and keeps the answer in the item (`dir`), so a folder stays a folder across restarts; older shelves are checked when they load.
- Not changed: the drop area, Paste, Clear, the header X, what a click does, the open / close rules, the card's size, the 60-item limit, vertical scrolling of the shelves.
- Files: `DockShelfCard.qml`, `ShelfBook.qml`, `ShelfFolder.qml`, `ShelfPolaroid.qml`, `ShelfTape.qml`, `ShelfVinyl.qml`, `ShelfBox.qml`, `ShelfNote.qml` (new, in `modules/sekai/dock/`), `functions/ShelfMath.js` (new), `ShelfService.qml`, `tests/dock-test/shelf_look_test.js`.

## Unreleased — Dock Shelf: an actual shelf, with the files as books

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source check passes (`tests/dock-test/shelf_look_test.js`). Look at: the dock's Shelf key, empty and with 1, 3, 9 and 12 files / texts (the "+ N more" tag), a picture among them, on every dock edge and in all three dock styles; light and dark mode and a group theme. Check: the books stand on the plank (their feet tucked just behind its top edge) and the card is tall enough for the tallest one lifted; pointing at a book slides it up and tilts it without flicker (also at its very bottom edge), shows its name above the row and its buttons on the spine; click opens a file / copies a text and closes the card; copy, folder and x work (x takes the book away); the tape does not touch a top-edge dock; the plank springs out wider as the card pops up; a file dragged over the card still shows the cyan frame.

- Changed: the Shelf card is drawn as a shelf in the shell's sticker look: a faint halftone of dots behind (`DotField`), two strips of washi tape on the top corners (the mint and pink ones the Edit Desktop shelf wears) and a **plank**: navy outline, hard shadow, the accent mint -> cyan gradient, a lit top edge, a diamond stud at each end and two diamond brackets under it (half hidden, so they read as triangles). The plank springs out from the middle as the card pops up.
- Changed: **each file or text is a book standing on the plank**, not a row. A spine in one of the five card colours (`Theme.attributeColors`), with a navy outline and hard shadow like every sticker, three white bands, a small label (the picture itself for an image, else a file / text icon) and the name running up the spine. Colour and height (four heights, 96 - 120 px) come from the item's id, so a book keeps its look. Up to **9** stand on the shelf (36 px, 4 px apart; was 6 rows); more show as "+ N more" on the line above.
- Pointing at a book slides it up 8 px and tilts it a little; its name shows on the line above the books ("Pick a book to open it" when none is pointed at), and its buttons (copy, open folder for a file, take it off) come up on the spine. The pointer's box does not move while the book slides, so it cannot flicker.
- Not changed: the drop area (files and text dropped anywhere on the card), Paste, Clear, the header X, what a click does, the open / close rules, the card's width. The card is taller (name line + book area + plank instead of rows).
- Files: `DockShelfCard.qml`, `tests/dock-test/shelf_look_test.js` (new).

## Unreleased — Dock: dragging things onto the Shelf

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The source check passes (`tests/dock-test/edit_tools_test.js`). ## Unreleased — Dock: Smart hide no longer flashes the dock after a reload or a kill

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (and I could not reproduce the flash here, so this is the cause I found by reading the code, not one I watched happen). New: `tests/dock-test/smart_hide_startup_test.js` (passes). Look at: `qs -c sekaipop` killed and started again, and a reload, with the dock on Smart hide and a window over its edge; then the same on Auto-hide. If Auto-hide still flashes, tell me and say whether the pointer was near that edge.

- Fixed: **Smart hide showed the dock over the windows for a moment at start-up.** The dock stays away until Hyprland's window list has come back, but that wait was cut off by the 0.6 s timer that switches the slide animations on, so a slow or failed `hyprctl` read (right after a kill or a reload, while Hyprland is busy) left it with "nothing is in the way" and the dock slid in until the next window event. The wait now ends when the list arrives, or after 5 s (`startupMs`) if Hyprland never answers (the hot edge still brings the dock up). Switching Smart hide on later, in a running shell, does not slide the dock away first. `Dock.qml` (`startupDone`, `smartWaiting`).
- Fixed: **a failed window-list read was never tried again** until Hyprland next reported a window event. It is asked again every 400 ms until the first windows and monitors lists are back. `services/DockService.qml`.

"Pin image" (which adds a new empty frame at the pointer) is hidden then, so a click can't add another picture by accident. ## Unreleased — Custom Image: right-click to pin it in front of windows and games

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The node tests pass (new checks in `tests/image-test/image_unit.js`; the preset, focus and size tests still pass). **Nothing was drawn**, so look at: right-clicking a picture on the desktop, a pinned picture over a tiled window, over a floating window and over a fullscreen game, clicking through it, "Unpin images" in the desktop menu, the PIN ON TOP switch in Edit Desktop's panel, and a GIF that is pinned.

- Added: **right-click a Custom Image on the desktop and it is pinned.** It moves from the desktop's layer up to the Overlay layer, so it is drawn in front of windows (tiled and floating) and of fullscreen games (Hyprland draws the Overlay layer over a fullscreen window), and a "PINNED ON TOP" note shows for a moment. `ImageWidget.qml` (`pinned`, `onTop`, `pinArea`, `pinNote`).
- Added: **a pinned picture takes no clicks**: the mouse goes through it to the window or game below (its input region is empty). It also never needs the whole screen as a window, even while "Lock all" is off, so there is no full-screen Overlay surface blending over a game.
- Added: **Desktop Focus Mode and Game Mode do not hide a pinned picture** (they hide the desktop's widgets behind your windows; a pinned one is meant to stay in front of them).
- Added: **three ways back**, since a pinned picture can't be clicked: the desktop's right-click menu has **Unpin images** while any picture is pinned (a right-click on the bare desktop where the picture is reaches it, because the picture passes the click through); **PIN ON TOP** in the picture's panel in Edit Desktop; and `qs -c sekaipop ipc call image unpin` (`pin` pins every picture). `DesktopMenu.qml`, `ImageInspector.qml`, `shell.qml`.
- Changed: **Edit Desktop shows a pinned picture as a normal frame** (under the editing chrome, selectable, draggable, resizable) and puts it back in front when you press Done. An empty frame is never pinned.
- State: new `pinned` key on each picture in `custom-images` state (`ImageMath.normalize`, `ImageService.setPinned` / `togglePinned` / `setAllPinned` / `anyPinned`). **A picture, or a desktop preset, saved before this has no `pinned` and wears unpinned**; a preset saved after it keeps each frame's pin.
- Cost to know about: a pinned GIF keeps drawing in front of a game, and an Overlay surface on a monitor where a game is fullscreen can stop Hyprland from scanning the game out directly. Pin stills over games if you see a change in latency.

## Unreleased — Fixes wave 1: top visualizer band, Pin image, popups on a bottom / right bar, tray menu on a standing bar

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The node tests pass (new: `tests/popup-test/popup_origin_test.js`, new checks in `image-test/image_unit.js`; `analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed before). **Nothing was drawn**, so look at: a band visualizer dragged to the top half of the screen, the desktop right-click menu's Pin image, a picture dropped / browsed into an empty and into a filled frame, every bar popup and the tray dropdown / menu on a bottom bar and on a right bar, and the tray menu on a left and a right bar.

- Added: **a band visualizer (bars, aurora, dots, mirror, tide, ribbons, scope, pulse) in the top half of the screen hangs upside down**: its base is on the top edge and it grows down, the way a vertical band already faces the edge it is nearest to. Decided when it is placed or dropped, so it does not flip while you drag it. The corner buttons (style, size, direction) go under the band instead of off the top of the screen. `VisualizerWidget.qml` (`card.topSide`, `updateSide`).
- Added: **"Pin image" in the desktop's right-click menu**: a Custom Image frame appears centred where you clicked (kept on the screen), already a drop target with BROWSE, selected for nothing else; the same empty frame the shelf tile and `image add` make. `DesktopMenu.qml`, `ImageService.addEmpty(screen, px, py)`.
- Fixed: **a dropped or picked picture looked stretched to the old frame.** The shape and the picture were drawn with the picture's size from the saved record, which is reset to 0 when a picture is put into a frame, so until the record had learned the new size the picture was squeezed into the old (empty) frame's shape. They now use the loaded picture's own size while the record does not have it (`picW` / `picH`). The record is also told again when a picture goes into a frame without its loading status changing (the same file put in again) and when the picture's size arrives after "Ready" (`onNatWChanged`, `onReframingChanged`, `onImplicitWidthChanged`); only the picture of the current file is reported. The frame then keeps its width and takes the picture's ratio, as before. `ImageWidget.qml`.
- Fixed: **the bar's popups on a bottom or a right bar grew out of (and shrank into) the wrong place**, as if the bar were on the top / left. A popup window is anchored by one corner and grows away from it (`Bar.popupGravity`: up on a bottom bar, left on a right bar), but the point it grows from was measured from `anchor.rect` as if it were always the window's top-left corner. It is now measured from the window's real top-left corner. Same for the tray dropdown and the tray menu. A bottom bar's popups also get the 6 px of air a top bar's have between bar and card. `SekaiPopup.qml`, `TrayPopups.qml`.
- Fixed: **the tray's right-click menu was far from a left / right bar.** The menu window is always as wide as the widest possible menu (so it never resizes), and the card was centred in it, about 70 px from the bar. Beside a standing bar the card (and the dropdown capsule) now sits against the bar's side of the window. Under a flat bar it is centred under the icon as before. `TrayPopups.qml`.

None of fixes_1's own changes (the palette icons, the bar's jump around Edit Desktop, the hot edge under a bottom bar, the CPU / RAM ring, sticky notes per preset, the performance work) touch those three files, so nothing had to be reconciled: those three files are taken from `white_line_7_wave` and everything else is `fixes_1` as it was. Every other file that differs between the two zips is a change only `fixes_1` made, and was left as it is.

## Unreleased — Welcome logo: the wave texture is back (it was built for a letter 9 px tall), and the rim gradient covers the letters instead of a strip

Checked as before (rendered frame by frame with Qt 6.12 and the compiled shaders, in a mint and in a peach palette like your screenshot's; Poppins stands in for Google Sans Flex; not run on a live Quickshell), and `welcome_unit.js` passes.

**What was wrong, and it was mine.** `FontMetrics.tightBoundingRect()` is a plain method: a binding that calls it is not run again when the font or the size changes afterwards. In two places the measurement was taken once, too early, for the default 9 px font:
- `WelcomeLogo.qml`: where each line's ink sits (the wave is laid on it). The wave texture was built for a letter 9 px tall, so it was a thin sliver at the baseline: the wave was missing. (The word-level restructure of the textures introduced this; before that each letter measured itself.)
- `WelcomeLetter.qml` (older): where a letter's ink sits. When the size or the font arrived after the letter was made (the unit is set after the logo is, or the font loads late), the measurement was stale (83 px for a glyph that is 165), the rim's gradient covered a strip across the middle of the letters instead of the whole rim, and the gloss highlights were put in the wrong place: the grey translucent band across "Sekai" in the screenshot.

Both measurements now read the metrics' ascent and descent first (those do announce a change), so they are taken again when the font is really set. A test checks that every `tightBoundingRect()` call has that guard.

- Changed: **the wave texture is rebuilt like the reference's**: along the bottom of each line a body of wave in a deeper shade of the letters' own colour (so it keeps its hue on any palette), a second, shorter wave behind it, and a bright crest line riding the edge of the first. One ripple across every letter and both lines, rising as it comes in, and it travels left to right two whole wavelengths per turn of the idle phase (before: one, and from a band too small to see). The ripple is 14% of the letters' height (was 5%).
- Files: `WelcomeLogo.qml`, `WelcomeLetter.qml`, `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`. No shader changed.

## Unreleased — Palette outline icons, bar jump around Edit Desktop, hot edge under a bottom bar, CPU/RAM ring, sticky notes per preset

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** The node tests pass (new: `tests/notes-test/sets_unit.js`, new cases in `notes_unit.js`, `preset-rename-test`, `preset-favorite-test`; `analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). **Nothing was drawn**, so look at: the Palette card and tool, a bottom and a right bar going into and out of Edit Desktop, the Quick Panel's hot edge with the bar on the bottom, the CPU/RAM pill next to the weather pill, and the sticky notes across two presets.

- Fixed: **filled icons in the Palette widget and its pop-up.** Three icons forced `fill` (the tool's header palette icon, the swatch's lock, the button's lit icon); they now follow `Theme.iconFill` (outline) like the rest. `PaletteTool.qml`, `PaletteSwatch.qml`, `PaletteButton.qml`.
- Fixed: **a bar on the bottom edge jumped to the top of the screen, and a right bar to the left, when Edit Desktop started and ended.** Cause: my last performance change (the bar window is only the strip) makes the window grow to the whole screen for Edit Desktop and shrink back. A layer-shell window anchored at the bottom or the right shows its old picture at the new surface's top-left corner for a frame or two when it changes size (the reason the bar never resized before). The compact window is now only used for a bar on the top or left edge (`Bar.growsFromOrigin`); a bottom or right bar keeps the screen-sized window as before. **Cost:** on those two edges the idle saving of the compact window is gone. For the top and left bars the card is now also worked out from the screen's size (`refWidth` / `refHeight`), not from the window that is still small for the first frames. `Bar.qml`.
- Fixed: **the Quick Panel's hot edge could not be reached with the bar on the bottom.** It was switched off there because its strip lay over the bar. It is on now: the bar cuts those 4 px (bottom centre, the hot edge's own size) out of its input region (`hotCut`, `Intersection.Subtract`), so the pointer reaches the hot-edge window under it. Nothing changes for the other edges. `QuickPanel.qml`, `Bar.qml`.
- Fixed: **the CPU / RAM pill's white ring was thicker than the other pills'.** Its ring was 8 px when the bar lies flat; it is 6 like the weather, date, battery and status pills. (The Discord and privacy pills still have 8 when flat; tell me if they should follow.) `ResourceIndicator.qml`.
- Changed: **sticky notes are separate per preset.** The notes on the desktop belong to the preset that is worn; every other preset keeps its own notes. Wearing a preset puts the worn one's notes away and brings that preset's own back; saving a preset gives it the notes you have (the worn one keeps its own); rename and delete take the notes along. A preset with no notes of its own yet (saved before this, or never worn since) keeps the notes you have and owns that copy from then on, so nothing is lost on the first switch. The to-do's tasks stay shared. The notes' on / off is still in the preset's file. State: `notes.json` gets `setName` and `sets` (an old file loads as before). `NotesService.qml` (`switchSet`, `adoptSet`, `renameSet`, `dropSet`), `NotesMath.js`, `DesktopPresetService.qml`, `PresetWidgets.qml`.

## Unreleased — Performance, stage 1: the bar's window is the strip, not the whole screen

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Measured on the real shell before the change (`tests/perf-test/gpu-attribution.py`, `bisect-gpu.sh`, 1080p / 144 Hz, Hyprland on the AMD iGPU, nothing playing): Hyprland 23 % of the GPU with everything on, 21 % with the dock hidden, 6 % with the dock and bar hidden, 3 % with only the bar hidden; `qs` itself under 1 % in all of them. Stopping the shell took Hyprland to 0.6 %. So the idle cost was Hyprland compositing the bar's surface, which was as big as the screen (`implicitHeight = screen.height`).

- Changed: the bar window is now the strip plus 24 px of room (for the popups' anchor points), anchored to its edge as before, so the strip is in the same place and every position in `Bar.qml` (mask, `popupX` / `popupY`, the status bar's x / y) is unchanged. It is the whole screen again while this monitor is in Edit Desktop, because `EditStage` shrinks the picture into the card from the window's size, and goes back after Done (when `EditMode.screenName` clears).
- New setting `bar.compactWindow` (default on). **If anything is off: `qs -c sekaipop ipc call options set bar.compactWindow false`** restores the screen-sized window at once.
- Check first: the tray dropdown and the tray right-click menu on a top bar (their anchor point is further below the strip than the other popups; if one goes missing or lands wrong, that is the cause), the other bar popups, Edit Desktop in and out (look for a jump on the bar when Done is pressed), a preset swap sliding the bar out and in, Game Mode, a vertical bar if you use one.
- Found afterwards with `tests/perf-test/qs-frames.py` (Qt's render-loop log): with the bar on, the shell rendered 144 frames a second at idle (the monitor's refresh rate; each one `sync=0 render=0`, i.e. nothing had changed), and none with the bar hidden. The cause is `UpdatesBadge`'s hop: one endless `SequentialAnimation` with a 5.2 s `PauseAnimation`, running whenever the pending updates reach `updates.nudgeAt` (30). A running animation makes Qt render every vsync, pause or not, and every frame is a commit Hyprland has to composite. Now a `Timer` starts the 0.4 s hop (same rhythm), so nothing runs between hops. `ipc call options set updates.nudgeAt 0` confirms it from outside (it switches the nudge off).
- Not changed: the dock (still 96 % of the screen high; hiding it did not change the idle load), the widgets, the wallpaper.
- Files: `modules/sekai/bar/Bar.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `tests/perf-test/gpu-attribution.py`, `tests/perf-test/bisect-gpu.sh`.

## Unreleased — Welcome logo: the light stroke is an even outline that follows the letters, its halftone wraps round them, and nothing is cut off

From a screenshot of the real shell: the stroke's halftone ended in a hard vertical edge on the right (and the stroke behind the kana in a flat edge on top), and the stroke was a wobbly blob (thick round "Pop", thin round "Sekai") instead of a band wrapped round the letters. I could not reproduce your exact screenshot (no Google Sans Flex here, Poppins stands in), so the cause is read from where the edge sat: about 70 px right of the last letter, which is where the logo's drawing area ended. Checked as before: rendered frame by frame with Qt 6.12 and the compiled shaders, not run on a live Quickshell; `welcome_unit.js` passes.

- Fixed: **the stroke was cut off at the edge of the logo's drawing area.** Everything of the logo is drawn into a box with a margin of 56 px round the word; the stroke and its shadow reached past it, and what reaches past is cut flat. The margin is 80 px now (`pad`), checked by a test against the stroke's width, its shadow and the kana, which stand highest.
- Changed: **the stroke is a true outline, not a blur cut at a level.** The silhouette of the whole logo (letters, rims, kana, sparkles) is drawn 28 times round a circle and the union is the stroke: the same width all the way round every curve, no blob, no bridging between letters. It grows from the letters outward as it comes in, and in again on the way out, so it hugs them the whole time (it used to scale the whole shape, which slid it off the letters). Width 17 px at 1080p (`strokeFull`). Its mint shadow, down and to the right, and its halftone are cut to the same outline.
- Changed: **the stroke's halftone wraps round the letters** (new `shaders/welcome_halo.frag`, replaces `welcome_dots.frag` for the stroke): the dots are biggest right beside the letters, shrink to nothing before the stroke's outer edge (so no dot is ever cut through) and swell on the lower right of every letter, like a shadow. The matte band that crosses it while it comes up still swells the dots under it, fading toward the edge. Before, it was one lattice shaded from the bottom right corner of the whole box, which is what ended in the hard edge.
- Fixed: a glint in the middle of its run when the exit started stopped where it was and hung over the letters while they collapsed; it is now reset when the idling stops.
- Without `welcome_halo.frag.qsb` the stroke is still the even outline, just without its dots. **Run `./shaders/compile-shaders.sh`** (a new shader).
- Files: `WelcomeLogo.qml`, `shaders/welcome_halo.frag` (+ `.qsb`), `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`.

## Unreleased — One thickness for the white line, taken from the clock

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** All the node tests still pass (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). **The clock is unchanged** (its digit blocks and its sticker numerals are drawn exactly as before). **Nothing was drawn**, so look at: the weather and calendar plates, the GOOD AFTERNOON chip, the weather card's "30" and "°C", "October" on the calendar, and the user card's UPTIME / SHELL / DESKTOP blocks, the weather disc and the calendar's keys. They should show the same white line as the clock's digit blocks.

- Why: the white line differed from item to item. The clock's digit blocks had 2 px, the other blocks 1 px, and sticker text scaled it with its size (the 64 px "30" about 3 px, the smaller texts 1.5 px).
- Now there is **one number**, `Theme.whiteLine` (2 px, the clock's digit blocks' line), in `modules/common/Theme.qml`. `StickerText` uses it at every size, and the other blocks write their ring as `Theme.borderWidth + Theme.whiteLine` (they were 1 px, now 2 px). The clock's digit blocks are written the same way and stay at 2 px. Change that one number to change them all together.
- Also the **big pink plates** on the weather and calendar cards (`SekaiPlate`'s default ring was 1 px, now `Theme.borderWidth + Theme.whiteLine`; the bar popups' plates follow because they share it) and the user card's **GOOD AFTERNOON chip** (it had no white line in this build, now it has the same one).
- Not changed: the clock (the sticker numerals keep their own 3.2 px line), the navy outline widths, the wide pale ring around each card, Dark mode's inner ring (`Theme.cardRing`), and the blocks that have no white line (ringWidth 3). `StickerText` is shared, so the popups, the lock screen and the wallpaper wheel get the same white line too.

- Fixed: a double-click on a day of the 2 x 2 calendar switched the card to its full size (to open the add form there), and that size was then saved. The size now only changes when you change it (Settings -> Desktop, the size key in Edit Desktop, or ipc). Single clicks, the wheel and the month name work as before.
