The pixel snap now also knows where the window's corner is on the monitor (`gridOffsetX/Y`, set by `QuickPanel.qml` in 2D only);
  - **sits dead centre on the monitor**: the straightened front's own middle is put on the middle (the tilted pose's middle is 27 / 14 design units away from it, which would leave a flat phone that far off-centre);
  - **every button, tile, card and chip casts its shadow straight down** (new `SekaiKey.flatShadow`; depth unchanged: OFF 3, ON 4): the page tiles, toggles, steppers, jump chips, header keys, clock chip, banner, name plates, the slider's value badge and knob, the section cards, the About mark, and the Bar page's round keys.
- Fixed (from a live look): **in 2D the top of the phone (the notch side) vanished for a moment when going to the Settings, then came back.** My first pass zeroed the entrance's tilt numbers (`tiltYaw/Pitch/Roll`) in 2D, but those are also the room the phone's picture and the window are sized with (`swingYaw/Pitch/Roll`), and the wobble that rings out when the turn lands rolls the phone by a few degrees: with no room left, its top was cut off by the edge of the picture while it rang. The numbers are back to the 2.5D ones (so the room and the phone's size are the same as in 2.5D) and only the entrance's tilt is switched off in 2D (`SettingsStage.tiltK`). Not run live; if something still blinks, note whether it is during the turn or after it lands.
- Switching the setting while the Settings are open straightens / tilts the phone in place (`straightK` eases).
- The turn out of the Quick Panel is unchanged. With 2D on, both phones are flat for the whole turn (it is a roll and a cross-fade).
- Not changed: the **Wallpapers** screen keeps its own look (its keys still use the down-right shadow), and the launcher and Overview phones.
- Files: `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsStage.qml`, `SettingsFace.qml`, `SectionCard.qml`, `SettingChip.qml`, `SettingSlider.qml`, `SettingsPageAbout.qml`, `SettingsPageBar.qml`, `SettingsPageQuick.qml`, `modules/sekai/phone/SekaiKey.qml`, `SekaiNavKey.qml`, `PhoneBody.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/SekaiTile.qml`, `SekaiHubTile.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`. Undo: put the old files back.
- Check on a live shell: pick 2D, open the panel and press the gear: the phone should turn into a flat landscape phone with a plain frame, centred on the monitor, with every shadow straight under its button; compare the text sharpness with the Quick Panel; open Wallpapers from it and back; switch to 2.5D with the Settings open: it should tilt back.
- Tune: `SettingsStage.flatLook` is the one switch; if the phone is a few pixels off-centre, look at `anchorX` / `anchorY`. If the page tiles look cramped with the straight shadow, the depth is `shadowBaseY` in each key.

## Unreleased — Welcome logo: every letter pops on its own, built in layers like the video, and slower

**Checked by rendering the real QML** (Qt 6.12, Mesa software GL, offscreen): the logo's timeline, a single big letter across its pop, the finished logo in three palettes, and the real `WelcomePage` intro. `node tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js` passes too. Not checked: Google Sans Flex itself (the renders used a stand-in rounded font, so the glyph shapes differ), and how it feels at 60 fps on a real GPU.

- Changed: **the logo is no longer one shape that scales in; every letter is a sticker built in layers**, the way the video's breakdown builds it (base, outline, extrusion, halftone, wave texture, hard shine). Each of the 8 letters and each of the 3 kana (they pop one by one now, like the video's hiragana) has its own pop (`WelcomePopLetter.qml`) and its layers come in one after the other:
  1. **motion**: one loose spring (`WM.popSpring`, about 20% past its size, swings back under once, settles). The tilt and the drop follow the same spring, so the letter swings past upright and back; it is stretched tall while it flies and squashed as it lands; it arrives out of focus and sharpens in the first third;
  2. **base**: gradient fill and gloss are there from the first frame;
  3. **rim**: the dark outline grows round the letter (starts at 4% of the pop);
  4. **extrusion**: the hard shadow slides out from behind a beat later (16%) and hops past its length on its own spring;
  5. **wave**: a deeper band with a rippled top edge rises up the bottom of the letter (30%);
  6. **halftone**: the dots unfold from the lower right corner (45%);
  7. **hard shine**: two slanted hard-edged bands sweep across the letter (60%).
  Every letter ends slightly crooked, each its own way (`LETTER_REST`).
- Changed: the logo's run is 2.8 s (a letter every ~175 ms, each popping for ~750 ms, overlapping), the stroke grows round them while they come and the sparkles pop last, bouncier. The video is a sped-up breakdown, too quick for a welcome screen.
- Changed: the logo as a whole has no pose (`HERO_POSE.logo` is neutral) and `WelcomePage.qml` skips the whole-hero blur and fade for it on the way in (`heroBox.ownMotion`).
- Added: while idle, every letter floats on its own phase (eased in over 0.9 s), the wave ripple drifts, and every few seconds a **glint** (the hard shine) runs across the word letter by letter.
- Fixed: the "Pop" line used to slide sideways while its letters appeared (a `Row` leaves invisible children out of its layout and the line is right-aligned by its width). Letters are hidden with scale and opacity instead of `visible`.
- Changed (`WelcomeLetter.qml`, also used by the icon slide): the layers are laid out on the glyph's real ink (`FontMetrics.tightBoundingRect`), not on the line box, so the gloss, dots and wave fall on small letters like "a" too; the rim, extrusion, halftone, wave and shine can be driven one by one (`outlineBuild`, `extrudeBuild`, `dotsRun`, `waveRun`, `wavePhase`, `shineRun`) and `blur` blurs the whole sticker through a padded layer that does not cut the shadow. Gradient fill (light, pastel, deeper), halftone only in the lower right, stronger gloss with a round highlight; the logo's fill is less washed out and its stroke's shadow softer.
- Files: `WelcomePopLetter.qml` (new), `WelcomeLogo.qml`, `WelcomeLetter.qml`, `WelcomePage.qml`, `WelcomeMath.js`, `WelcomeSlides.js`, `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`.

## Unreleased — Welcome screen: a five-slide first-start tour in the style of the Abimas Works logo-animation video

**Merged onto `pjsekai_2d_quickpanel_full_6_.zip`** (the 2D Quick Panel, Focus Mode and Game Mode version): only new files were added, plus two hunks in `shell.qml` and this entry; every existing test passes as it did before (`tests/analyzer-test/chart_test.js` fails the same way on your zip, it wants a command-line argument), and the page renders the same against the new `Theme.qml`. **Not run on a live Quickshell or Hyprland** (none here): the layer-shell windows, the exclusive keyboard focus, `Hyprland.focusedMonitor`, and Quickshell's own `Process` / `IpcHandler` have never run. Checked: `node tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js` (92 checks: the progress maths, the slide data, the real `welcome-state.sh` in a temp folder, bracket balance and wiring of the QML as text, and the Game Mode rules below); `qmllint` (Qt 6.12, no errors; it only notes the `Loader.item` type and unqualified accesses); the whole page **drawn and compared with the video's frames** under Qt 6.12 + Mesa software GL at 576x768 (the video's page size) and 1920x1080, with the real `Theme.qml`, the real widgets, Google Sans Flex and Material Symbols Rounded (the shell's own fonts); `WelcomeService.qml` itself driven through a first run, a skip, a manual show and the safety timer with stand-ins for Quickshell's `Process` and `IpcHandler`, including every Game Mode case below. Not measured: frame rate on a real GPU (the renders were software-only), memory, how it feels on your monitor, how your Qt version draws the stroke (it needs `MultiEffect` masks, Qt 6.5+, and font variation axes, 6.7+; the shell already needs both).

About the reference: **the video is a logo-animation breakdown (a "NodoCha" logo, made in After Effects), not an app screen.** What was taken from it: the pale page; tiny labels in three corners (what this is, the `n/5` counter, the brand); a centred hero; a bold title with a grey line under it; the giant faint copy of the logo behind the first and last slide; the logo springing in letter by letter with sparkles popping last; the pastel sticker lettering with its dark rim, extrusion, halftone and gloss; the pale stroke with a darker shadow; the words blurring in one by one and blurring out on a slide change; the black full-width band of the "Track Matte" slide; the left-aligned closing block; the framed picture. The video has no controls, so **how you move through it (click or Space / Enter / Right = next, Left / Backspace = back, Esc = skip), the words on the slides and the hint line at the bottom are my choice.** The logo is an original SekaiPop lettering in the same technique; none of the NodoCha artwork is reused.

- Added: **`WelcomeScreen.qml`** (windows), **`WelcomePage.qml`** (the page: layout, animations, keys) and the pieces it is made of in `modules/sekai/welcome/`: `WelcomeLogo` (the logo), `WelcomeLetter` (one sticker glyph), `WelcomeSparkle`, `WelcomeGhost` (the giant faint logo), `WelcomeBlurText` / `WelcomeCaption` (the words), and three heroes: `WelcomeGlyphHero` (an icon sticker built up layer by layer, like the video's breakdown), `WelcomeUnitsHero` (the black band with one sticker per unit theme, the current one marked), `WelcomeFrameHero` (your wallpaper, framed).
- Added: **`services/WelcomeService.qml`** and **`scripts/state/welcome-state.sh`**. The tour opens by itself the first time (1.5 s after the wallpaper and settings have loaded) and then stays away. `qs -c sekaipop ipc call welcome show | hide | toggle | reset | dismiss | status`: `reset` makes it come back at the next start, `dismiss` marks it as seen without showing it. A manual `show` never touches the "seen" state.
- How it is built (what was learned from Serpantinum's `Start.qml` + `FirstLaunch.qml`, adapted, not copied): one overlay window per screen, only the focused monitor shows the slides and takes the keyboard (the same latch `PolkitPrompt.qml` uses); a `Loader` that is inactive until the tour is on screen, so nothing of it exists otherwise; the page is driven by five 0..1 numbers (`pageIn`, `chromeIn`, `ghostIn`, `heroIn`, `textIn`) and every part draws itself from them, as Serpantinum's single intro animation drives scalar progress properties; the page reports `leaveRequested` / `exited` and the service decides what happens, like `FirstLaunch`'s `finished` / `closed` handling; a state script, like its `first_launch.sh`. **Different on purpose:** the "seen" flag is written when the tour *ends* (finished or Esc), not before it starts, and a try counter (3) stops a crash in the tour from ever trapping you in a start-up loop; the page itself imports nothing from Quickshell.
- Cost: the blur on a word, and on the hero, is a layer that exists only while that word / hero is coming in or going out. A finished slide is plain items. The logo's stroke is one blur + threshold on a texture that updates only while something moves. The slow float of the logo, the icon and the unit stickers runs only on those slides and only after they have landed; the picture slide and the last slide are fully still.
- Colours: everything follows the current unit theme (`Theme.pal`), so in Virtual Singer the page is a pale mint-cyan, not the video's green. The page is light in dark mode too, as the video is.
- Found while building, worth knowing for other animations: **a `NumberAnimation` inside a `SequentialAnimation` reads its `to` and `duration` when the whole sequence starts, not when its own step starts.** The slide swap first used `to: root.slide.ghost ? 1 : 0` after changing the slide, and got the old slide's value (the giant logo came back on slide 2). `go()` now works out `ghostDuring`, `ghostAfter` and `heroMsAfter` before starting, and the test fails if the swap animation reads `root.slide` again.
- Game Mode (new in this base) is respected, the way it is for Edit Desktop. A full-screen window that takes the keyboard exclusively has no business on top of a game, and Game Mode turns blur and animations off to save what this screen spends. So: **the automatic showing waits until Game Mode's state has been read and does nothing if it is on, without asking the script (a "check" counts as a try), so the tour comes at the next start with Game Mode off; `ipc call welcome show` does nothing while it is on; and if Game Mode comes on while the tour is open, the tour closes itself.** `services/WelcomeService.qml` reads `GameModeService.loaded` / `.enabled`; nothing else of Game Mode is touched.
- Not changed: every other file except `shell.qml` (the import and `WelcomeScreen {}`, two places) and this changelog.
- Known: the small kana line over the logo needs a font with Japanese glyphs on the system (any CJK font; Qt falls back to one by itself). The slide text lives in `modules/common/functions/WelcomeSlides.js`: change it there. `tests/analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed before this change (it expects a command-line argument).
- Files: `modules/sekai/welcome/*.qml` (11, new), `services/WelcomeService.qml` (new), `scripts/state/welcome-state.sh` (new), `modules/common/functions/WelcomeMath.js` (new), `modules/common/functions/WelcomeSlides.js` (new), `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js` (new), `shell.qml` (two lines). Undo: delete the new files and the two lines in `shell.qml`.

## Unreleased — Quick Panel 2D phone

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Checked: bracket balance of every edited file against the original zip; the preset unit tests still pass. Nothing was drawn, so the look is the thing to check first.

- New: **Settings -> Quick -> Quick Panel -> Phone: 2.5D / 2D.** 2D is the flat design the panel was drawn from ("Quick Panel Placeholder 2D" in the Figma). Off (2.5D) by default, so nothing changes until you pick it. The key is `quick.flat`; presets leave it alone.
- In 2D:
  - the phone **faces straight on** (yaw 0, pitch 0), has **no side wall, side buttons or bottom holes** (and its wall layer is not built, which also saves graphics memory), and **does not tilt** as it comes out of the pocket: it just rises, bounces and settles. The Power rail beside it follows the phone's pose, so it is flat too.
  - **every button casts its shadow straight down**, like the bar's pills (OFF 3 px, ON 4 px, hover +4), instead of down and to the right: the toggles and header keys, the Power rail, the volume sliders, the Notifications card, the heads-up card, and the Wi-Fi / Bluetooth / Night Light / volume mixer cards.
- 2D, second pass (from a live look): **the phone was off the middle** (the window reserves room on the right for the Power rail, and with no tilt left to reserve room on the left the phone sat to the left of the monitor's middle). In 2D the left now gets the same room as the right, so the phone's screen is dead centre. **It still read as 2.5D**: the 2D design has a plain rim and a plain notch, so 2D now leaves out the bright line along the rim and the camera lens (`PhoneBody.plain`); and the Power rail now stands as in the design: closer to the phone (`railGap` 26, was 60), 89 apart like the header keys (was 83) and starting higher (`railRawY` 197, was 276).
- 2D, third pass (from a live look): **less of the panel showed than in 2.5D** (the face was cut at y 925 against 2.5D's ~1020, since the tilted pose and the rail's lean reveal more for the same `panel.peek`): 2D now comes up 10% further (`peekEff` = `peek` x 1.10, capped at 1), which puts the cut at ~1018, so the same amount of notifications shows. (The blur it also tried to fix is dealt with in the next entry.)
- 2D, fourth pass (from live looks): **icons and text soft, the banner's corner stair-stepped, and soft again during the pull-out and the way out.** The 2.5D pipeline (the 2x `scene` picture plus the `face` picture) resamples a front that faces the screen twice, which hides in a tilted pose but not in a flat one, and drawing straight into the window instead gave up the anti-aliasing (the corner went jagged) and left the text scaled by the fit factor. Now the 2D phone is drawn the way the Wallpapers phone is (`PhoneBody.faceDirect`, as in WallpaperStage `direct`) the whole time it faces front, **including the pull-out, the bounce, the buzz and the way out**: the screen as a whole number of texels per device pixel, its top-left put on a whole device pixel every frame (`stage.direct`, `stage.snapX` / `snapY`, worked out from where the phone is each frame, so the motion simply steps by whole pixels), and the multisampled picture for the corners. Only the turn into the Settings uses the 2.5D pipeline. For this the 2D window is a whole, even number of pixels each way, so the compositor centres it on whole pixels.
- **Game Mode turns the 2D phone on by itself** (`Theme.quickFlat` = the Phone setting OR `GameModeService.enabled`), and switching Game Mode off puts back whatever the setting says. Game Mode already switches Hyprland's animations and blur off, so a flat, pixel-snapped phone that draws less is in step with it. Switching it from the panel's own Game Mode tile changes the open phone at once (it straightens in place; no animation to match, Hyprland's are off).
- 2D: **the round button beside the Microphone / Speaker sliders (the bell) was 11 px lower than the pills** -- a row holding a volume slider is taller (the pill plus its bar), and the other buttons in it were centred against that whole height. In 2D (`QT.pack(tiles, columns, topAlign)`) they are level with the pill instead, with the same 2 px the round buttons sit low in any row, so the bell lines up with the mic / speaker pills the way the coffee button does with Wi-Fi. 2.5D keeps its centring.
- The turn into the Settings is unchanged: the phone tips into the Settings pose on its way and the side wall comes back for the turn.
- Not changed: the launcher phone, the Settings phone and the Wallpapers phone keep their own look (their buttons still use the down-right shadow).
- Files: `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `modules/common/Theme.qml` (`quickFlat`), `modules/sekai/phone/PhoneBody.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `QuickPanelFace.qml`, `QuickToggle.qml`, `VolumeSlider.qml`, `PowerMenu.qml`, the four dialogs, and `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageQuick.qml`. Undo: put the old files back.
- Check on a live shell: pick 2D under Settings -> Quick -> Quick Panel; open the panel: it should be upright with a plain rounded frame and no side wall, tiles and header keys with the shadow straight under them; open Power: the round keys should stand in a straight column beside the phone; right-click Wi-Fi: the card's shadow should be straight down; open Settings from the panel: the turn should look as before; pick 2.5D again: everything returns.
- Tune: the 2D shadow depth is `shadowDy` in `QuickToggle.qml` / `VolumeSlider.qml` (`flatShadow` branches); the tile slot is 2 px taller in 2D (`implicitHeight` 77) to hold it.

## Unreleased — Game Mode bar

**Not run on a live Quickshell or Hyprland** (neither was available). Checked: the unit tests that run on their own still pass (`analyzer-test` was not run, it needs its own runner and nothing it covers was touched); bracket balance of the edited files against the original zip; the shell commands of `GameModeService` (switch on, switch off, Hyprland refusing each, marker kept / removed, restore read) were run against a fake `hyprctl`. The QML edits were read, not drawn.

- New: **Game Mode now also changes the shell**, not only Hyprland's effects. While it is on:
  - the **bar is a fixed strip with only the date, the clock and the weather** (in that order, in the middle), whatever your bar layout is. It keeps your bar style and edge. It is passive: the window takes no clicks, hovers or scrolls (the same 1 px as in Edit Desktop), and every open popup is put away as Game Mode comes on. Time and weather keep updating; everything else on the bar is hidden, and the CPU/RAM poll stops with it.
  - the **dock is gone** (its windows are not built), and the dock's own hot edge with it.
  - **all five desktop widgets are unloaded** (clock, to-do, visualizer, user card, weather card), not just hidden, so their timers, fetches and audio tap stop. Their own "shown" switches are not touched, so each comes back as it was.
  - the bar's cava feed (media pill visualizer) stops. Edit Desktop will not open (and is closed if it is open).
- Switching Game Mode off brings the bar, the dock and the widgets back at once, then reloads Hyprland's config as before.
- It survives `qs` restarts: `gamemode-state.sh` already leaves a marker in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` while it is on, and the shell reads it at start (cleared by logout / reboot).
- New: `qs -c sekaipop ipc call gamemode toggle | enable | disable | status` (a keybind can use it). The Quick Panel tile and the two Settings switches work as before.
- Changed: **Game Mode is no longer read back from Hyprland on every poll.** It used to be guessed from "animations are off", which would now take your dock away just because you turned animations off yourself. The cost: if you reload Hyprland's config by hand while Game Mode is on, Hyprland's effects come back but the bare bar stays until you switch Game Mode off (or off and on to apply the effects again). A refused switch ON changes nothing and notifies; a refused reload on OFF still gives you your dock back.
- Not changed: the bar stays on the Top layer, so a true fullscreen game covers it (a windowed / borderless game shows it). The notification popups, OSD, Quick Panel phone and launcher keep working.
- Files: `services/GameModeService.qml` (new), `services/QuickSource.qml`, `services/EditMode.qml`, `services/PcCavaService.qml`, `shell.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/Bar.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/Status_Bar.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`. Undo: put the old files back and delete the new one.
- Check on a live shell: switch Game Mode on from the Quick Panel tile — the bar should shrink to date / time / weather, the dock and widgets should disappear, and clicking the bar should hit the window under it; switch it off — everything returns where it was; restart `qs` with it on — it should start straight in the bare state; `ipc call gamemode toggle` should do the same as the tile.

## Unreleased — Stabilization pass 2: Game Mode tile turned itself off, and three errors from the log

**Not run on a live Quickshell or Hyprland.** Checked: the real poll fragment was run against a fake `hyprctl` printing `int: 0`, `bool: false`, `bool: true`, `int: 1`, only the dotted spelling, and garbage, with and without the marker; `gamemode-state.sh` was run in four modes and the marker follows ON / OFF / refused; the poll's printf has 15 fields and 15 arguments; all 14 unit tests pass. The QML edits were read, not drawn.

- Fixed: **Game Mode stayed on but the tile turned itself off** (reported from a live shell). Game Mode was being applied, but the tile reads it back with `hyprctl getoption animations:enabled` and only understood an answer printed as `int: 0`; Hyprland prints a bool option another way (`bool: false` is the likely one, the old line never matched it), so every poll read "no answer = OFF". The read now accepts `int:`, `bool:` and `float:` and treats `0` or `false` as Game Mode on. If no answer can be read at all, a marker file (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/pjsekai-gamemode`, written by `gamemode-state.sh` when ON succeeds, removed when OFF succeeds) decides, so the tile does not turn itself off while Game Mode is on. **Not confirmed**: what your Hyprland actually prints; if the tile still jumps back, run `hyprctl getoption animations:enabled` with Game Mode on and send the output.
- Fixed: **`SekaiScrollBar.qml` threw "Cannot read property 'yPosition' of undefined"** (5 times at start in the log). Its position lines assumed any `view` has a visible area; only a Flickable / ListView / GridView does. It now shows no bar unless it has one.
- Fixed: **`QuickPanelFace.qml` "Value is undefined and could not be converted to an object" (lines 1139, 1145, 1153)**: a button's `pos` could point past the layout slots for a moment after the button list changed. The three position Bindings now wait for the slot (`tile.slot`).
- Found, not changed: **binding loops on `implicitWidth` in `ClockWidget.qml:213` and `TodoWidget.qml:194`** (`implicitWidth: win.boxW`; boxW is built from `card.x / card.width`, which probably read the window's own width back). Qt breaks the loop on its own, so it is a warning, not a visible fault; fixing it means tracing the card's geometry on a live shell.
- Seen in the log, nothing to fix: `matugen` "File does not exist" on the first read (the file is written right after), `warp-cli isn't installed` (the tile says "Not installed"), `qt.qpa.wayland.textinput` (Qt's own).
- Not looked at yet: **`qt.svg.draw: The requested buffer size is too big`** (14 times): some SVG is asked to draw at a size over Qt's limit, probably at a high `sourceSize` with the shell scale.
- Files: `services/QuickSource.qml`, `scripts/hyprland/gamemode-state.sh`, `modules/common/widgets/SekaiScrollBar.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanelFace.qml`. Undo: put the old files back.

## Unreleased — Stabilization pass 1: Game Mode, one keyboard state, atomic state writes

**Not run on a live Quickshell or Hyprland** (neither was available). Checked: all 14 node unit tests pass (before and after); a static scan of all 242 QML files for unbalanced brackets, missing import paths, unknown `Theme.` / singleton members and duplicate ids found nothing real (the 5 bracket hits are regex literals the scanner can't read); `gamemode-state.sh` was run against a fake `hyprctl` in four modes (Lua config, hyprlang, refusing, missing); the four state scripts were run against a throwaway state dir. The QML edits themselves were only read, not drawn.

- Fixed: **Game Mode used `hyprctl keyword` only, which a Lua Hyprland config ignores** (the shell's own `hyprland-settings.sh` and `antiflashbang-state.sh` say so). On a Lua config the tile would flip ON, nothing would change, and the next poll would flip it back OFF. New `scripts/hyprland/gamemode-state.sh on|off` does what `hyprland-settings.sh` does: `hyprctl eval "hl.config({...})"` first, `hyprctl keyword` if Hyprland does not answer the eval, `hyprctl reload` for OFF. Same option set as before. **Not confirmed against a live Hyprland**: the Lua option names (`decoration.shadow.enabled`, `general.allow_tearing` ...) follow the dotted names `hyprland-settings.sh` documents; if one is refused the tile now says so instead of silently bouncing back.
- Fixed: **Game Mode reported success whatever happened.** A refused change (or no `hyprctl`) now sends one "Game Mode — Hyprland did not accept the change." notification (the shell's `error` glyph); the tile then follows what Hyprland really has.
- Fixed: **Game Mode's read-back only tried `animations:enabled`.** It now tries `animations.enabled` too (the same two-spelling look the anti-flashbang line already does).
- Fixed: **a second click while Game Mode was still being applied** flipped the tile but ran nothing (the running process swallowed it), leaving the tile showing the opposite of the screen until the next poll. Clicks are ignored until the last one has finished.
- Fixed: **the bar's Keyboard button and the Quick Panel's Keyboard tile each kept their own idea of whether wvkbd was running.** The bar started wvkbd as its own child (died with a shell reload, and did not know about one the tile had started, so a click started a second keyboard); the tile used pgrep/pkill. New `services/KeyboardService.qml` is the one state and the one toggle for both. The toggle decides from what is really running at that moment, starts wvkbd detached (survives a reload), ignores clicks until the last one has finished, and looks again 350 ms later. `QuickSource.keyboardOn` now reads the service, and the poll's `kb=` field is gone (14 fields, 14 arguments, checked).
- Changed (small): **a missing `wvkbd-mobintl` now shows a notification** ("wvkbd-mobintl is not installed.") instead of the button doing nothing. The bar header comment still says "does nothing"; the notification is the only difference.
- Fixed: **`barposition-state.sh`, `barstyle-state.sh`, `theme-state.sh` (theme, dark, darker) and `extras-state.sh` wrote straight into the live file** (`> "$file"`, or truncate-then-append), so a reader or a crash in the middle saw an empty or half-written file. They now write `<file>.tmp` and `mv` it over, like the other 14 state scripts.
- Test: `tests/notify-kinds-test/kinds.js` now also scans `services/KeyboardService.qml` (28 call sites, all on a listed glyph).
- Found, not changed: **`toggleNetwork`, `toggleBluetooth`, `toggleSpeaker`, `toggleMicrophone`, `toggleEasyEffects` in `QuickSource.qml`** have the same rapid-click gap Game Mode had. They put themselves right on the next poll (about 350 ms), so it is a brief wrong tile, not a stuck one. Left alone until it can be watched on a live shell.
- Files: `scripts/hyprland/gamemode-state.sh` (new), `services/KeyboardService.qml` (new), `services/QuickSource.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/UtilButtons.qml`, `scripts/state/{barposition,barstyle,theme,extras}-state.sh`, `tests/notify-kinds-test/kinds.js`. Undo: put the old files back and delete the two new ones.

## Unreleased — App launcher: typing works when the mouse is not over it

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Checked: the bindings read against `QuickPanel.qml` (`launching`, `open`) and the other windows that already use `Exclusive` (`Overview.qml`, `CheatSheet.qml`); nothing was opened.

- Fixed: **opening the app launcher with the mouse somewhere else, the search box took no keys until you moved the mouse onto it.** The launcher is the Quick Panel phone's Apps screen (`applauncher` / `launcherphone` IPC, SUPER tap), and its window asked for `OnDemand` keyboard focus, which Hyprland only gives to a window once the pointer is over it or it is clicked. While the Apps screen is up (`open && launching`) the window now asks for `Exclusive`, so the search box gets the keys right away. Everything else is as before: `OnDemand` for Settings, the Wi-Fi password and Quick's type-to-search, `None` otherwise.
- Also changed (the first try at this, kept): the corner phone's window (`LauncherPhone.qml`, the Wallpapers on its own) is `Exclusive` while open too.
- Fixed (second try): **it still needed a hover, and the focus grab was the cause.** The click-away grab (`HyprlandFocusGrab`) gives the keyboard to one of its windows when it starts (the panel or a bar, Hyprland's pick). The Apps screen now has **no focus grab at all**: it takes the keyboard `Exclusive`, and click-away is a new window, `catcher` in `QuickPanel.qml`: transparent, the size of the screen, on the Top layer under the phone, whose click closes the launcher. While Apps is up the phone's window is on the Overlay layer so it is always above the catcher (`useCatcher`). The Quick screen, Settings and the Wallpapers keep the focus grab exactly as before.
- Third try: **clicking beside the launcher still did nothing.** Two things now catch the click: `catcher` (the screen-sized window under the phone), and `awayArea` inside the phone's own window (`QuickPanel.qml`, right after `mask`): a huge `MouseArea` under everything, for setups where the compositor keeps the pointer on the window that holds the keyboard Exclusive, so the click arrives at the phone's window from far outside it. A press that is not on the phone, the Power rail or the Settings rectangle closes the launcher. Each logs a line (`pjsekai click-away: ...`; see it with `qs log -c pjsekai`), so if clicking still does nothing, the log says whether the click reaches the shell at all.
- Two earlier tries are gone: the grab with only the panel in it (clicking away stopped working), and bars joining the grab 400 ms later (same).
- Side effect: a click on a bar, the dock or a window closes the launcher too (the click is used up by the catcher). Not covered: a fullscreen window (the Top layer is under it; Esc still closes).
- Side effect (exclusive keyboard): while the Apps screen is up, no other window gets the keyboard (Esc closes it).
- Files: `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `modules/sekai/launcher/LauncherPhone.qml`. Undo: put `OnDemand` back in the two `keyboardFocus` lines.

## Unreleased — One scroll bar for the whole shell, and six lists that had none

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Checked: bracket balance of the nine changed files; the view / parent chain the bar relies on (a child of a Flickable has the content item as its parent, whose parent is the Flickable); nothing was drawn.

- New: **`modules/common/widgets/SekaiScrollBar.qml`**, the notification list's bar as a component: 6 wide, round, `Theme.pSurfaceRing`, only when there is more than fits (and the track is at least 40 px). Put `SekaiScrollBar {}` inside a ListView / GridView / Flickable and nothing else is needed: it rides in the view's content, follows `visibleArea`, and does not take part in the view's layout, so it works inside a Column or a card. Options: `view` (to use it from outside), `margin` (gap above / below the track, 8), `inset` (from the right edge, 2).
- Added to the six scrolling views that had no bar: **the launcher's Apps grid** (`LauncherFace.qml`), **the emoji picker** (`EmojiCard.qml`), **the Bluetooth list** (`BluetoothDialog.qml`), **the Wi-Fi / network list** (`NetworkDialog.qml`), **the volume mixer's stream list** (`VolumeMixerDialog.qml`) and **the Discord popup's list** (`DiscordPopup.qml`).

- Already the same: the Quick Panel's notification list and edit tray, and Settings.
- Left alone on purpose: the cheat sheet (its bar is part of the paper look), the Todo desktop widget (a desktop widget with its own light look), the notification popup stack (a stack of cards in the corner, not a list), and the three sideways rows in edit mode (shelf, presets, themes), which have no indicator.
- Not checked: how the ring gray looks on each background (the dialogs and the launcher have different ones). One line to change if one is hard to see: `color:` in `SekaiScrollBar.qml`.
- Files: `modules/common/widgets/SekaiScrollBar.qml` (new), `modules/sekai/launcher/LauncherFace.qml`, `EmojiCard.qml`, `ClipboardCard.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/BluetoothDialog.qml`, `NetworkDialog.qml`, `VolumeMixerDialog.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/DiscordPopup.qml`, `modules/sekai/wallpaperSelector/WallpaperFace.qml`. ## Unreleased — Settings: jump chips under the header, so you don't get lost on a long page

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Checked: bracket balance of the two changed files and the geometry from the code (header ends at y 98, chips sit at 107..153, the first card's plate at 170); nothing was drawn, so the chip look and the scroll animation are the things to look at first.

- New: **a row of section chips** at the top of every Settings page that has two or more titled cards (General, Bar, Desktop, Hyprland, About, Quick). One chip per card, with the card's name. They are the same bouncy keycaps as the other Settings buttons (`SettingChip`): they press into their shadow and spring back, and the one for the card in view is lit in the white ring and pulses as it takes over while you scroll. Click a chip and the page scrolls there (300 ms glide; grabbing the page stops it). The row scrolls sideways (mouse wheel or drag) when it is wider than the page (Desktop has 11 cards), and follows the lit chip. Cards that are switched off (for example "One-time setup") get no chip, and a card that appears later gets one.
- Fixed: **clicking the second-to-last chip lit the last one.** The last cards of a page are short, so the page cannot always bring the card you picked to the top, and the "at the bottom, so the last card" rule won. A chip you click now stays lit (`pinned`) until you scroll by hand.
- Changed: a page with chips has its cards 48 px lower than before (the row needs the room). A page with fewer than two titled cards has no row and its cards sit exactly where they did. The chips are the shell's lit look, not each card's own colour.
- Changed: `SettingChip.capH` is now a normal property (default 46, unchanged); the chips use 42.
- Files: `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPage.qml` (sections, active card, scroll-to, the strip), `SettingChip.qml`, `SettingsFace.qml` (the page area starts 12 higher and is 12 taller, same bottom; the menu is untouched). Undo: put the two files back.
- Tune: chip height is `capH: 42` in `SettingsPage.qml` (their text is SettingChip's 18 px; below about 42 the text no longer fits inside the white ring); `topGap` (60) is the room the row takes, keep it about 18 more than the chip height.

## Unreleased — Power menu: Keep Awake no longer blocks Sleep / Hibernate; they lock first; Log out closes windows first

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no `qmllint`, no GPU): none of the buttons was pressed. Checked: bracket balance of the two changed QML files (the counts match the original's offsets, so only balanced brackets were added), and `session-logout.sh` against a fake `hyprctl` (real pids terminated, `pid: -1` / `0` skipped, `hyprctl dispatch exit` called last). The lock-then-suspend timing is reasoned from `LockState.qml` only.

- Fixed: **Sleep and Hibernate failed while Keep Awake was ON.** Keep Awake held `systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep --mode=block`, and a `sleep` block lock makes `systemctl suspend` / `hibernate` refuse for a normal user. It is now `--what=idle`: auto-lock, screen-off and auto-suspend (hypridle, logind's idle action) still stay off, but manual Sleep / Hibernate work, and closing the lid suspends as usual. If you want the lid held awake too, put `idle:sleep` back, knowing the two buttons then fail while it is on.
- Changed: **Sleep and Hibernate lock first.** `QuickSource.lockThen()` starts the lock ring (`LockState.lock()`), waits for phase `locked`, waits 400 ms for the lock surface to be drawn, then suspends / hibernates, so the machine wakes on the lockscreen. Already locked: straight to sleep after the 400 ms. Safety: if the lock has not engaged after `ringMs + 3 s`, it suspends anyway. `QuickPanel.qml` closes the panel for both, the way Lock does.
- Changed: **Log out asks every window's program to quit first** (`scripts/system/session-logout.sh`: SIGTERM to each pid from `hyprctl clients -j`, 1.5 s grace, then `hyprctl dispatch exit`). Pids <= 1 are skipped.
- Changed: power off, reboot, sleep, hibernate and firmware reboot try `systemctl ... || loginctl ...`.
- Not changed: a Keep Awake lock held by *another* program (check with `systemd-inhibit --list`) still blocks Sleep / Hibernate; the buttons still show no error when a command fails. For lid-close and idle sleeps (not the Power menu) the lock still has to come from hypridle, e.g. `before_sleep_cmd = qs -c sekaipop ipc call lock lock` and `after_sleep_cmd = qs -c sekaipop ipc call lock focus`.
- Files: `services/QuickSource.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `scripts/system/session-logout.sh` (new). Undo: put the old files back.

## Unreleased — Memory: the phones give their layer buffers back while closed

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no GPU): nothing was drawn or measured. Checked: bracket balance of the six changed files, the node tests (unchanged logic), and Quickshell's `proxywindow.cpp` (a hidden window is a plain `setVisible(false)`, no `persistentSceneGraph` override, so Qt's default applies: a hidden window keeps its scene graph and the buffers of every layer that is still switched on). How much comes back is **not measured**: it depends on your monitor size and scale, and Qt / the driver may keep some freed memory in the process. Compare `grep -E 'Rss|Pss' /proc/$(pidof quickshell)/smaps_rollup` before and after, with the phone not opened for over 20 s (`tests/perf-test/perf-baseline.sh` samples the same figures).

- Why: `PhoneBody` has three big layers (scene, side wall, face). Each one keeps a multisample colour buffer, a multisample depth/stencil buffer and a resolve texture, at `samples` x the picture's size. They were switched on for as long as the phone existed, so after the first open of the Quick Panel, the Settings, the Wallpapers or the corner Apps phone, those buffers stayed allocated until logout. On an iGPU that is system RAM.
- Changed: **`PhoneBody` has a cold / warm state.** `windowShown` (set by the owner: is my window mapped?) and its own `visible` give `onScreen`. A phone that has been off screen for `coolDelay` (20 s) goes cold: `scene`, the wall layer, the face layer and the three edge-button layers are all switched off and their buffers are freed. The instant it is on screen again it is warm again, in the same event-loop turn and before the first frame is drawn. Warm, every layer is exactly what it was: same size, same samples, same mipmaps, same quality tiers. A phone opened again within 20 s finds its buffers still there. A phone created hidden (all of them, at shell start) never allocates them until it is first shown.
- Wired: `QuickPanel.qml` (`windowShown: win.visible`), `LauncherPhone.qml` (`win.visible`), `SettingsStage.qml` and `WallpaperStage.qml` (`root.active`). An owner that does not set it keeps the old always-resident behaviour.
- Changed: **`PowerMenu`'s 8-sample layer is only on while the menu is up** (`layer.enabled: root.visible`), about 25 MB that was kept for the whole session after the first open. Same samples and size while it shows.
- What you may notice: the first open after 20+ s closed allocates the buffers on its first frame (that frame is already drawn on the light tier, `moving` is true while the window is unmapped). If you see a hitch there, raise `coolDelay` in `PhoneBody.qml`; if you see nothing, lower it.
- Not changed: `restSamples` (4), `superSample` (2), the megapixel caps, every quality tier. To trade a little edge smoothness for about half the remaining multisample memory, try `restSamples: 2` in `PhoneBody.qml` and look at the diagonal edges of the tilted phone.
- Files: `modules/sekai/phone/PhoneBody.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanel.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/PowerMenu.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsStage.qml`, `modules/sekai/wallpaperSelector/WallpaperStage.qml`, `modules/sekai/launcher/LauncherPhone.qml`. Undo: set `coolDelay: 2147483647` in `PhoneBody.qml` (phones stay warm), and `layer.enabled: true` in `PowerMenu.qml`.

## Unreleased — Desktop Focus Mode: optional "Unload hidden widgets" (frees the widgets' memory)

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Checked: bracket balance of the changed files; the node tests (unchanged logic). How much memory comes back is not measured here: Qt may keep freed memory in the process instead of returning it to the system, so the saving you see in `htop` can be smaller than the widgets' own size.

- Added: **`focus.unload`** (default off), Settings > Desktop > Desktop Focus > Unload hidden widgets. A widget that has been hidden for 2 s is destroyed by its `LazyLoader` in `shell.qml` (one `FocusGate` per widget with `unloads: true`) and built again when the desktop is back, one after another (70 ms apart). Position, shown state, design and the to-do list live in the services, so they survive; only what the widget itself held (images, scroll position, animation state) is rebuilt.
- Needs suspension on (unloading is suspension taken to its end). The visualizer is not unloaded while it may keep listening in the background (its audio tap is inside the widget).
- Edit Desktop and the `resetPosition` / `refresh` ipc calls load the widgets as before.
- Files: `services/FocusService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/FocusGate.qml`, `shell.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`. Undo: switch the option off.

## Unreleased — Desktop Focus Mode: the desktop widgets leave while you work, and stop working

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no `qmllint`, no GPU): nothing has been drawn or toggled. Checked: `tests/focus-test/focus_unit.js` (new, runs under node: the visibility rules, incl. 10,000 random layouts), bracket balance of the changed files, and Quickshell's own source (the Hyprland models, signals and `refreshToplevels()` this relies on exist as used; it does not refresh a window's floating state on `changefloatingmode`, which is why the service asks).

- Added: **`services/FocusService.qml`** (visibility) and **`FocusGate.qml`** (what each widget reads). Per monitor, from that monitor's active workspace: a tiled window hides the widgets; floating windows alone do not (switchable); tiled + floating hides; a fullscreen window counts as tiled; scratchpads count only while shown; minimised, unmapped, hidden and shell-owned windows never count. Edit Desktop always shows everything. Hiding is instant; coming back waits 90 ms for the answer to hold, then the widgets wake one after another (70 ms apart).
- Events, not polling: no new `hyprctl` process and no new socket. Quickshell's models drive it, `Hyprland.refreshToplevels()` is asked after openwindow / changefloatingmode / movewindowv2 / fullscreen / pin, and every other event is ignored. With Focus Mode off nothing is listened to.
- Four separate ideas per widget: **hidden / show** (visibility + rendering: the existing swallow and unmap), **suspended** (clock, uptime, weather polling, audio tap). Hiding without suspending is a valid setting.
- Visualizer: its audio tap (analyzer or cava) stops while hidden unless "Visualizer in background" is on, or suspension is off. In the background the engine slows to 10 updates a second and the key bed ignores notes. Playback and shared media services are untouched.
- Settings > Desktop > Desktop Focus: master, hide with tiled, hide with floating, suspend hidden widgets, visualizer in background, reduce effects (pauses a video wallpaper while a window is on the monitor). Keys `focus.*`, saved with the other options and not part of Desktop presets. Defaults: on, tiled on, floating off, suspend on, visualizer background off, reduce effects off.
- Changed: `WeatherSource` no longer fetches every time polling is switched back on; it looks again only if the reading is over 5 minutes old (the first look at start is unchanged).
- IPC: `qs -c sekaipop ipc call focus toggle | enable | disable | status`.
- Files: `services/FocusService.qml`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/FocusGate.qml`, `modules/common/functions/FocusMath.js`, `tests/focus-test/focus_unit.js` (new); `ClockWidget`, `TodoWidget`, `UserCardWidget`, `WeatherCardWidget`, `VisualizerWidget`, `SekaiVisualizer`, `WallpaperLayer`, `SettingsPageDesktop`, `SettingsService`, `DesktopPresetService`, `WeatherSource`, `shell.qml`. Undo: switch Focus Mode off in Settings, or put the files back.

## Unreleased — The shell's own notifications get their own icon (screenshots, recordings, timers, errors ...)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no `qmllint`, no GPU): the badge has not been drawn, so its proportions, the glyph outline and the plate colours are reasoned only. Checked: `tests/notify-kinds-test/kinds.js` (new, runs under node: the glyph -> colour map, and that all 26 call sites below send a known glyph), `sh -n scripts/system/region-action.sh`, bracket balance of the changed QML files, and the earlier popup logic test still passes.

- Added: **`ShellBadge.qml`**, the icon a notification the shell sent itself wears: the Project SEKAI card-attribute look, a pastel fill (Cool / Cute / Happy / Mysterious / Pure), a white glyph outlined in `#555473`, a thick outline and a hard offset shadow in the same colour, and a small highlight. App and browser notifications are unchanged: they still show the app's own icon or the sender's picture.
- How the shell tells them apart: **a notification whose icon is `material:<glyph>` is the shell's own** (that convention already existed, e.g. Music Recognition). `NotifyKinds.js` picks the colour from the glyph (screenshots / copy / text recognition / image search: Cool; recording and errors: Cute; timers: Happy; terminal / VPN / music recognition: Mysterious; updates and wallpapers: Pure). A glyph that is not listed still gets a stable colour from its name, so `notify-send -i material:whatever` from your own scripts gets a badge with no change to the shell.
- Changed: the badge shows on all three cards: the popup (its plate also takes the badge's colour), the phone's heads-up card, and the Quick Panel's list. Saved history needs no migration (the icon string was already saved).

Those that had no app name now say `pjsekai`.
- Changed: **the region selector's "Screenshot saved" no longer uses the screenshot itself as the notification picture** (it was `-i <file>`), so it shows the camera badge like the other screenshot paths. Say so if you would rather have the thumbnail back.
- Files: `modules/common/widgets/ShellBadge.qml` (new), `modules/common/functions/NotifyKinds.js` (new), `tests/notify-kinds-test/kinds.js` (new), `modules/sekai/notificationPopup/NotificationPopupCard.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/QuickPanelFace.qml`, `modules/sekai/quickPanel/NotificationCard.qml`, `services/NotificationSource.qml` (comment), and the call sites in `modules/common/functions/Extras.js`, `modules/sekai/bar/UtilButtons.qml`, `services/QuickSource.qml`, `services/RegionService.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `services/PackageUpdatesService.qml`, `services/ScrollOverviewService.qml`, `services/TimerService.qml`, `scripts/system/region-action.sh`. ## Unreleased — Notification alert defaults to the phone; the popup cards get depth

The option row lists Phone first. `NotificationSource.alertStyle` has the same default. Only values that differ from the default are saved, so anyone who never touched the setting is on the phone now, and anyone who had picked Phone keeps it (the saved key simply matches the default and is dropped on the next change).

Moments: the sticker pops in as the card lands, one band of light crosses the card, the sticker wiggles and the plate brightens while the pointer is on it. All of them are one-shots, so an idle stack animates nothing.

## Unreleased — Notification popups: a stack of cards in the corner (Illogical Impulse style), next to the phone alert

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no `qmllint`, no GPU): nothing below has been drawn or clicked. Checked: bracket balance of every changed file against the original; `tests/notification-popup-test/popup_logic.js` (runs under node, 31 checks) takes the real function bodies out of `NotificationSource.qml` and `NotificationPopup.qml` and tests the stack: newest first, the cap of 4, read time, expiry, hover pause (and a moment more after leaving), close / dismiss / clear, the monitor choice, and the cards' list staying in step with the stack (arrivals on top, leavers kept until their card has slid out). The drawing, the animations, the mask and the placement are **reasoned only**.

- Added: **a notification popup stack.** A new notification now shows as a card in the top-right corner (newest on top, up to 4, the rest wait in the Quick Panel's list as always). It slides in, stays for the same time the phone's heads-up card did (4.5 to 12 s, longer for long or critical ones), and slides out. The pointer on a card stops its clock; leaving gives it at least 1.5 s more. **Click**: hide the popup (the notification stays in the Quick Panel's list). **Right click or swipe**: dismiss the notification (the same as in the list). It looks like the heads-up card (outline, hard shadow, the panel's colours) a little smaller. Do not disturb works as before (nothing announces itself; critical ones break through). It stays out of the way while the phone is up (its list shows everything), and it is drawn on the monitor that was focused when the stack started to fill.
- Where: like the reload report. Top-right, clear of the bar (a top bar pushes it down, a right bar pushes it left); with the bar at the bottom: bottom-right, above the bar, the stack growing upward. One window per screen, as tall as 4 cards, but only the cards take the pointer (a mask), so the rest of the corner still clicks through.
- Added: **Settings > General > Notification alert: Popups | Phone.** `notify.style`, default `popups`. `phone` is the old behaviour (the phone rises, buzzes and shows the heads-up card) and shows no popups. Switching live hides the other style's leftovers. "Buzz on arrival" only matters in the phone style (the row says so).
- Changed: `NotificationSource` gets `popups`, `pushPopup` / `closePopup(s)` / `clearPopups` / `holdPopup`, a 200 ms tick that only runs while the stack is not empty, and `alertStyle` / `popupsSuppressed` (set in `shell.qml`). `dismiss`, `dismissMany` and `clearAll` also take the popup off the stack. The read-time formula moved into `readMs()` (same numbers as before; the toast uses it too).
- Scaling: the window is on `ScaleHost` like the OSD (an unconverted window until the pixel-snapped rewrite reaches it), so at 75 / 125 / 150% its edges are as soft as the other `ScaleHost` windows.
- Not changed: the phone alert itself (buzz, heads-up card, rising), the notification history file, the Quick Panel's list, do-not-disturb, `scale.json`.
- Files: `services/NotificationSource.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `shell.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, `modules/sekai/notificationPopup/NotificationPopup.qml` (new), `modules/sekai/notificationPopup/NotificationPopupCard.qml` (new), `tests/notification-popup-test/popup_logic.js` (new). Undo: set Notification alert to Phone, or delete the `NotificationPopup { ... }` block and the `alertStyle` / `popupsSuppressed` lines in `shell.qml` (and put `showToast(e)` back as the only alert in `ingest`).

## Unreleased — Performance pass 2: the status pill's 8-second poll forks half as many processes (nothing visible changes)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no `qmllint`, no GPU): nothing was timed on the real shell, so no speed-up is claimed beyond what is counted below. Checked: `services/status-icons.sh` old vs new, byte for byte, over 7,131 combinations of mocked `nmcli` / `bluetoothctl` output (Wi-Fi and Ethernet up or down, names with colons, spaces, unicode, empty names, several matching lines, Bluetooth off, no adapter, device with no name, tools missing) under dash and bash: 0 differences. External processes per run, counted with PATH shims: 16 before, 8 after (the 8 left are `timeout`, `nmcli` and `bluetoothctl`, two of each kind of question). Command-substitution subshells: 6 before, 4 after.

- Changed (**tested**: output identical, see above): **`status-icons.sh` picks the first matching line with shell builtins instead of a `sed | head` pipeline per value.** `StatusIconsService` runs it every 8 s for the whole life of the shell (it is the one repeating Timer that is always on), and each run used to start 8 extra short-lived processes (4 `sed`, 4 `head`) on top of the `nmcli` / `bluetoothctl` calls. The questions asked and the `key=value` output are unchanged. File: `services/status-icons.sh`. Undo: put the old script back.
- Added: `services/perf-test/perf-baseline.sh` — read-only idle/interaction sampler (CPU of quickshell and of its finished child processes, wake-ups, RSS/PSS, system-wide GPU busy where the driver exposes it). Run it before and after any change under the same conditions.
- Not changed: the 8 s interval, any look, size, colour, animation, IPC target or saved-state format.

## Unreleased — Performance pass: five small, verified savings (nothing visible changes)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no `qmllint`, no GPU): nothing below was timed, so no speed-up is claimed, only what each change stops doing. Checked: bracket balance of every changed file against the original; `services/perf-test/parse_equiv.js` (new, runs under node: the new cava parser gives byte-for-byte the same numbers as the old one over 20,000 random lines). Every change is marked **reasoned only** (read and traced, not run).

A whole-tree audit (timers, polling, Images, layers and effects, Canvas, ScreencopyView, Repeaters, windows, scripts) found the shell already gated nearly everywhere: every repeating Timer except the ones fixed below has a `running:` gate, images are decoded at display size and loaded asynchronously, Canvas items paint once per size, thumbnails are cached on disk, the clock, to-do, user-card and visualizer windows already shrink to their card at rest. So this pass is five targeted fixes, not a rewrite; the rest is listed in the report as "left alone" or "optional".

- Changed (**reasoned only**): **the CPU & RAM poll only runs while its module is on the bar.** `ResourceSource` read `/proc/stat` and `/proc/meminfo` every 5 s, forever, once per monitor, and ran two regular expressions over them each time, even though the module is **off in the default bar** (`bar.resources: false`). `Bar.qml` now sets `wanted: statusBar.shows("resources") || win.resourcePopupOpen`. Switching the module on (Settings or Edit Desktop) reads at once; the CPU figure is a difference of two reads, so it is right from the second read on (same as right after a shell start). Files: `ResourceSource.qml`, `Bar.qml`. Undo: delete the `wanted: ...` line in `Bar.qml` (`wanted` defaults to `true`, the old behaviour).
- Changed (**reasoned only**): **Edit Desktop only photographs the widgets while the shelf's WIDGETS tab is showing.** `EditPreview` took a `grabToImage` of every desktop widget once a second (a GPU render plus a read-back into RAM, five widgets) the whole time Edit Desktop was open, but only the WIDGETS tab draws those pictures (`EditTraySlot.qml`). It now stops on the BAR, THEME and PRESETS tabs (`EditMode.currentTab`), keeps the last picture, and takes a fresh one on the first tick after you come back. The pictures are still dropped when Edit Desktop ends. File: `EditPreview.qml`. Undo: in `EditPreview.qml` set `running:` back to `EditMode.active && root.target !== null && root.widgetId !== ""` and `onRunningChanged` back to `if (!root.running) { ... }`.
- Fixed (**reasoned only**): **dragging a Settings slider no longer re-runs every option binding and re-saves the file on every mouse move.** `SettingSlider` emits `moved` on every mouse-move event, most of them on the value it already snapped to, and `SettingsService.set()` replaced `values` each time (re-running the roughly 190 bindings that read an option) and started `settings-state.sh save` each time. `set()` now returns early when the option already has that value, once the saved file has been read (before that it still writes, so a change made during start-up is kept). The saved file's format is unchanged. File: `services/SettingsService.qml`. Undo: delete the `if (root.loaded && root.get(key) === value) return` lines in `set()`.
- Changed (**tested**: output identical, see above): **the cava line is parsed in one pass.** `PcCavaService.qml` (the bar pill's visualizer) and the two cava readers in `VisualizerWidget.qml` built three or four arrays per frame with `split().map().filter().map()`, at up to 60 frames a second while music plays. Now a loop builds one (`VisualizerWidget.parseBands`, and the same loop in `PcCavaService`). Same numbers out. Files: `services/PcCavaService.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `services/perf-test/parse_equiv.js` (new). Undo: put the old expression back (`data.split(";").map(v => parseFloat(v.trim())).filter(v => !isNaN(v))`, and for the pill `.map(v => Math.min(1000, v * s))` after it).
- Not changed: any look, size, colour, font, animation, duration, easing, resolution, IPC target, keybind or saved-state format.
- Files: `ResourceSource.qml`, `Bar.qml`, `EditPreview.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/PcCavaService.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `services/perf-test/parse_equiv.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Apps launcher: a smooth entry (like the Quick Panel's)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here), and nothing was timed on a real GPU. Checked: brace, bracket and parenthesis balance of `LauncherFace.qml` and `QuickPanel.qml`, and that every use of the removed reveal and the new warm-up timer reads right.

- Fixed: **the grid faded and rose in while the phone was still sliding up, and that redrew the whole screen on every frame of the slide.** The Quick screen's entry is cheap because its picture does not change while the phone moves (only the phone is moved). The Apps screen changed it on every frame (opacity, scale and height of the grid for 380 ms), so each frame redrew the big supersampled, multisampled screen layer and then the phone around it. `LauncherFace.reveal` (new, `false`) turns that off: the grid is just there with the phone. Set `reveal: true` for the old rise and fade.
- Fixed: **the Wallpapers gallery was built in the middle of the slide.** `wallpapersWarm` builds the gallery (reads the wallpaper folder, loads thumbnails) whenever the phone is up on Apps, and it started the moment the phone opened, so that work landed on the slide-in as a stall. `QuickPanel.qml` now waits until the phone has landed: `warmReady` turns on `warmDelayMs` (900) after the phone is up on Apps, and only once it is no longer sliding or turning (otherwise it tries again a little later). It goes off again when the phone leaves the Apps screen or goes away; going into the Settings or Wallpapers keeps it. The Apps screen has no Wallpapers tile now (only the `wallpaper` action row when you type it), so if you never open Wallpapers that way, `wallpapersWarm: false` stops the warm-up for good.
- Not changed: the slide itself (`launchInMs`, `launchTiltMs`, the curves), the Quick screen, and the Quick-to-Apps screen change while the phone is already up.
- Files: `LauncherFace.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Apps launcher: smooth typing and scrolling

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here), and nothing was timed on a real GPU. Checked: brace, bracket and parenthesis balance of `LauncherFace.qml` and `AppSource.qml`; `services/launcher-test/rank_unit.js` (new) runs the real functions out of `AppSource.qml` against the old ranking over 400 fake apps and ~580 queries: same apps, same order, no mismatches.

- Fixed: **every letter typed rebuilt the whole grid.** The grid's model was a fresh array each time, so Qt threw away every tile and built them all again (each one a `Pill` with its own colour fades, press animations and icon lookup), all on the one thread that draws the phone. `LauncherFace` now feeds the grid a `ScriptModel` keyed by app (`objectProp: "key"`), which compares the new list with the old and only adds, removes and moves the tiles that really changed.
- Changed: **`AppSource` builds its list once.** `entries` is one plain object per app (name-sorted, search fields already lower-cased, a unique `key`), rebuilt only when apps are installed or removed. Typing compares strings instead of reading every desktop entry, and the last query's answer is remembered. `rankEntries(q)` gives entries; `rank(q)` and `allApps` still give DesktopEntry objects, with the same order as before.
- Changed: **icon paths are looked up once** (`AppSource.iconFor`), not by every tile that is built or reused.
- Changed: **the tile is lighter, the same picture.** Four shapes with the Apps screen's own colours instead of a `Pill` (which walked up the tree for its colours and carried two press animations, three colour fades and a ring gradient per tile). Resting, lit, picked and pressed look the same; the press is one animation (80 ms in, 260 ms spring back, as before). The lit glide is 120 ms instead of 170 (every frame of it redraws the whole phone): put `170` back in `Behavior on litP` if you miss it.
- Changed: **`reuseItems: true`, and one row built ahead instead of two.** A tile that scrolls out is handed to the next app scrolling in instead of being destroyed and built again.
- Not changed: what the Apps screen does, the search, the extras (calculator, clipboard, colour, emoji, web), keyboard moves, the reveal, and how the phone itself is drawn (`PhoneBody.qml`).
- If the grid ever shows blank or wrong tiles after typing: delete the `objectProp: "key"` line in `LauncherFace.qml` (the entries are the same objects every time, so the model still tells them apart).
- Files: `AppSource.qml`, `LauncherFace.qml`, `services/launcher-test/rank_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Apps launcher's buttons look like the Quick Panel's

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Checked: `qmllint` finds no syntax errors in `LauncherFace.qml`; the real tile code and the real `Pill.qml` rendered in Qt 6 (headless, stubbed theme) at rest, picked with the arrow keys, hovered and pressed, next to the old tile.

- Changed: **each app tile is now a `Pill`, built like `QuickToggle`'s square key** (the Quick Panel's header keys). At rest it is white with a 3px outline and a small hard shadow (3 right / 1 down). When the pointer is over it, or the arrow keys have it, it lights up: the mint-to-cyan gradient inside a white ring, a 5px outline and a longer shadow (5 / 2), all gliding with one value over 170 ms. Pressing sinks the cap onto its shadow and it springs back, like every Quick Panel button. The icon grows a little when lit.

The name under the tile uses the Quick Panel's bold text style. Grid size, spacing, search, keyboard moves and the reveal animation are unchanged.
- Fixed: `LauncherFace` now says `classicPanel: true` like the Quick Panel, Settings and Wallpapers screens. Pill and MaterialIcon only use the phone's colours inside something that says so, so in the first dark mode the search icon (and the Colour / Emoji cards) used the bar's pale ink on white.

## Unreleased — clipboard: the picture and the text now match the row you picked

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Checked: the real `ClipboardCard.qml` loaded in Qt 6 (headless, stubbed theme) and driven the way `ExtrasSource.qml` drives it, before and after; `qmllint` finds no syntax errors in the edited files; the picture-decode script run for real against a fake `cliphist` (png, jpeg, and webp turned into png).

- Fixed: **the sheet showed the entry one step behind the highlighted row, and the newest entry never loaded.** `onEntriesChanged` / `onCurrentChanged` in `ClipboardCard.qml` read `currentEntry`, but inside a change handler that binding has not been re-evaluated yet, so it still held the previous entry (or `null`). Reproduced: when the list arrived the card reported `null` (newest entry blank; a copied picture never appeared), moving Down to row 8 loaded row 9, and a new copy landing on top made the selection drift to another row. The handlers now read `entries[current]` directly (`entryAt()`), and `activate()` does too.
- Fixed: **a pick made while a decode was still running was dropped.** A Quickshell `Process` stays "running" until it has really exited, so `running = false; running = true` did nothing and the new entry stayed on "Loading" until the 4 s timer said it failed. `ExtrasSource.selectClip` now keeps the latest pick, stops the old decode, and starts the new one from `onRunningChanged` (latest pick wins).
- Changed: a failed decode is reported **when the process ends** without an answer (the 4 s timer is now a 10 s backstop, so a big picture being converted is not called failed), and the card shows what `cliphist` / `magick` printed under "Could not load this picture" (`clipError`).
- Changed: the dock's Clipboard card names a picture like the launcher does (`Image · 1.2 MiB png 1920x1080`) and puts it back on the clipboard with its own type.
- Files: `ClipboardCard.qml`, `ExtrasSource.qml`, `LauncherExtras.qml` (passes `error`), `DockClipboardCard.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — rename a preset

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here), so how the tile looks and feels is untested. Checked: brace balance of the edited QML files; `services/preset-rename-test/rename_unit.js` (new) runs `preset-state.sh rename` for real in a temp folder (file and pictures move together, the name inside changes, a taken name is refused with nothing touched, bad names, capitals-only, a stray pictures folder doesn't come along, the pictures still restore under the new name), runs `rename()` from the service against fakes, and checks the board's wiring; the other preset tests still pass.

- New: **a pencil on every preset tile** (top left of its picture, on hover, next to SAVE OVER). One press turns the name under the picture into a field with the name selected: **Enter** keeps it, **Esc** cancels. A click on the rest of the tile while typing does nothing (it doesn't wear the preset). Leaving the tab drops a half-typed name.
- Refused names turn the field red and leave it open: an **empty** name, or one **another preset already has** (capitals ignored, so `night` is refused when `Night` exists). Renaming never adds " 2" the way saving does, since that would not be the name you typed. The same name, or only its capitals changed (`night` to `Night`), is fine.
- The name is cleaned like a new preset's: no slashes or control characters, no leading dots, one space between words, 40 characters. The tile changes at once and the list is read again when the file is done moving.
- What moves: the preset's file, its pictures folder (`<name>.images/`, the avatar and banner it keeps) and the `_meta.name` saved inside it. Nothing else in the preset is touched, and its saved time stays. The tick on a tile, and a preset being worn right now, follow the new name.
- New: `qs -c sekaipop ipc call presets rename "Night desk" "Late desk"` (prints the name it ended up with, nothing when it was refused).
- `services/preset-state.sh rename <old> <new> <json>`: refuses with exit 3 when `<new>` is another preset (nothing changed), 1 when `<old>` isn't there, 2 for a bad name; a failure half way puts the pictures folder back.
- Files: `EditPresetBoard.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (`rename`, `byName`, IPC), `services/preset-state.sh`, `services/preset-rename-test/rename_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — window borders that follow the theme (Settings -> Desktop -> App colours -> Window borders)

**Not run on a live Hyprland** (none here). Checked: `sh -n services/app-theme.sh`, brace balance of the edited .qml files.

- New: **Window borders**, a fifth switch in App colours (`appTheme.hyprland`, target `hyprland` in `app-theme.sh`): Hyprland's `general.col.active_border` is the theme's accent and `general.col.inactive_border` its muted colour, so they follow the theme, the light / dark mode and the matugen wallpaper colours, like the other app switches (rewritten a moment after a change). Applied live with `hyprctl eval`, and kept as one line tagged `-- pjsekai:apptheme:hyprland` in `~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua` (hyprland.lua has to load it, see the Hyprland page's one-time setup). **Revert** removes that line and reloads Hyprland, which gives back the colours of your own `hyprland.lua`.
- Colours: the active border is the theme's accent pushed to a stronger colour of the same hue, and the inactive one is that hue darker, not the grey mix of text and background an earlier build used (which looked grey with every wallpaper). Sent to the script as `hlactive` / `hlinactive`.
- Not changed: the border thickness (Settings -> Hyprland -> Gaps & borders; `0` means no border at all), so a border has to be on for the colours to show. The ScrollOverview plugin draws windows with their Hyprland borders, so they show there too (not confirmed against the plugin's source).
- Files: `services/app-theme.sh`, `services/AppThemeService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — an option to use the ScrollOverview plugin (niri-style) as the Overview

**Not run on a live Quickshell or Hyprland** (none here). Checked: brace balance of the edited files, `sh -n` on `hyprland-settings.sh`. Needs a real test, see below.

- New: **Settings -> Hyprland -> Overview -> Overview style**: `Cork board` (the Overview this shell always had, still the default) or `Scroll (niri)`, the Hyprland plugin https://github.com/yayuuu/hyprland-scroll-overview (`overview.style` in `SettingsService`, `board` | `scroll`). With `Scroll` picked, `ipc call overview toggle | show | hide` and the dock's Search-key fallback drive the plugin instead (`scrolloverview:overview toggle` / `on` / `off`, or `hl.plugin.scrolloverview.overview("toggle")` on a Lua config), so the key you already bound keeps working and nothing in `hyprland.lua` has to change. The plugin draws the overview itself (the compositor), so its look is the plugin's, not this shell's.
- Auto-load: with `Scroll` picked and the plugin not loaded (at start-up, right after picking it, on **Check again**, or on the first overview key press), the shell runs `hyprpm reload -n` once itself, so no `hl.on("hyprland.start", ...)` line is needed in `hyprland.lua`. The plugin still has to be installed and enabled with hyprpm once. When it still did not load, you are told: a notification ("Scroll overview did not start", once per try, with what `hyprpm` said and whether the cork board opened instead) and the same text on the Settings card.
- Safe fallback: if `Scroll` is picked but the plugin is not loaded (not installed, or hyprpm has not loaded it yet), the cork board opens instead, so the key never does nothing. The check is `hyprctl plugin list`, done at start-up, when the option changes, when the card is built, on **Check again**, and only while the plugin is not known to be loaded, right before an open.
- New: the card shows whether the plugin was found; when it was not, it shows the install line (`hyprpm add ... && hyprpm update && hyprpm enable scrolloverview`) with a **Copy the commands** button. When it is loaded: **Workspaces stack** (vertical / sideways), **Zoom out** (`scale`, 0.1 to 0.9) and **Gap between workspaces**. These are kept in the shell's own settings (`overview.layout` / `.scale` / `.gap`; unset leaves your `hyprland.lua` plugin config alone) and applied live with `hyprctl eval` once the plugin is loaded and after every config reload (`configreloaded`).
- Fixed (same release): an earlier build of this feature wrote those options to `~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua`, which runs before hyprpm has loaded the plugin, so Hyprland showed "unknown config key 'plugin.scrolloverview.workspace_gap'" on every reload. Nothing about the plugin goes in that file any more; if you ran that build, delete the leftover lines: `sed -i '/plugin\.scrolloverview/d' ~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua`.
- New: `ipc call scrolloverview toggle | show | hide | status` (drives the plugin directly) and `ipc call overview style [scroll|board]` (reads or switches the option).
- Changed: presets leave `overview.style` alone (`keepPrefix`), like the other personal switches. Edit Desktop starting also hides the plugin's overview if it is up.
- Not changed: the cork board, its tiles and its gestures; they are used exactly as before when `Cork board` is picked (and as the fallback).
- To test: pick `Scroll (niri)`, press your overview key (plugin loaded: the plugin's overview; not loaded: the cork board). `qs -c sekaipop ipc call scrolloverview status`. Move Zoom out and Gap, then open it. Lua config only: confirm the dispatch string `hl.plugin.scrolloverview.overview("toggle")` takes effect through `Hyprland.dispatch` (the same path `hl.dsp.focus(...)` uses elsewhere); if it does nothing, tell me what `hyprctl dispatch` prints.
- Files: `Overview.qml`, `SettingsPageHyprland.qml`, `services/ScrollOverviewService.qml` (new), `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/HyprlandSettings.qml` (read-only paths for the plugin's live values), `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Apps launcher: no Home, a clean slide instead of the bounce

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Checked: brace, bracket and parenthesis balance of `LauncherFace.qml` and `QuickPanel.qml`, no leftover references to what was removed.

- Removed: **Home.** The Apps screen is the A to Z grid and nothing else: the Home page, its pinned apps, the Home / All apps button at the bottom, the Back chip, and the Settings and Wallpapers tiles are all gone (Settings is the gear on the Quick Panel; the Wallpapers keep their key and `ipc call wallpaperpicker toggle`). The grid uses the room the button had. Escape clears the search, then goes back.
- Changed: **the phone's motion on the Apps screen** (`launching` in `QuickPanel.qml`): a slide, no bounce, softer than the first try. The lift runs 340 ms (`launchInMs`) on a sheet-style Bezier curve (`launchCurve`: gentle start, speeds up, long soft stop) instead of the hard `OutQuart` that made it stiff; the tilt straightens a little behind it (460 ms, `launchTiltMs`) so the phone settles into place instead of riding up as one block. The close (fixed from a screen recording: it waited about 40 ms before it moved, then jumped into a fast, even drop with the tilt on a different timing): it now starts at once, with no redraw wait, and the lift and the tilt go together on one curve (`launchOutCurve`, 300 ms `launchOutMs`) that gathers speed and ends softly. No pull-up. The Quick Panel keeps its bounce and its wait.
- New: **the grid arrives with the phone**: it follows a beat behind the phone (60 ms), rising 30 units, fading and growing from 96% over 320 ms every time the screen comes up (`revealT`). Tile hover and press are a touch smoother. The keyboard is still handed to the search box after 20 ms, and the app icons load asynchronously.
- Tune: `launchInMs` / `launchTiltMs` / `launchOutMs`, `launchCurve` and `launchOutCurve` in `QuickPanel.qml`; `revealAnim` in `LauncherFace.qml`.
- Files: `LauncherFace.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`, `LauncherPhone.qml` (its `onBuiltinActivated` line: the signal is gone, and it stopped the shell loading), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — vertical visualizer bands

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here). Checked: the QML files' brackets balance, and a mock desktop was rendered by turning two of the shaders' CPU-simulated frames on their sides the same way the widget does (`vertical_preview.png`).

Settings > Desktop > **Visualizer direction** (Horizontal / Vertical), a new **rotate button** on the band's corner buttons in Edit Desktop (next to style and size), or `qs -c sekaipop ipc call visualizer vertical true` (also `vertical false`, `toggleVertical`).
- How it looks: the band runs the full height of the screen, as wide as the size you picked (S / M / L / XL), up the **right edge** by default with its base on the edge and the bass at the bottom. Drag it across in Edit Desktop (it moves sideways instead of up and down) and, when you drop it nearer the left half, it turns to face the left edge (base on the left, bass still at the bottom). It does not flip while you are dragging.
