## Unreleased — Concept D, step 1: one phone, one door (Quick + Apps + Settings behind one router)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). What was checked: `qmllint` (Qt 6.12) read every changed QML file and found no syntax errors; the navigation rules in `PhoneRoute.js` have a node unit test (`node services/route-test/route_unit.js`, all pass). Nothing was drawn, no key was pressed, no window was focused. The riskiest part is the keyboard (see "Check" below).

This is the first of three shippable steps of Concept D ("Phone + Stage", from the phone-centred shell brainstorm). Step 2 is the Wallpapers hub with the desktop as the live preview, step 3 is the Overview Stage; until then Wallpapers and Overview are still the corner phone's screens.

- Fixed: the clock, To-Do, user card and visualizer no longer jump a little when Edit Desktop's Done is pressed. Cause (found by switching the small windows off and on): at rest each widget had a small window of its own, Edit Desktop gives it the whole screen, and switching back after the desktop had grown back shifted every widget. They now keep a full-screen window all the time (input still only on the card). Trade-off: the compositor blends a full-screen layer for every frame a widget draws (the visualizer most), which the small windows had saved. `qs -c sekaipop ipc call desktopedit compactwindows true` brings the small windows back until the next reload (with the jump). Not run on a live Quickshell.
- New: **the Apps screen lives inside the Quick Panel's phone.** Same body, same pose, no new posture: the Quick screen slides up and away while `LauncherFace` (search, Wallpapers / Overview / Settings tiles, pinned apps, All apps drawer) slides up into its place (about 200 ms, one progress value, so it can reverse mid-slide).
  - Mouse: a new **Apps button** (grid icon) at the start of the Quick header row. The row now has five buttons, 82 apart starting at x 206 (it was four, 89 apart from x 240); the `user@host` text under the avatar is narrower (131) to stay clear of it. `headerX` / `headerPitch` at the top of that row in `QuickPanelFace.qml` move it.
  - Keyboard: with the Quick screen up, **a plain letter starts a search** (it carries over into the search box, and letters typed while the screen is still sliding in are kept). `SUPER + A` (the old `launcherphone` target) opens straight on Apps.
  - Setting: Settings > General > Quick Panel > **Type to search apps** (`panel.typeToSearch`, on by default). Off = the phone keeps its old keyboard behaviour on Quick.
- New: **one router** (`PhoneRoute.js`): a stack of screens. Back undoes the steps you took and never invents one; at the screen the phone was opened on it closes.
  - **Escape**: puts away an open dialog or the power menu first, then clears the search, then goes back one screen, then closes.
  - **Back chip**: one small outlined "Back" pill at the top right of the Apps screen (same as Escape).
  - **Floor rule**: hot edge / bell / `phone toggle` open on Quick (Escape closes); `SUPER + A` opens on Apps (Escape closes, it does not detour through Quick).
  - **Settings** are on the stack: opened from Apps, their X / Escape turns the phone back to **Apps**, not Quick.
  - **Ctrl + 1..5** (while the phone has the keyboard): 1 Quick (Home: clears the stack), 2 Apps, 3 Wallpapers, 4 Overview, 5 Settings.
- New: **`ipc call phone ...`** is the one entry point: `toggle`, `show`, `hide`, `go quick|apps|wallpapers|overview|settings`, `back`, `home`. The old names keep working: `quickpanel` (Quick), `launcherphone` (now Apps), `settings`, `overviewphone`, `wallpaperphone`. Suggested binds are in `shell.qml`.
- Changed: **the bottom-left hot corner is gone** (`LauncherPhone.qml`: the strip, its dwell and the `launcher.hotCorner` setting and switch are removed). The bottom-centre edge still opens Quick only. `LauncherPhone` no longer has a Home screen of its own to reach.
- Changed: the **Wallpapers and Overview tiles** on the Apps screen bring the corner phone up on that screen (the Quick Panel's phone drops while it rises, as before), and **Back / Home / X there returns to the Apps screen** instead of closing (`LauncherPhone.returnToApps`). Opened by `overviewphone` / `wallpaperphone` / the bar's wallpaper key they still close on Back.
- Changed: while the phone is open on Quick (and `Type to search apps` is on) or on Apps, the window takes the keyboard on demand (`WlrKeyboardFocus.OnDemand`); before, it only did for the Settings and the Wi-Fi password box.
- Changed: `CheatBinds.js` knows the new IPC targets, so binds on them are grouped and named on the cheatsheet.
- Not changed: the Quick screen itself (tiles, sliders, notifications, alert card), the pull-out / drop animation and the Settings turn. A notification alert while the Apps screen is up still buzzes and shows no card (the card belongs to the Quick screen).
- Check, in this order: (1) **keyboard**: open Quick (SUPER+N or the hot edge), type `fire`: Apps should slide in with `fire` already in the box and no letter lost; then Escape (back to Quick), Escape (closes). If typing goes to the window behind instead, tell me what Hyprland does with focus (that is `phoneKeysWanted` / `focusPhoneKeys` in `QuickPanel.qml`); (2) accidentally pulling Quick up with the hot edge while typing in another window: letters now go to the phone, turn **Type to search apps** off if that bites; (3) the Apps button and the header row: five buttons, nothing touching the avatar or the card edge, `user@host` cut with "..." if long; (4) `SUPER+A` (`launcherphone`): opens on Apps, Escape closes; pressed again closes; (5) Apps > Settings tile > X: back on Apps; Apps > Wallpapers (or Overview) tile > Back: the corner phone drops and the Quick Panel's phone comes up on Apps; (6) Ctrl+1..5; (7) the slide at 80% / 125% scale and with the bar on the bottom edge (Apps' bottom button must clear the monitor edge: `visibleBottom`); (8) the pull-out straight onto Apps (`SUPER+A` from nothing): Apps should be there from the first frame, not slide in during the bounce.
- If it needs tuning: the slide is `routeAnim` (200 ms) and `routePrep` (40 ms) in `QuickPanel.qml`; the 34 px travel is in the two faces' `y`.
- Files: `PhoneRoute.js` (new), `services/route-test/route_unit.js` (new), `QuickPanel.qml`, `QuickPanelFace.qml`, `LauncherFace.qml`, `LauncherPhone.qml`, `shell.qml`, `SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `CheatBinds.js`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — end4-pC's audio visualizers, ported

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no Qt): brackets checked in every changed file, nothing was drawn. The four shaders are end4-pC's own compiled `.qsb`, copied as they are.

- New: **the desktop visualizer has a Style** (Settings > Desktop > Visualizer style, `qs -c sekaipop ipc call visualizer style <sekai|bars|aurora|ring|dots|mirror>` / `nextStyle`, or the new style button on the card in Edit Desktop). `sekai` is the keycap card as before (default). The other five are end4-pC's desktop visualizer styles:
  - `bars` (plain QML, one rounded bar per 12 px), `aurora`, `dots`, `mirror` (GPU shaders): a band across the bottom of the screen. Fades in and out with the music, takes no clicks. In Edit Desktop the band can only be dragged up and down (`bandY`); S / M / L / XL is its height (180 / 240 / 300 / 380).
  - `ring` (shader): round, movable like the card, with the cover in the middle. S / M / L / XL is its diameter (260 / 340 / 420 / 520).
  - Fed by cava with end4's own config (`services/cava-pc.conf`: 50 bands, waves mode), through `ShaderVisualizerEngine.qml` (port of end4's `VisualizerEngine.qml`: fast attack / slow release, falling peaks, beat pulse) and `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml` (port of `VisualizerShader.qml`). They do not use the note analyzer.
  - Colors follow the existing "Visualizer colors" setting: Cover (the track's most saturated colors, as end4's cover colors; `VisualizerCover.qml` downloads the art into `~/.cache/pjsekai/cover-art/`), Sekai (the five card attributes), Theme (the shell's two accents).
  - Added beyond end4's: a demo song plays in Edit Desktop so the style (and its picture on the shelf) shows something; sensitivity, perf level (frame cap) and show / hide / IPC apply to these styles too.
- New: **Tide**, a visualizer of our own (style `tide`; `TideVisualizer.qml`, plain QML Shapes, nothing to compile). Three sheets of cut paper rise from the bottom of the screen with the music, in flat colour with the shell's navy outline and a hard flat shadow on the sheet behind. The spectrum is folded round the middle (bass in the centre, highs at the edges); the back sheet is slow, the front one quick. Nine paper pins (a diamond on a stem) hold each lane's falling peak, and a beat lifts the whole tide. A slow sway keeps a quiet passage moving. Every sheet is one smooth spline curve (an SVG path, not a polygon; 80 points, each sheet blurred 3-4 times over with a [1 4 6 4 1] kernel and eased in time, the beat's lift eased too), swells roll along it and a pale foam line follows each crest; the pins ride just above the front crest on short stems. Same size choices as the bands (180 / 240 / 300 / 380), same colors setting, and Lite drops the shadows and the multisampling.
- New: **Media pill visualizer** (Settings > Desktop, `ipc call visualizer pillStyle <none|dots|wave>`, off by default): end4-pC's bar visualizer (`PillBars.qml`) or `WaveVisualizer` (`PillWave.qml`, a blurred filled wave) inside the bar's media pill. The pill grows to make room. Its own cava tap is `services/PcCavaService.qml`, and runs only while a player plays and this is on. The wave fill is 35% (end4's is 15%) so it shows on the pill's gradient.
- Fixed: the visualizer is always behind everything but the wallpaper: the other desktop widgets (clock, to-do, user card), the dock, the bar and every window. It used to share the Bottom layer with the widgets and the dock (so who was in front was left to the compositor) and went up to the Top layer while selected or dragged in Edit Desktop. It is now on the Background layer, the wallpaper's own, and is mapped after the wallpaper (`VisualizerWidget.qml`: `WlrLayershell.layer: WlrLayer.Background`). If the wallpaper window is ever recreated while the visualizer is on (a monitor plugged in), the wallpaper may cover it until the visualizer is toggled off and on.
- Not ported: end4's `Player.qml` backdrop wave, since the media panel is the turntable now.
- Check: (1) each style plays with a local file and with Spotify / YouTube, with `cava` installed; (2) `ring` with and without cover art, and the colors in Cover mode; (3) the band: bottom edge at 80% / 150% scale, dragging it in Edit Desktop, a second monitor; (4) switching styles while music plays (the Sekai card should pop back in); (5) the pill's dots and wave next to a long title.
- Files: `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml`, `services/EditMode.qml`, `shell.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `MediaPill.qml`, `Status_Bar.qml`, `Bar.qml`; new: `ShaderVisualizerEngine.qml`, `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`, `ShaderBarsVisualizer.qml`, `VisualizerCover.qml`, `PillBars.qml`, `PillWave.qml`, `services/PcCavaService.qml`, `services/cava-pc.conf`, `shaders/visualizer/{aurora,dots,mirror,ring}.frag.qsb`.

## Unreleased — Weather, Calendar, Battery and Discord popups redesigned

The four popups share one design language now instead of four takes on it, and each has a new layout, not just new paint.

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). They were rendered offscreen in Qt with the Quickshell parts stubbed (`Theme`, the real popup files, your palette sampled from your screenshots, Figtree standing in for Google Sans Flex, so text widths are close, not exact), in Dark and Light, and every file passes `qmllint`'s syntax check. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is off.

- **New parts.** `SekaiPlate.qml`: the header plate every popup opens with (gradient on the Pill, a halftone corner, two slanted bands of stage light; what is declared inside is not clipped, so stickers break out over the edge). It is the Discord panel's blurple plate, made reusable. `SectionTab.qml`: a slanted label tab with a diamond that names each block ("FORECAST", "LIFE GAUGE", "POWER MODE", "IN THE ROOM").
- **Weather.** A sky poster: the temperature as a white sticker numeral with today's high and low beside it, the weather as a tilted dark disc you can poke, the place on a tab hanging off the plate's lower edge, the refresh key beside it. Three graphic gauges (humidity tank, wind dial whose arrow turns, UV diamond meter) over one slim strip for feels like / pressure / visibility. The forecast is a song list: a row per day with a range bar on a shared temperature scale and the chance of rain filling the row from the left (`Sat 10` dates; `WeatherSource` carries `date`). Hourly is the same melody on an inset lane.
- **Calendar.** A poster plate (tilted date sticker that breaks out of it, the month as a white sticker, the year chip), a weekday strip with a slanted gradient key on today's column, two soft weekend bands, a warm ribbon under today's week, and the month lane on its own track with the page-turn keys at the foot. Keys are 40 px so the card is as wide as the others. Weekends use a lighter coral-pink on the dark looks (`weekendInk`).
- **Battery.** The plate wears the colour of the judgement (green GOOD, red MISS, the accent gradient for PERFECT) with the number and a tilted stamp. The gauge sits on a track with a 0 / 50 / 100 scale; a part-full segment is a narrower slant filling from the left at full colour. The time is the same icon key + two lines when there is no pace to show. Power mode is one connected switch with a thumb that slides to the chosen mode.
- **Discord.** The plate is a `SekaiPlate` (so it matches the rest), a section tab names the list, each member's colour tab is a `//` pair, and whoever is talking gets live rhythm-game bars instead of a static icon.
- **Readability.** Big sticker numerals on the plates (the temperature, the month, the battery percentage) are a dark fill inside the white ring (`SekaiPlate.numberFill` / `numberRing`); a white fill merged into the white ring and the digits lost their shapes on the pale plates. The weather icons (the disc, the hourly notes, the day rows) are drawn by the new `WeatherGlyph.qml` as vector OUTLINES, stroked with no fill and not from the icon font, so they are sharp at any size with no pixel grain; Discord's live dot is a plain circle. The font's filled glyphs came out grainy on screen (its outline ones were always clean), and an earlier `nativeRender` experiment did not fix that, so it is gone and `MaterialIcon.qml` is back to the original. Every remaining font icon in the four popups sets `fill: 0` explicitly, so none can turn filled whatever `Theme.iconFill` is. "HUMIDITY" fits its tile, the wind dial clears the unit (tiles are 82 / 132 / 74 wide), the calendar's weekday names and month-lane diamonds are brighter.
- **Light mode.** Accent-on-chip ink (the stamp word, the date number, the disc icon, the strip icons, the section-tab diamond) switches to a darker ink on Light.
- **Check.** (1) The weather icons' stroke weight in the disc and the rows (`WeatherGlyph.stroke`). (2) A long place name and a 3-digit temperature on the Weather plate. (3) The Calendar in a month whose name is long ("September"). (4) Battery at 100 %, at 3 %, and while scrubbing. (5) Discord with 1 and 6+ people. (6) Light and Darker modes.
- **Where to tune.** `SekaiPlate` (the shared plate), `SectionTab`, `Water` and the tile sizes at the top of the gauge row in `WeatherPopup.qml`, `weekendInk` in `CalendarPopup.qml`.
- **Files.** New: `SekaiPlate.qml`, `SectionTab.qml`, `WeatherGlyph.qml`. Changed: `WeatherPopup.qml`, `WeatherMath.js`, `WeatherSource.qml`, `CalendarPopup.qml`, `BatteryPopup.qml`, `DiscordPopup.qml`, `DiscordMemberRow.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the launcher, Overview and Wallpapers phone moves to the bottom-left corner (hover)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no Qt): brackets checked in every changed file, and the window / pivot numbers were worked out with `PhoneMath.js` and `FlipMath.js` in node for 1920x1080 (scale 1 and 1.25), 2560x1440 and 1366x768. Nothing was drawn. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is off, most likely in the turn into the Wallpapers and in where the phone rests.

- Changed: **the launcher phone is now a hover phone in the bottom-left corner, built the way the Quick Panel's phone is** (`LauncherPhone.qml` rewritten around the Quick Panel's window and animation; nothing opens in the middle of the monitor any more).
  - **Hover**: a hot corner (a 360 x 4 strip at the bottom-left of every screen, not with the bar on the bottom edge) pulls the phone up after the same dwell (`panel.hotDwell`, Settings > General > Quick Panel > Edge delay). Same "fresh arrival" rule (the mouse has to leave the strip before it can fire again) and the same 1.2 s block after it closes. Moving the mouse away does not close it; click outside, press the keybind again, or pick an app.
  - **Same body and movement as the Quick Panel**: same phone size and pose, same pull-out (rise past rest, drop back, small hop), same pocket tilt (roll 12 / pitch 10 / yaw -6) that rings out as it lands, same pull-up-then-drop on the way out. It rests with the same part showing as the Quick Panel (`panel.peek`, "How much of the phone shows").
  - **Home**: the bottom "All apps / Home" button and the pages are laid out above the monitor's edge instead of under it (`LauncherFace.visibleBottom`, worked out from the resting pose the way the Quick Panel sizes its notification list). With the whole phone showing (peek 100) it is the old layout, to the pixel.
  - **Overview and Wallpapers** are still screens of this same phone (Overview eases upright and flat, Wallpapers rolls a quarter turn into the landscape one), but now in the corner: for them the phone rises the rest of the way (`riseK`) so the whole screen is on the monitor, and lowers to its peeking spot when you go back to Home. The Wallpapers phone is the Quick Panel's size (never wider than the monitor).
  - **The window is only as big as the corner needs** (not the whole monitor): sized once from the peek, the full rise and a walk through the Wallpapers turn, like the Quick Panel's `flipRoom`. At 1920x1080 that is about 1290 x 1070 px, mostly empty and see-through; only the phone takes the mouse.
- New: `ipc call wallpaperphone toggle|show|hide` opens the phone straight on the Wallpapers (`launcherphone` and `overviewphone` work as before).
- Changed: **the bar's wallpaper key and the Quick Panel's two wallpaper actions (desktop and lockscreen) open the Wallpapers screen of the corner phone** instead of the old board in the middle (`shell.qml`: `wallpaperEntry` stands in for `picker`). The lockscreen action opens the gallery on the Lockscreen side (`WallpaperStage.initialTarget` / `setTarget`).
- Changed: only one of the two bottom phones is up at a time: opening the corner phone closes the Quick Panel (and its Settings), and the other way round (`shell.qml` / `LauncherPhone.qml`).
- New: Settings > General > **Launcher phone > Hot corner** switch (`launcher.hotCorner`, on by default).
- Not changed: the old Overview + App Launcher board and the old wallpaper picker are still in the shell and still answer `ipc call overview|applauncher|wallpaperpicker`, so existing keybinds keep working. Repoint them to `launcherphone`, `overviewphone` and `wallpaperphone` to use the corner phone. The old board's calculator / clipboard / emoji / shell / web modes are not in the phone's launcher (they were not before either).
- Check: (1) the hot corner: dwell, the pull-out and its landing wobble next to the Quick Panel's; (2) Home at the resting peek: the "All apps" button fully above the monitor's edge and the grid scrolls; (3) Wallpapers and Overview: the rise and the turn together (the left corner of the phone may touch the screen's left edge for an instant mid-turn), then the sharpness once landed (`faceDirect` is the same code as before, now fed the corner position); (4) bar wallpaper key, the Quick Panel's wallpaper actions and the lockscreen side; (5) 80% / 125% / 150% scale, bar on every edge, a second monitor; (6) the corner phone and Quick Panel taking over from each other.
- If it needs tuning: `cornerGap` (distance from the screen's left edge), `hotWidth` / `hotHeight` and `bounce` / `tilt*` are properties at the top of `LauncherPhone.qml`, with the Quick Panel's values.
- Files: `LauncherPhone.qml`, `LauncherFace.qml`, `WallpaperStage.qml`, `shell.qml`, `SettingsPageGeneral.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Wallpapers phone drawn sharp

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here): braces and brackets checked in the changed files, nothing was drawn.

- Changed: **the landed Wallpapers phone is drawn straight to the pixels**, the same way as the Overview phone (see the entry below): `WallpaperStage.qml` turns on `PhoneBody.faceDirect` once the phone has landed and is still, so its screen is a whole number of texels per device pixel and its top-left sits on a whole device pixel, with no big picture to shrink afterwards. (It was flat already, but the body's 4x4 still scaled the screen by about 1.0114, so the same off-grid double resample applied.) Off while it is turning, popping in or out, or still overshooting its entrance (`pop === 1`).
- Changed: **wallpaper thumbnails and the viewer picture are decoded as dense as the screen they land in** (`WallpaperFace.qml`): the cells' `sourceSize.width` was a fixed 520 and the viewer's a fixed 1680 x 1050; both now follow `renderScale` x 2, as the Quick Panel's banner and avatar already do (the cached thumbnail files themselves are 720 wide, so a thumbnail cannot get sharper than that).
- Check: (1) the landed phone's text and thumbnails against the Quick Panel; (2) the moment it switches to flat after it lands (nothing should move or flash; `faceDirect: root.direct` in `WallpaperStage.qml` can be set to `false` to go back); (3) 80% / 150% scale.
- Files: `WallpaperStage.qml`, `WallpaperFace.qml`, `PhoneBody.qml` (the landscape wall also hidden while direct), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Overview phone drawn sharp

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, no Qt, nothing was drawn or captured): braces and brackets were checked in the four changed files, and the flat-pose numbers below were worked out with `PhoneMath.js`. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is still soft.

Why it was blurry (found by reading, not by seeing it): the Overview phone is the Quick Panel's tilted body eased upright, and an upright phone drawn that way is NOT the same as a phone that was flat from the start (the Wallpapers phone). With every angle at 0 the body's 4x4 still scales the front by 1.0137 (the perspective, because the body is centred in its own thickness) and puts it at an odd, fractional spot (57.147, -19.185 design units for the resting pose). So the screen's texels never lined up with the big picture's texels, and that picture was then shrunk to the monitor from yet another grid: two off-grid resamples, drifting in and out of phase across the phone (sharp bands, soft bands). On top of that, the window pictures were a plain, un-filtered shrink of a whole window, and the app icons were decoded at their design size.

- Changed: **the flat Overview phone is drawn straight to the pixels** (`PhoneBody.faceDirect`, switched on by `LauncherPhone.qml`'s `win.flatNow` once the phone has landed and is still).
  - The screen is drawn into a texture that is an exact whole number (`directK`, normally 2) of texels per device pixel, `directW` x `directH` device pixels on the monitor, with a plain scale and move instead of the 4x4. No big picture (`scene`) and no side wall layer is built while it is flat, so it also uses less graphics memory.
  - The SCREEN's top-left (not the big picture's, as before) is put on a whole device pixel (`snapX` / `snapY` in `LauncherPhone.qml`). One device pixel is then exactly `directK` x `directK` texels and the only shrink left is a clean box filter, the way the Quick Panel's text is.
  - Same size and place as before to within half a device pixel, so nothing jumps when it switches over. While the phone is turning in or out, or is any other phone (Quick Panel, Settings, Wallpapers, launcher Home), nothing changes: `faceDirect` is off and every old code path is the one used.
- Changed: **window pictures are filtered down properly** (`OverviewPhoneTile.qml`). Each `ScreencopyView` is drawn into a texture twice as dense as the screen it lands in, with a mip chain (`layer.mipmap`), so a 1920 px window shrunk to a thumbnail keeps its text and edges instead of coming out smeared. Cost: one extra texture per tile, redrawn when the window delivers a frame. `sharpPreviews: false` in `OverviewFace.qml` turns it off.
- Changed: **app icons are decoded at the density they are shown at** (`texDensity`, at least 2 texels per design unit; they were decoded at 1 per design unit and then stretched), with `mipmap`. The Quick Panel decodes its icons at 3x for the same reason.
- Check: (1) the Overview phone once it has landed, text in the strips ("Workspace 1", "NOW", the clock) next to the Quick Panel's; (2) the moment it switches to flat right after it lands (nothing should move or flash; if it does, set `faceDirect: false` in `LauncherPhone.qml` to get the old drawing back, the other two changes stay); (3) window pictures with many live windows or a playing video (set `sharpPreviews: false` if it is heavy); (4) 80% / 150% scale and a second monitor.
- Files: `PhoneBody.qml`, `LauncherPhone.qml`, `OverviewPhoneTile.qml`, `OverviewFace.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Overview phone (part 3 of the phone-as-home-screen plan)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here): `qmllint` (Qt 6.12) read the three changed / new files and found no syntax errors, nothing was drawn, no window was captured. Expect a first-run fix or two, most likely in the drag and the entrance.

- New: the **Overview phone** (`OverviewFace.qml`, `OverviewPhoneTile.qml`): the Overview as a flat, 2D, portrait phone, the way niri's overview works. The launcher phone's **Overview** tile now turns THIS phone, in place, into it (the tipped phone eases upright and flat while its screen dissolves into the Overview's); its **Home** button turns it back.
  - **Stacked workspaces**: workspaces 1-5 are strips in one column, each a to-scale picture of the monitor (its shape comes from `hyprctl monitors`, a tall monitor is shrunk so a strip is never more than about 60% of the column). The column scrolls; the picked strip sits in the middle, the ones next to it peek in above and below and fade out at the edges. A **NOW** badge marks the workspace you are on, a cyan ring the picked one, and a column of dots at the right shows where you are (click one to jump).
  - **Live pictures with icon and title, as the old one**: every window is a live `ScreencopyView` at its real place on its strip, its app icon in the corner, its title on hover, the focused window outlined in cyan. Pictures are only captured for strips near the middle of the column.
  - **Mouse**: wheel = next / previous workspace; click a strip = switch to it; click a window = focus it; middle click or the X that shows on hover = close it; drag a window onto another strip = move it there (hold it near the top or bottom edge and the column scrolls by itself); drag a FLOATING window on its own strip = move it there.
  - **Keys**: Up / Down (K / J) pick a workspace, 1-5 jump, Left / Right (H / L, Tab) pick a window on it left to right, Enter focuses it (or switches to the workspace when no window is picked), Delete or X closes the picked window, Escape drops the window pick and then steps back.
  - It only uses the dispatches the old Overview uses (`focuswindow`, `closewindow`, `workspace`, `movetoworkspacesilent`, `movewindowpixel`, and the `hl.dsp.*` forms when Hyprland is on Lua), through the same `HyprlandData`. Nothing niri-specific is used: this is niri's look and movement on Hyprland. Workspaces are the fixed 1-5 the old Overview shows.
  - Open it straight away with `qs -c sekaipop ipc call overviewphone toggle` (also `show` / `hide`), e.g. `bind = SUPER, TAB, exec, ...`. Opened that way it comes up already flat and Escape closes the phone; opened from Home, Escape goes back to Home.
- Changed: `LauncherPhone.qml` owns the turn (`ov`, `overviewOpen`, `openOverview()` / `leaveOverview()` / `showOverview()`), hands the keyboard to whichever screen is up (`giveKeys()`, one place for what three timers each did), and shrinks its click area to the face while the phone is flat. The Overview screen is a `Loader`, built only while the Overview is up or turning, so nothing is captured and hyprctl is not asked anything the rest of the time.
- Not changed: the old Overview + App Launcher board (`Overview.qml`, its cork board, its keybinds) is untouched; the launcher's Overview tile no longer opens it (`overviewBoard` is no longer used by the launcher phone, as `wallpaperBoard` is not).
- Check: (1) the turn upright, then that the picture is as sharp as the Wallpapers phone once it has landed; (2) live pictures inside the phone's face layer (the layer is redrawn on every frame a window delivers; if that is heavy set `live: false` in `OverviewPhoneTile.qml`'s `ScreencopyView`); (3) dragging a window to a strip that is off screen (edge scroll speed is `0.05` strips per 16 ms in `OverviewFace.qml`'s `edgeTimer`); (4) a second monitor's windows (faded, as in the old Overview).
- Files: `OverviewFace.qml` (new), `OverviewPhoneTile.qml` (new), `LauncherPhone.qml`, `shell.qml` (comment only), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — launcher phone (part 1 of the phone-as-home-screen plan)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here, and no QML linter): braces and brackets checked in the three changed files, nothing was drawn. Expect a first-run fix or two.

- New: the **launcher phone** (`LauncherPhone.qml`, `LauncherFace.qml`): the home screen as a phone. Portrait 2.5D, the same body as the Quick Panel's, but standing in the middle of the monitor and fully on screen. The Quick Panel is a separate phone and is untouched.

The old Overview + App Launcher board and the wallpaper picker are unchanged and keep their keybinds. Next in the plan: Wallpapers as a flat 2D landscape gallery app (the phone flips in place), then a niri-like flat 2D portrait Overview.

## Unreleased — fish follows the theme (App colours > fish)

**Not run with a real fish or a live Quickshell** (neither here): `app-theme.sh apply fish` / `status` / `revert` were run in a throwaway home folder with a stand-in `fish` that logs the commands it is given, on a copy of the `config.fish` from `fish.zip`; revert gives that file back byte for byte. The fish commands themselves were not run.

- New: **App colours > fish** (a switch and a Revert, like kitty and foot). Two layers, both live:
  1. fish's own colours (`fish_color_*`, `fish_pager_color_*`) are set as universal variables with `set -U`, so every running fish picks them up without restarting (the prompt repaints at the next one).
  2. the terminal palette (colours 0-15, text, background, cursor, selection) is written as escape sequences to `~/.local/state/pjsekai/terminal/sequences.txt` and pushed into every open terminal (`/dev/pts/N`, same idea as pC's `applycolor.sh`). starship, eza and anything else that uses the named colours follow it.
- New: one line is added at the END of `config.fish` (managed block, removed by Revert) that prints that file in new interactive shells. Being last, it also wins over the `cat ~/.local/state/quickshell/user/generated/terminal/sequences.txt` block that came with end4's config, so old pC colours no longer override ours. `config.fish` is otherwise not touched.
- Revert: removes that block and the file, erases the `fish_color_*` universal variables (fish goes back to its default colours) and sends a palette reset to open terminals so they return to their own colours.
- Not covered: starship colours written as hex in `starship.toml` (named colours follow, hex ones do not).
- Files: `services/app-theme.sh`, `services/AppThemeService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Dolphin and other KDE apps recolour while open

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no KDE here): `system-theme.sh apply` was run with a stand-in matugen, `plasma-apply-colorscheme` and `dbus-send` that log what they are asked, for three cases (plasma present twice in a row, live update off, plasma missing). Found because an open Dolphin kept its old colours until restarted, while end4's pC recolours it at once.

- Fixed (suspected cause): `kde_publish` wrote the new colours and `ColorScheme=` into `kdeglobals` BEFORE `plasma-apply-colorscheme` ran. By then there was nothing left for it to change, so KDE had no change to announce and open apps were never told. Now `kde_publish` only writes the next `PjsekaiA` / `PjsekaiB` file, and `plasma-apply-colorscheme` (the way pC does it) is the one that switches `kdeglobals`; the test shows `kdeglobals` still holds the previous scheme when it is called.
- New: after a successful `plasma-apply-colorscheme` the shell also sends the palette-changed signal (`org.kde.KGlobalSettings.notifyChange 0 0` through `dbus-send`, the same call pC's `system-appearance.sh` uses).
- Kept as the fallback: no `plasma-apply-colorscheme` (or it fails) or live update switched off: `kdeglobals` is written by the script itself (`kde_commit_manual`) and the D-Bus signals are sent by hand, as before.
- New: the log line `system-theme: kde: PjsekaiX applied with plasma-apply-colorscheme` (or the failure text) after every apply, in `qs log -c pjsekai`.
- Needs on your side: `plasma-apply-colorscheme` (part of plasma-workspace, usually) and `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=kde` with plasma-integration. Settings > Desktop > System colours > Check setup says which one is missing.
- Files: `services/system-theme.sh`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — System colours: switches in Settings, live update on by default

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt, KDE, GTK apps or matugen here): `system-theme.sh` was run end to end with a stand-in matugen in a throwaway home folder (apply, KDE A/B slot, GTK import line, qt5ct/qt6ct files, revert, the new `hints`), the QML was only bracket-checked. How end4's pC does the same (matugen templates for GTK, `kde-material-you-colors` + `plasma-apply-colorscheme` for Qt/KDE) is what `system-theme.sh` already follows; this entry makes it controllable and live by default.

- New: Settings > Desktop > **System colours**: one switch for the whole thing, separate switches for GTK, Qt and KDE apps, **Update open apps live**, **Live for dark GTK4 apps too**, **Apply now** and **Check setup**. They are saved in `settings.json` (`systemTheme.*`) and `ThemeService` follows them (`systemSync`, `systemTargets`, `systemReload`, `systemBounceDark` are now read-only bindings; the IPC calls `system`, `systemOn`, `systemOff` set the switch instead).
- Changed: **live update for dark GTK4 / libadwaita apps is now on by default** (it used to be off, so open GNOME apps only recoloured after a restart). The cost is a short light/dark flash in those apps on each change; turn **Live for dark GTK4 apps too** off to avoid it.
- New: `system-theme.sh hints` and **Check setup**: lists what stops apps following (matugen missing, `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME` not `kde` so Dolphin ignores the scheme, adw-gtk3 missing, dconf/gsettings missing, `plasma-apply-colorscheme` missing, KDE apps with their own `ColorScheme=`). The tile shows the first problem, `qs log -c pjsekai` the full text.
- Changed: nothing is applied before `settings.json` is loaded, so a person who switched it off is not recoloured once at start-up.
- Known: switching one of GTK / Qt / KDE off stops updating it but leaves the files already written; switch the main switch off and on to take everything off.
- Not changed: the colours themselves (still pjsekai's own templates, not `kde-material-you-colors`).
- Files: `services/ThemeService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/system-theme.sh`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Clock, user card and to-do are no longer full-screen windows

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): brackets checked in every changed file, nothing was drawn. Found from `hyprctl layers` on a 1920x1080 monitor, which listed `pjsekai-clock`, `pjsekai-usercard` and `pjsekai-todo` as three permanently mapped, transparent, full-screen layers (plus the wallpaper, mpvpaper, the dock and one more full-screen layer). Freezing mpvpaper made no real difference to Hyprland's 25-30% on the iGPU, and with the shell stopped it fell to 0%, so the cost is the shell's surfaces, not the video.

- Changed: at rest each of the three windows is now only the card plus some room around it (pop-in overshoot, shadow, name tag), the same way the visualizer already worked. A Qt window reports its whole surface as changed on every frame, so a full-screen window made Hyprland redraw (and blend, and blur if blur is on for it) the entire screen each time the card drew anything. Edit Desktop, and dragging on an unlocked desktop, still get the full screen; everything inside is still in screen coordinates (`shifter`), so cards are drawn and dragged where they always were.
- Fixed: a widget that is switched off kept a full-screen window mapped. The windows are now unmapped when the widget is off (after its swallow animation) and mapped again in Edit Desktop, like the visualizer.
- Changed: room for placement and dragging is taken from the screen size (`fullW` / `fullH`), not from the window's own size, because the window is no longer the screen.
- Check: card position after a restart, dragging in Edit Desktop and then leaving it, the name-tag button on the clock, and the pop-in when a widget is switched on. If a card is cut off at its edge, `boxPad` in that widget's file is the margin to raise.
- Not changed, next in line: `pjsekai-dock` (1920x1066) and one more full-screen layer on the top level, and the wallpaper plus mpvpaper surfaces, which are both full-screen by nature.
- Files: `ClockWidget.qml`, `UserCardWidget.qml`, `TodoWidget.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — mpvpaper leftovers are cleaned up

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in, no mpvpaper here): brackets checked, nothing was run. Found from an nvtop screenshot showing three `mpvpaper` processes (about 284 MiB of VRAM and 270-330 MiB of RAM each) running at once.

- Fixed (suspected cause of the extra processes): a stopped mpvpaper did not always exit, and the next start added another one beside it. Now every start first kills any mpvpaper still holding this output's control socket (`pgrep -x mpvpaper`, matched on `input-ipc-server=<socket>`), and a stop is followed 1.5 s later by the same clean-up if the process is still there. The Edit Desktop stop also asks mpv to `quit` through its socket, because a paused mpvpaper may be slow to act on SIGTERM.
- Changed: the Edit Desktop frozen frame is decoded at the wallpaper's size, not the video's.
- Files: `MpvpaperWallpaper.qml`, `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Edit Desktop: the live wallpaper freezes on its last frame and carries on afterwards

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in, no mpvpaper here): brackets checked in every changed file, nothing was drawn or played. The mpv commands used (`set_property pause`, `get_property time-pos`, `screenshot-to-file <file> video`, `request_id`) and the `start` / `screenshot-format` options were checked against mpv's manual from memory, not run.

- Fixed: in Edit Desktop a video wallpaper kept playing full screen while the desktop shrank into its card. With mpvpaper (the default) the video is drawn on its own full-screen surface above the shell's wallpaper layer, so it covered the card and could not shrink.
- Now: when Edit Desktop opens, mpvpaper is paused, its current frame is saved through mpv's control socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/pjsekai-mpvpaper-<o>-frame.jpg`) together with the playback position, and then the process is stopped. The wallpaper card shows that frame and shrinks like a static wallpaper. When the card is back at full size mpvpaper starts again at the saved position (**Pause**) or from the start (**Restart**), per *When it pauses*; the frame stays up for 0.9 s so there is no flash before mpvpaper's surface appears. Only the monitor being edited is affected.
- Fallback: if the socket is not connected or the frame can't be saved within 1 s (`qs log -c pjsekai` says so), the card shows the video's first frame instead, as a static wallpaper would.
- Built-in player (mpvpaper off, or fell back): pauses on entering Edit Desktop and shrinks with the card on the frame it was showing; resumes on exit.
- Changing the wallpaper while editing drops the saved frame.
- Files: `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `MpvpaperWallpaper.qml`, `VideoWallpaper.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Pause or restart the live wallpaper

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in, no mpvpaper here): brackets checked in every changed file, nothing was drawn or played. `Socket` (`path`, `connected`, `write`, `flush`) was checked against the Quickshell docs and `input-ipc-server` against mpvpaper's man page.

- New: **Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> When it pauses** (`wallpaper.videoResume`): **Pause** (default) or **Restart**. Pause continues where the video stopped. Restart starts it over from the beginning. It applies to every reason the video pauses (lock screen, fullscreen window, any window), for mpvpaper and the built-in player.
- How: with mpvpaper, Pause keeps the process running and sets mpv's `pause` property over its control socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/pjsekai-mpvpaper-<output>.sock`, no extra package such as socat), so it resumes instantly and in place; Restart stops the process and starts it again, as before. If the socket never appears (10 tries, 5 s), the video falls back to stop and restart and `qs log -c pjsekai` says so. With the built-in player, Restart rewinds to 0 before playing.
- Changed: mpvpaper is started with `pause=yes` when it is wanted while already paused, so a video that starts under a fullscreen window does not play for a moment first.
- Files: `MpvpaperWallpaper.qml`, `VideoWallpaper.qml`, `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Choose when the live wallpaper pauses

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): brackets checked in every changed file, nothing was drawn.

- New: **Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> Pause video when** (`wallpaper.videoPause`): **Never**, **Fullscreen** (default, as before) or **Any window**. With Any window the video stops as soon as the monitor's active workspace has a window (floating ones count) and plays again when you switch to an empty workspace or close the last window. Works for mpvpaper and the built-in player; the lock screen always pauses it. Each monitor follows its own active workspace.
- Changed: the pause check also refreshes on `movewindow` / `movewindowv2`, so moving the last window off a workspace resumes the video.
- Files: `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Video wallpapers through mpvpaper

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in, no `qmllint`, and no mpvpaper here): brackets checked in every changed file, nothing was drawn or played. mpvpaper's options were checked against its man page (`-o` forwards mpv options, the output name comes first, background layer by default).

- New: video wallpapers play through **mpvpaper** (Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> *Use mpvpaper for video*, `wallpaper.mpvpaper`, on by default). mpvpaper draws the video on its own background surface with mpv's hardware decoding, so the shell itself does no video work. `MpvpaperWallpaper.qml` only owns the process, one per output: `mpvpaper -o "no-audio loop=inf panscan=1.0 hwdec=auto-safe terminal=no" <output> <file>`. Edit `mpvOptions` there to change the mpv options.
- The shell's own wallpaper layer keeps drawing the video's first frame underneath, so the transitions, the lock screen, the theme colours and the gap while mpvpaper starts are unchanged. mpvpaper is started after that layer exists, so it is stacked above it.
- Pausing: while the lock screen is up, while a fullscreen window is on the monitor's workspace (previous change) and during a wallpaper transition, mpvpaper is stopped and the still frame shows; it starts again from the beginning afterwards.
- Fallback: if mpvpaper dies within 3 s of starting (not installed, wrong output name) the built-in QtMultimedia player is used until the shell restarts, and the reason is in `qs log -c pjsekai`. Turn the setting off to use the built-in player always.
- Needs the `mpvpaper` package. Not carried over to the mpvpaper path: parallax (the video does not drift with the workspace).
- Not checked: that mpvpaper's surface lets clicks through to the desktop's right-click menu. If right click on the wallpaper stops working while a video plays, tell me.
- Files: `MpvpaperWallpaper.qml` (new), `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `VideoWallpaper.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Idle GPU audit: the video wallpaper pauses when nobody can see it

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in, no `qmllint` here either): brackets checked in the changed file, nothing was drawn, and there are no GPU measurements. This was a static audit of the whole tree for anything that keeps the GPU busy while the desktop is idle.

- Fixed: a video wallpaper decoded and drew every frame forever. `VideoWallpaper.qml` has a `paused` switch, but nothing ever set it, so the video kept playing under the lock screen and under fullscreen windows. `WallpaperLayer.qml` now binds it (`videoPaused`): paused while `LockState.phase` is not `idle`, and while the monitor's active workspace has a fullscreen window (`lastIpcObject.hasfullscreen`, refreshed on Hyprland's fullscreen / workspace / focus / open / close events, only while a video is actually playing). Playback resumes by itself. A still wallpaper is unaffected.
- Checked, nothing to change: every infinite animation (14) is gated by a state (busy, live, attention, open); every repeating `Timer` that is not state-gated is a slow poll (2.5 s weather/resource, 4 s status icons, 15 min weather) that only redraws a bar pill when its value changes; every `FrameAnimation` is gated (visualizer, drag-scroll, perf probe); the wallpaper `ShaderEffect` is only visible during a transition and its layers are only enabled then; `Polkit` windows only exist while a prompt is active; `ScreencopyView` is only used in Overview and dock previews.
- Left alone, worth knowing: the clock, to-do and user-card widgets each own a full-screen transparent layer window that stays mapped (input is masked to the card). The clock redraws once a second (its beat diamonds and seconds), and each redraw makes Hyprland re-composite the screen. Shrinking those windows to the card's box, as was done for the visualizer, is the next real saving, but it touches placement, dragging and Edit Desktop, so it is not done blind.
- Files: `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Visualizer: nothing runs while it is off, and a Lite mode for weak laptops

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): brackets checked in every changed file; the analyzer was profiled on the synthetic test song (about 0.4 ms of Python per 16.7 ms hop on a fast machine, so it is not the main cost) and left alone. Nothing here was drawn, so the GPU numbers are expectations, not measurements.

- Fixed: the visualizer kept the GPU busy while it was switched off. Its full-screen window stayed mapped, and the seek bar inside the (scaled to nothing) card kept running: a timer polled the player every 450 ms and each reading started a 440 ms glide of the playhead and the progress bar, so while any player played there was an animation going all the time and a full-screen frame was drawn and blended on every vsync. The window is now only mapped while the widget is shown, fading in or out, or Edit Desktop is open (`VisualizerWidget.qml`: `visible: loaded && (live || EditMode.active)`), and `SekaiVisualizer` has an `active` switch (`active: win.live`) that stops its frame loop and the position poll when the widget is off.
- New: **Visualizer performance** (Settings -> Desktop, or `qs ipc call visualizer performance <0|1|2>`), saved in `visualizer.json` as `perf`. **Full** (0, default) is as before. **Balanced** (1): sheet and engine at about 28 fps instead of 36, at most 240 keys. **Lite** (2): 20 fps, at most 150 larger keys, plain-colour keys instead of one gradient per key (every key has its own colours, so each gradient was its own texture and draw call), and the seek bar moves in small steps instead of gliding (Balanced and Lite). At the L and XL sizes Full draws about 330 keys, Balanced about 240, Lite 150; the small sizes drop from about 100-170 to 60-85 in Lite.
- Changed: **the visualizer's window is only as big as the card at rest.** It was a full-screen transparent layer, so every frame the card drew made Hyprland redraw and re-blend the whole screen (in `nvtop`: Hyprland 26% -> 49-51% of the iGPU with the card on, even in Lite, while `qs` itself stayed at 1-2%). Now (`VisualizerWidget.qml`) the window is anchored top-left with margins and is the card's box plus room around it for the pop-in overshoot (at 1080p, UI scale 1, the M card's window is about 8% of the screen). A wrapper `shifter` slides the whole stage by the window's own position, so everything inside still works in screen coordinates (placement, saved position, masks, Edit Desktop). The window goes back to full screen, as before, while Edit Desktop is open or closing and while widgets are unlocked for dragging on the desktop (`EditMode.desktopDragAllowed`). `roomW/roomH` now come from the screen, not the window, so placement works while the window is small or not mapped. `services/visualizer-test/box_unit.js` checks the box maths (2124 cases: the card at its largest pop-in is inside the box, whole pixels, same screen position as in a full-screen window).
- Files: `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `SekaiVisualizer.qml`, `VisualizerEngine.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `services/visualizer-test/box_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — First-run defaults: Stack clock on the right, shipped default wallpaper

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): brackets checked and the seed script tested in a shell (fresh folder, existing copy kept, folder that can't be made, missing source); the existing unit tests still pass (12 and 20).

- Changed: the clock's default design is **Stack** (3), the sticker time on end, hours over minutes with the two beat diamonds and the AM tag (was Keys). Its default place is the right edge, vertically centred, with the To-Do card's 40 px margin, so it sits in one column under that card (was top-left). Only a clock that was never placed uses it: anyone with a saved `clock.json` keeps their design and position. `ipc call clock resetPosition` and moving the clock to another monitor in Edit Desktop go to the new spot.
- New: a default wallpaper ships with the shell, `wallpapers/default-wallpaper.png` (2520 x 1440). When nothing is saved yet (or the saved file is gone), `shell.qml` runs `services/default-wallpaper.sh seed`, which copies it into the wallpaper folder (without overwriting) and returns the path; that becomes the desktop wallpaper, so it also shows in the picker and the theme is made from it. If the folder can't be written, the bundled file is used in place; if that fails too, the first file of the folder as before.
- Files: `services/ClockService.qml`, `ClockWidget.qml`, `services/EditMode.qml` (comment), `shell.qml`, `services/default-wallpaper.sh` (new), `wallpapers/default-wallpaper.png` (new), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Memory audit: clipboard image previews no longer pile up in RAM

Static audit of the whole tree (no live Quickshell to profile). One real leak found and fixed; the rest checked clean (see below).

- Fixed: selecting an image in the clipboard card decoded it to `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/pjsekai-clip-<id>` and never deleted it. `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` is tmpfs, i.e. RAM, so every screenshot browsed in the clipboard stayed in memory until logout. Now each decode removes the previous file in the same command (so only the current one exists), dropping the selection removes the last one, and the shell clears any leftovers at start-up.
- Changed: the clipboard card's image preview no longer keeps decoded copies in Qt's pixmap cache (`cache: false`); it is a one-off preview of a temp file.
- Checked, no leak found: wallpaper slots (every create has a matching `destroy()`; traced fast switching, switching before decode, resize re-decode), the notification history (capped at 100, pruned entries are dismissed so the server lets go), clipboard list (300), the pactl / cava / analyzer streams (`SplitParser`, gated by `running`), Overview screen capture (only while open), Edit Desktop previews (one grab kept, dropped on exit), region screenshots and recordings (cleaned by a timer and at start-up), music recognition (`trap` removes its WAV), queues (all drained), `ddcutil detect` (runs once).
- Files: `ExtrasSource.qml`, `ClipboardCard.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Random wallpaper transition

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in). Checked: `services/lock-transition-test/lock_transition_unit.js` (12 cases, 5 new: random only picks drawable styles and never plain fade, never repeats the last pick and reaches every style, one pick per change across monitors, lock rolls only in random mode, the wiring) and the lockscreen wallpaper test (20 cases, unchanged).

- New: **Random** in Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> Transition. Every wallpaper change plays a different style from Ring, Star, Sweep, Iris, Flash and Blocks (never the same one twice in a row; plain Fade is left out, it is the no-shader fallback). With "same wallpaper on every monitor", all monitors play the same pick.
- Changed: `WallpaperLayer.qml` keeps the style of its own switch (`activeStyle`, taken from `WallpaperService.styleForSwitch()` when the switch starts) instead of reading `WallpaperService.transitionStyle` live, so changing the setting mid-blend can't swap shaders under a running transition.
- Changed: with Random, locking and unlocking each pick their own style too (`LockState.lock()` / `beginUnlock()` call `WallpaperService.rollLockStyle()`); `WallpaperService.transitionStyle` is now the style the lock plays. A wallpaper change during a lock animation does not change it.
- Needs the same compiled shaders as before (`shaders/compile-shaders.sh`); a style whose shader is missing behaves as it did.
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `services/LockState.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `services/lock-transition-test/lock_transition_unit.js`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Locking and unlocking play the wallpaper transition

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in), and **the new shaders are not compiled** (no `qsb` here): run `shaders/compile-shaders.sh` after copying this in. What was checked: `services/lock-transition-test/lock_transition_unit.js` (7 cases: the timing rules match `WallpaperLayer.qml`'s `blendAnim`, every style maps to a lock shader pair, every pair's vert/frag uniform blocks match and only use uniforms `LockRing.qml` supplies, the exact p = 0 / p = 1 ends, the fallback wiring; three deliberate breakages were caught) and the lockscreen wallpaper test (20 cases, still passing). The GLSL was not compiled with `qsb`, but every `*_lock.frag` was type-checked and *run* on the CPU through a small C++ shim (`services/lock-transition-test/shader-sim/shader_sim.py`, needs g++): mean alpha goes 0 -> 1 locking and 1 -> 0 unlocking for all seven styles, no NaNs, nothing brighter than its alpha. That run found and fixed one bug in `sekai_sweep_lock`: each lane's glow was still on screen on the last frame (18% of the screen's alpha when unlocking), which would pop at the hand-over to the real lock surface; the glow now dies out as a lane finishes.

- Changed: lock and unlock now play the wallpaper's **transition style** (Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper) instead of always the sekaiRing: **sekaiRing, star, diagonalBlocks, sekaiIris, sekaiSweep, sekaiFlash and fade**, each over the live desktop exactly as the ring already did. The lock uses the style's own tuning (`ring*`, `star*`, `blocks*` on `WallpaperService`), speed and easing, so it feels like a wallpaper change in that style. Changing the style in Settings changes the lock too.
- New: a lock version of each other style, `shaders/<style>_lock.vert` / `.frag` (`fade_lock`, `sekai_sweep_lock`, `sekai_iris_lock`, `sekai_star_lock`, `sekai_blocks_lock`, `sekai_flash_lock`; `sekai_ring_lock` already existed). Each is the wallpaper shader's own maths with one texture (the lockscreen) and premultiplied alpha out, so the desktop shows through where it is transparent; the extra uniform `int unlocking` picks the direction (0 locking: desktop old / lockscreen new, 1 unlocking: the reverse).
- New: `WallpaperService.transitionMsFor(style)`, `transitionLinearFor(style)` and `lockShaderStemFor(style)` (the style's duration, whether it is driven Linear, and its lock shader). `WallpaperLayer.qml` is unchanged and still has its own copy of the timing rule; the test checks the two agree.
- Changed: `LockState.ringMs` follows the style's duration (sekaiIris is 2 s, as a wallpaper change in that style is; ring / star / blocks use `ringDuration`; the rest `transitionDuration`). New `LockState.transitionStyle` (the style being played) and `wantedStyle` (replace its binding with a style name to pin one).
- Safety: if a style's lock shader can't be loaded (its `.qsb` was never built), `LockRing.qml` logs it and plays the ring for the lock (`LockState.styleFallback`) until another style is picked or the shell restarts. Not run live: if a missing `.qsb` does not report `ShaderEffect.Error` on your Qt, the lock for that style would show no transition (the session still locks and unlocks on time), so build the shaders.
- New: `LockRing.qml` logs which style and shader each lock plays (`qs log -c pjsekai | grep LockRing`), and `shaders/check-shaders.sh` lists shader sources that have no `.qsb`. **If locking shows only the ring for every style, the `*_lock.qsb` files are missing: run `./shaders/compile-shaders.sh`, then restart the shell** (the fallback to the ring lasts until a restart or until another style is picked).
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `services/LockState.qml`, `LockRing.qml`, `shaders/{fade,sekai_sweep,sekai_iris,sekai_star,sekai_blocks,sekai_flash}_lock.{vert,frag}` (new), `services/lock-transition-test/lock_transition_unit.js` (new), `services/lock-transition-test/shader-sim/` (new), `shaders/check-shaders.sh` (new), `shaders/README.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Equalizer: your 10-band EQ, as a spinning record with ten pegs

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): the QML was checked for syntax only (qmllint) and `equalizer.sh` with `bash -n`; nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- New: the equalizer card (`EqualizerPopup.qml`). Open it with the new **EQ key** on the turntable (beside play) or `qs -c sekaipop ipc call eq open` (focused monitor); the album key on the card goes back to the turntable. It comes and goes like the other bar popups, under the media pill.
- The disc: the cover is a record that spins while the song plays (7 s a turn, still when paused), on a fixed plate with **ten pegs**, one per band (32 Hz .. 16 kHz), clockwise from the bottom left like a gauge. Drag a peg in or out for -12 .. +12 dB (one dB a step), wheel over it = 1 dB, double or right click = back to 0. A line runs through the pegs (the curve wrapped round the record) with a stem from 0 dB to each peg. The middle of the record shows the band you hold (frequency and dB); the sticker at the bottom is the preset in use, with a pink dot while a change is heard but not saved.
- The side: the eight presets (Flat, Bass, Treble, Vocal, Pop, Rock, Jazz, Classic) plus your own saved curves (right click one of yours to delete it), a preamp lane, **AUTO** (follows the song's genre through Last.fm tags, in the background even with the card closed), **SAVE / UNDO / SAVE AS**, and the tune key for setup: which EasyEffects preset the equalizer is merged into (pick one, or make a new one) and the Last.fm key. A pink note shows when EasyEffects has never saved the chosen preset (the equalizer would wipe the rest of the chain).
- Same behaviour as the end4-pC version: dragging only previews (a scratch preset), SAVE writes the real preset, and closing the card without saving puts the sound back to the saved preset.
- Changed from the end4-pC version: all of it is one singleton (`services/EqualizerService.qml`), and every call to `equalizer.sh` goes through one queue, one at a time (the script reads and rewrites a small state file, so two at once could lose a change). Auto's player is picked from Mpris directly (Spotify, and Firefox on youtube.com, as before; edit `allowedPlayerIdentities` at the top of the service).
- Not carried over: the blurred-art dim slider (the disc replaces that background) and the delete-an-EasyEffects-preset button (`delete_preset` is still in the script; delete in EasyEffects itself).
- Needs `easyeffects`, `jq`, `python3` (and `curl` for Auto). State: `~/.local/state/pjsekai/eq`.
- If typing into the card does nothing (the compositor did not give the popup the keyboard): `qs -c sekaipop ipc call eq lastfmKey <key>`, `... eq saveAs <name>`, `... eq preset <name>`, `... eq auto on|off`, `... eq get`.
- Files: `EqualizerPopup.qml`, `services/EqualizerService.qml`, `services/equalizer.sh` (new); `MediaPopup.qml` (EQ key, `eqRequested`), `Bar.qml` (`eqPopupOpen`, loader, hand-over, ipc hook), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Separate Lockscreen Wallpaper

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): the logic is unit-tested with node (`services/lock-wallpaper-test/lock_wallpaper_unit.js` runs the real function bodies cut out of `WallpaperService.qml`, 20 cases, all passing, and each of three deliberate breakages of the code was caught); every touched QML file was parsed with `qmllint` (no syntax problems), nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- New: **Settings → Desktop → Wallpaper → Separate Lockscreen Wallpaper** (`wallpaper.separateLock`, off by default). Off: the lock screen shows the desktop wallpaper exactly as before. On: it has a wallpaper of its own, independent of the desktop's. Changing the desktop wallpaper (a pick, Random, the timer, a per-monitor change) never changes the lock screen's, and a lock screen pick never changes the desktop's. Turning it on starts from what the lock screen shows now, so nothing moves until you pick; turning it off follows the desktop again and the choice is kept for next time.
- New: the wallpaper picker has a **Desktop | Lockscreen** switch where its title was (`WallpaperTargetSwitch.qml`; Tab flips it) while the option is on. The Lockscreen tab changes the header to the lock screen's own colours (`LockPalette`, so it follows the theme), the applied ring and check too, the subtitle says *Lockscreen only* / *Desktop only*, and a card that is also what the other side shows wears a small **Lockscreen** / **Desktop** sticker. Switching tab scrolls to that side's applied wallpaper. With the option off the picker is unchanged.
- New: a lock screen pick keeps the picker **open** (nothing visible changes on screen, so the applied ring moving to the new card is the confirmation, and you can go on to the Desktop tab). A desktop pick closes it as before. To close after a lock pick too, add `root.close()` after the `setLockWallpaper` calls in `pick()` / `pickAll()` in `SekaiWallpaperPicker.qml`.
- New: with **One wallpaper per monitor** on each monitor can have its own lock screen wallpaper (click = the monitor the picker is on, Shift+click = every monitor). A monitor with none shows the lock screen default, so a pick on one monitor can't reach another. Turning the per-monitor mode off keeps the main monitor's lock screen wallpaper for every monitor, the same way the desktop does.
- New: a tile *Lockscreen wallpaper* under the switch (only while it is on) names the current one and opens the picker on the Lockscreen tab (`SettingsFace` / `SettingsStage` / `QuickPanel`: `lockWallpapersRequested`).
- New: IPC `wallpaper setlock | getlock | setlockmonitor | getlockmonitor` and `wallpaperpicker showlock | togglelock`; `options set wallpaper.separateLock true|false` works too.
- New: a video can be picked for the lock screen; it shows its first frame (the lock screen does not play video).
- Changed: `LockSurface.qml` takes its picture from `WallpaperService.lockImagePathFor()` (one property, `wallpaperFile`), so the real lock surface and `LockRing.qml`'s copy of it always show the same one; the lock ring plays between the desktop wallpaper and the lock screen's own.
- Note: theme colours (matugen) and the lock screen's text and accents keep following the **desktop** wallpaper / the shell theme, not the lock screen wallpaper.
- Note: saved in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/lockwallpaper` and `lockwallpaper-screens`, apart from the desktop's `wallpaper` and `wallpaper-screens`. Nothing existing is migrated or changed; an install without these files behaves as before.
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `services/wallpaper-state.sh`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `LockSurface.qml`, `SekaiWallpaperPicker.qml`, `WallpaperGallery.qml`, `WallpaperCard.qml`, `WallpaperTargetSwitch.qml` (new), `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `SettingsFace.qml`, `SettingsStage.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`, `services/lock-wallpaper-test/lock_wallpaper_unit.js` (new), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — New default bar: three zones, Transparent style

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): the QML was checked for balanced brackets only, nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- Changed: the default bar arrangement is now **Left** Active window, Media, Tray; **Center** Wi-Fi / Bluetooth / sound, Weather, Workspaces, Clock, Date; **Right** Utility keys, Battery, Privacy, Discord call, Notification bell. CPU & RAM, Keyboard layout and Updates are in the right zone so they land somewhere sensible when they are on.
- Changed: the default bar style is **Transparent** (was Float), until a style is picked in Settings → Bar → Style.
- Changed: **CPU & RAM** and **Updates** are off by default (`bar.resources`, `bar.updates`), as neither is in the default bar. Settings → Bar → Modules switches them on.
- New: **CLEAR ALL** on Edit Desktop's BAR tab (beside RESET, which now closes up around it): every module off the bar in one click. Modules keep their zones and land in the OFF lane; RESET (or dragging them back) restores them. Same switches as taking one module off (Settings -> Bar -> Modules; with separate bars only that monitor's own list). `ipc call barlayout clearall`.
- Fixed: a popup (most of all the media panel) no longer leaves a small copy of its card that stays and fades when it is put away. The window was unmapped the instant the card reached zero size, so the last picture the compositor had was the frame before, with the card still about a fifth of its size in it, and Hyprland faded that frozen picture out. The window now stays mapped for 80 ms (`settling`) after the card is gone, so the last picture is an empty one. The taller the card, the more of it was left in that frame, which is why the turntable (490 x 402) showed it most. Also covers a popup that is put away at once because another one opens (`PopupHandoff`). Not run live: if a faint rectangle shows for a moment after a popup closes, lower `settleTimer.interval` in `SekaiPopup.qml`.
- Fixed: the media panel no longer shows a small, cut-off copy of itself when it is put away. Every popup shrinks toward a point just above its window (so the whole card slides into the pill and leaves the window), which only works for a small card: the 490 x 402 turntable was still sticking out of the window's top edge when it had got small, so it was sliced off and what was left stayed on screen. A `bare` card (the turntable) now shrinks to the point on the window's edge nearest its pill (on a top bar, onto the bar's edge), so all of it stays in view down to nothing; the other popups and the way the turntable grows in are unchanged. (`SekaiPopup.qml`: `exitToEdge`, `exitOriginX`, `exitOriginY`.)
- Changed: the package-update checker (`services/PackageUpdatesService.qml`) only looks and sends its "updates are waiting" notification while the Updates module is on, so with it off by default there is no background `checkupdates` and no notification pointing at a badge that is not there. `ipc call updates refresh` still looks when asked.
- Changed: Edit Desktop's RESET brings back the modules of the default bar (CPU & RAM and Updates are left as they are), and no longer shows just because one of them is in the OFF lane.
- Note: a saved bar layout (`bar-layout.json`) and a saved style (`bar-style`) still win over the new defaults. Run `qs -c sekaipop ipc call barlayout reset` and `qs -c sekaipop ipc call barstyle set transparent` to switch an existing install over.
- Files: `services/BarLayoutService.qml`, `services/BarStyleService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/PackageUpdatesService.qml`, `SekaiPopup.qml`, `Status_Bar.qml`, `EditBarLayout.qml` (CLEAR ALL), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Edit Desktop: denser grid and four side lines on drop

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none to run it in): the maths is unit-tested with node (`services/grid-test/grid_unit.js`, updated for the new cell size, all passing); the QML was checked for balanced brackets only. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- Changed: the alignment grid is twice as dense: 20-px cells (was 40), so 96 x 54 cells on 1080p. Three line weights: a hairline every cell, the old medium line every second one, a bar every eighth (160 px, the same spacing the old strong lines had). Small faint diamonds on the new crossings; the old medium and big diamonds are unchanged. Snapping follows the grid: a card now lands on any 20-px line, and the grid's pull is 7 design px (was 11), so there is still free room between two lines. Widgets' own lines still win over the grid.
- New: on release, four lines, one along each side of the dropped widget (left, right, top, bottom), run edge to edge across the screen for about a second (cyan with the navy outline, they shoot out from the card), then fade out with the rest of the guides. Like end4-pC's snap lines, but on every drop, so the full second is always given (the shorter "nothing lined up" hold is gone).
- Changed: the four side lines are quick: they shoot out in 140 ms, stay about 0.4 s in all (`EditGrid.railMs`), and fade in 160 ms. The names, brackets and flares still run the full second.
- New: the widget being edited or moved is in front of the other widgets. While it is selected or dragged its window goes from `WlrLayer.Bottom` to `Top` (`EditChrome.raised`), and back to `Bottom` after. A surface that changes layer is put on top of that layer, so the one edited last stays in front. Not run live: if the compositor does not reorder on a layer change, the widget will still go up while edited but fall back behind afterwards.
- Changed: the Visualizer's card has the same corner radius as the To-Do, Clock and User card (`Theme.radiusOuter`, 13) at every size; it was 28. The rounded shapes inside it were eased to stay nested in it: the screen 18 -> 10, its top shade 15 -> 7, the paused-keys tint 12 -> 5. Nothing else about its look or size changed. (`SekaiVisualizer.qml`.)
- Changed: the bar's pills share one white ring. The tray, the Wi-Fi/status pill, the clock (time), the date, the weather and the battery pills had a ring width of 8 on a flat bar; they now use the Utility pill's 6 (the outline stays 3, so the white band is 3 px instead of 5). Standing bars already used 6. The tray's arrow button (`TrayButton.qml`) follows.
- Fixed: the tray capsule is the same height as the other bar pills (40 px with its shadow); it was 46 px. In the bar its vertical padding is 3 (was 4); a standing bar's tray keeps its old width (side padding 8). The dropdown's capsule (not compact) is unchanged: ring 8.
- Changed: the tray's popups come and go the way the bar's other popups do (SekaiPopup.qml): they grow out of the bar's edge in 340 ms with a little overshoot and shrink all the way back into it in 170 ms, with no fade to hide the end. That is the dropdown (the unpinned apps) and the right-click menu (Pin / Unpin and the app's entries); both had no dismiss animation (the window just went), the dropdown had no entry animation either, and the menu had a smaller scale-and-fade. The dropdown also hands over to other popups (`PopupHandoff`) and ignores clicks while it shrinks. The menu card has the inner ring the other popups' cards have. The dropdown's capsule uses the bar pills' ring (6); its padding grew by 2 to keep its size. The menu keeps its entries (and "Unpin" / "Pin") while it shrinks away.
- Fixed: a popup no longer leaves a small piece of itself behind to fade away. It used to shrink toward a point under the bar's edge and fade out over its last stretch. Now it shrinks toward the middle of its own pill (the point is outside the card, so the whole card slides into the pill and leaves the window there) and is not faded out at all; it still fades in a touch as it opens. For every `SekaiPopup` (Calendar, Battery, Weather, Media), the tray dropdown (to the tray button) and the tray's right-click menu (to the pinned icon, or the tray button when the dropdown is open). Entry grows from the same point.
- Files: `EditGridMath.js` (`CELL` 20, `MAJOR` 8, `PULL_GRID` 7), `services/EditGrid.qml` (`dropRect`, `railsLive`, `railMs`, `major`; `quietHoldMs` removed), `EditGuides.qml` (three-weight grid, four side lines), `EditChrome.qml` (`raised`), `ClockWidget.qml`, `TodoWidget.qml`, `UserCardWidget.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml` (layer follows `chrome.raised`), `SekaiVisualizer.qml` (card radius), `TrayCollection.qml`, `TrayButton.qml`, `StatusPill.qml`, `DateIndicator.qml`, `WeatherIndicator.qml`, `BatteryIndicator.qml`, `Status_Bar.qml` (bar ring and tray height), `TrayMenu.qml`, `TrayPopups.qml`, `SekaiPopup.qml` (popup animation and where it shrinks to), `services/grid-test/grid_unit.js`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Edit Desktop: alignment grid, snapping and alignment guides

**Not yet run on a live Quickshell** (there was none to run it in): the maths (snapping, alignment, same size, guide slots) is unit-tested with node (`services/grid-test/grid_unit.js`); the QML was checked for balanced brackets and duplicate ids only, nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- New: while a widget is dragged in Edit Desktop a subtle grid of diamonds (40-px cells, hairlines, a stronger line every fourth cell) fades in across the card. The widget is pulled onto grid lines and onto other widgets' lines (edges to edges, centres to centres, the screen's edges and middle); widgets win over the grid when both are in reach. An edge on a grid line lights that line up and pings a diamond on the corner.
- New: guides while dragging (light) and on release (full strength for about a second, then they ease out): TOP / BOTTOM / LEFT / RIGHT / CENTER / SCREEN CENTER / FLUSH lines in cyan, mint and white with the navy outline, one line through every widget that shares it, with a dot on each. Widgets of the same width or height get a pink bracket with "=" and a "= SAME WIDTH / SAME HEIGHT" sticker on the dropped one. The widgets it lines up with flare their outline, and so does the dropped one.
- New: `EditGuides.qml` (one click-through overlay per monitor, `Overlay` layer, namespace `pjsekai-edit-guides`, mapped only while that monitor is edited), `services/EditGrid.qml` (shared state: phase, guide slots, rectangles), `EditGridMath.js` (the maths), `services/grid-test/grid_unit.js` (its tests).
- Changed: `EditChrome.qml` reports its card's rectangle, snaps the card on every move (so the shield drag and a selected widget's own drag are both covered) and flares when something is dropped on a line with it; its outline goes mint and a little thicker while the card is held. The four widgets' drag areas set `chrome.ownDrag` (one line each). `shell.qml` loads the overlay.
- Not changed: positions are still saved the way they were (`setPosition` on drop, now of the snapped position); locking, selecting, removing and the shelf work as before.
- Files: `EditGuides.qml`, `EditGridMath.js`, `services/EditGrid.qml`, `services/grid-test/grid_unit.js` (new); `EditChrome.qml`, `ClockWidget.qml`, `TodoWidget.qml`, `UserCardWidget.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `shell.qml`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Desktop right-click menu: the inner ring in Dark and Darker
- Fixed: the menu you get by right-clicking the desktop (Edit Desktop, Wallpapers, Group themes) showed only the outline against its dark fill in Dark and Darker. It now has the inner ring just inside the outline (white in Dark, the dim grey in Darker: `Pill.innerRing: Theme.cardRing`), the same as the bar's popups and pills. It is drawn over the edge of the fill, so the card keeps its size and the rows do not move. Light mode is unchanged (no ring).
