## Unreleased — Colour themes live in Edit Desktop (a THEME tab with previews)
- Changed: the colour-theme picker is no longer a card under the media pill. It is a third tab, **THEME**, on Edit Desktop's shelf (next to WIDGETS and BAR). Ctrl+Shift+T, `ipc call theme toggle` / `open` and the desktop right-click menu's "Group themes" now open Edit Desktop straight on it (toggle again, or Done, to leave; from another tab it switches to THEME).
- Redesigned: each group is a tile with a small picture of the shell in that group's colours (a bar, a widget card, two Quick Panel keys, one on and one off) and its name under it; the active one is lit with the accent gradient and a tick badge. The pictures use each group's own palette and the current Light / Dark / Darker look for their surfaces. The last tile is "Matugen" (colours from the wallpaper) and shows the live palette. Click picks it, and the shell, the card included, re-colours at once; the mouse wheel scrolls the row when the tiles do not fit.
- Changed: the three tab keys are shorter (60 x 42, were 60 x 60) so three fit on the shelf; the how-to lines and the subtitle on the left follow the tab.
- New: `EditMode.open(screen, tab)`, `EditMode.showTab(tab)` and `requestedTab` / `currentTab` (the shelf tells EditMode which tab is showing); `ipc call desktopedit theme`.
- Removed: `ThemePopup.qml`, and its loader and wiring in `Bar.qml`.
- Not yet run on a live Quickshell (there was none to run it in): the QML was checked for balanced brackets only, nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

## Unreleased — Media popup redrawn as a turntable (the "Spotify Turntable" design)
- Changed: the media card is now a record player copied one for one from the "Spotify Turntable" picture (Fons Mans, Figma): black body, the cover as a picture disc with grooves in a platter, the progress as a white ring ending in a dot, the tonearm and its grey base, the dial and the disc with the light at the top, the app's name top left, the artist and title turned on their side down the right, the avatar, and play / pause bottom left with rewind and fast forward bottom right. The positions and sizes were measured off the picture (design pixels 680 x 558, drawn at 0.72, so the card is 490 x 402) and checked by overlaying them on it.
- New: it moves like a turntable. While a song plays the record spins (7 s a turn) and the arm swings onto the record and walks inwards with the song; paused, the arm is parked beside it, as in the picture. `motion: false` in `MediaPopup.qml` keeps both still.
- New: press and drag on the ring (or the rim of the record) to seek, with a time chip showing where you would land. The dial at the top turns to the next app when several are playing ("1/3" after the app's name); the disc with the light goes to the player's window.
- Fixed: the arm lagged when a paused song was played again (its angle was tied to the song position, which updates every half second, and every update restarted the swing from its slow start). The swing now only follows play / pause (`engaged`), and the walk inwards rides on the already-smooth progress.
- Changed: down the right side the title is the bold line and the artist the small grey one under it (a video with no artist shows its album, or the app, there).
- Kept: everything the card did with the players (one entry per app, the pill's player first, Firefox forks named by their window class, the YouTube-thumbnail fallback for cover art, keys the player cannot do dimmed, Space / N / P / Left / Right) and its wiring in `Bar.qml`. With no player the turntable stays up with an empty record and "Nothing playing".
- Changed: `SekaiPopup.qml` has a `bare` mode (`bare: true`, content in `bareContent`) for a card that draws its own body: no navy outline, white ring or hard shadow, and the card is exactly the size of the numbers it is given. Calendar, Battery and Weather do not use it and look as before.
- Removed: the rhythm chart, the combo, the banner, the stickers and the slanted keys of the previous media card. `MediaMath.js` still has their helpers (`chart()`, `combo()`, ...); nothing uses them now.
- Not yet run on a live Quickshell (there was none to run it in): the QML was checked for balanced brackets and duplicate ids, and the layout against the picture, but nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.
- Files: `MediaPopup.qml`, `SekaiPopup.qml`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Media popup rebuilt as a Project SEKAI live (the song is a chart)

**Not yet run on a live Quickshell** (there was none to run it in): the chart / combo / time math is unit-tested (`MediaMath.js`), `MediaPopup.qml` was checked for balanced brackets, duplicate ids and `Theme` names, but nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- Changed: the media card is laid out again around the game's live screen, on the shared `SekaiPopup.qml` frame (so it grows out of the bar's edge, shrinks back into it, hands over to the other popups through `PopupHandoff`, and closes on Escape). Wiring in `Bar.qml` is unchanged (`source`, `parentWindow`, `anchorItem`, `open`, `closeRequested`), and so are the Light / Dark / Darker looks (every colour is a `Theme` token).
- **The chart**: the seek bar is now a rhythm chart. Three lanes of notes (taps, and holds that are long bars) are made up from the song's title, so a song always has the same chart (decoration with a game's manners, not an analysis of the audio; 12 to 36 notes, about one every six seconds). A glowing judgement line with a diamond head is the playhead and glides between the player's position readings while it plays. Every note it reaches ripples, hops and goes dim, and a PERFECT / GREAT / GOOD sticker pops off it. Click or drag the chart to seek; slide the pointer along it to see where you would land. A stream with no length shows no chart.
- **The combo**: how many notes the playhead has reached, as sticker numerals that hop with every hit, next to a slanted COMBO tag. Past 97 % of the song a FULL COMBO stamp slaps onto the jacket with a puff of diamonds.
- **The rest**: a NOW PLAYING / PAUSED / STANDBY banner on a slanted gradient plate with three beat diamonds that take turns while it plays; the app as a tilted sticker in its own attribute colour (when more than one app is playing it counts "1/3" and a click turns to the next one; the pill's player comes first); the cover as a big tilted jacket with a star stuck on it (click it to go to the player's window); the time as sticker numerals; and Prev / Play / Next as three slanted keys, Play sitting pressed in while it plays. Everything pops in as a cascade when the card opens, and the jacket hops on a new song. Nothing animates on its own while the card sits there.
- Changed: one player at a time instead of a stacked card per player (this replaces the list that scrolled past three). The choice is forgotten when the card closes.
- Kept: the YouTube-thumbnail fallback for cover art, one entry per app (`MediaOwner.js`), Firefox forks named by their window class, keys the player cannot do dimmed, and the empty state ("No song on stage").
- New keyboard, best effort whenever the compositor gives the popup focus: Space plays / pauses, N / P next / previous, Left / Right seek five seconds.
- Removed: `MediaPlayerCard.qml` and `MediaButton.qml` (nothing else used them).
- Files: `MediaPopup.qml`, `MediaMath.js`, `MediaOwner.js` (comment only); `MediaPlayerCard.qml` and `MediaButton.qml` deleted.

## Unreleased — Calendar, Battery and Weather popups rebuilt (stickers, keys, a gauge, a melody)

**Not yet run on a live Quickshell** (there was none to run it in): the date, battery and weather math is unit-tested (`CalendarMath.js`, `BatteryMath.js`, `WeatherMath.js`), every QML file was checked for balanced brackets, `Theme` names and ids, but nothing was drawn. If a piece misbehaves, `qs log -c pjsekai` names the file and line.

- Changed: the three popups are laid out again from scratch instead of dressed up. They keep their wiring in `Bar.qml` (same properties) and the Light / Dark / Darker looks (every colour is a `Theme` token).
- **Calendar**: a big tilted date sticker that shows whichever day you point at (a TODAY tag peels off it while you are on another month; click it to jump back), the month as sticker text with two lines that talk back (week number and days left; or "Friday, 23 Oct / in 16 days" for the day you point at), a twelve-diamond month lane with a hopping playhead (click a diamond to jump), the year chip (click = next year, right click = last year), and the days as keycaps that drop in as a cascade, with a ribbon under the row you point at. Click a day to stick a diamond on it in one of the five attribute colours (again = next colour, then none, right click = clear); click a faded day to turn to its month. New setting key `calendar.marks` holds the marks (it also counts in the "changed settings" number on About, and `options reset` clears it).
- **Battery**: the life gauge. The charge as sticker numerals, a tilted judgement stamp (ALL PERFECT / PERFECT / GREAT / GOOD / BAD / MISS, thresholds in `BatteryMath.js` `tier()`; poke it for a puff of diamonds), ten slanted segments that fill in one by one (a pulse runs up them while charging; they shake once when low). Slide the pointer along the gauge to forecast: the stamp shows the judgement of that level and the strip says how long until the battery gets there at the current pace (a straight line from UPower's estimate). Time left on two keycaps like the clock's, and Power mode as three keys (Saver / Balanced / Boost, Boost only when the daemon offers it), the active one pressed in.
- **Weather**: a sky ticket. A tilted disc with the weather on it (poke it: it squashes and sends diamonds), the temperature as sticker numerals with a conditions tag, the place on a slanted tab, six stat keys that straighten when pointed at (humidity is a tank that fills, the wind arrow turns to where the wind is going, UV is a five-diamond meter), and one switch for two forecasts: **Hourly** is a melody (notes on a temperature curve with a rain gauge under each) and **Days** is three tickets that fill with water as high as the chance of rain. The switch is the existing `weather.view` setting.
- Entry / outro: the card now grows from nothing out of the bar's edge (whichever edge the bar is on) and shrinks all the way back into it on dismiss, with no fade (340 ms in, 170 ms out). Pressing another popup's button while one is still going away puts the old card away at once (new `services/PopupHandoff.qml`), so two cards are never on screen together; pressing the same button again halfway turns the card round instead of replaying its pop-ins. The window's flat side now follows the bar's edge (bottom and side bars no longer get an extra 14 px gap).
- New shared parts: `SekaiPopup.qml` (focus grab, placement, pop-in and swallow, Escape), `StickerText.qml`, `DiamondDot.qml`, `SegmentSwitch.qml`, `Burst.qml`. Nothing animates on its own while a popup sits there, apart from the refresh arrow while loading and the charging pulse while the battery card is open and charging.
- Files: `CalendarPopup.qml`, `BatteryPopup.qml`, `WeatherPopup.qml`, `CalendarMath.js`, `BatteryMath.js`, `WeatherMath.js`, `SekaiPopup.qml`, `StickerText.qml`, `DiamondDot.qml`, `SegmentSwitch.qml`, `Burst.qml`, `services/PopupHandoff.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`.

## Unreleased — Visualizer: the key-bed answers every hit
- Fixed: the sheet missed a lot of the music. It was pressed by the chart-writer's notes, which are thinned for a rhythm game (the beat grid drops off-beat hits, and each instrument has a budget): in a test song about a quarter of the kicks, a third of the snares and most of the hats never reached it. `VisualizerEngine` now also announces every hit the analyzer (or the demo) names, as it hears it (new signal `heard(kind, strength, lane)`), and the key-bed presses on those, with a short minimum gap per kind so a burst is a patter. The chart's notes are only used for the critical star (and as before when the audio comes from cava).
- Fixed: a bass note was drawn as a kick (the key-bed told them apart by strength, not by size), so bass notes pressed the middle like a drum. A kick is 2.6 wide and a bass note 1.3; they are told apart by that now, and by name on the new path.
- Fixed: `VisualizerWidget.qml` declared `armed` twice (Duplicate property name, the widget did not load). The "switched on" one is `switchedOn`.
- Changed: hits are bigger and instant. A press used to be only a push on the keys' speed, so a kick peaked about a sixth of a second after it was heard and a hat barely moved a key (depth 0.1). Now every hit also puts the keys down in the same frame (`dent(..., snap)`), and flashes them white: kick and bass a wide dent with a flash, a hat two crisp taps with a glint, a snare a band you see run across (pressed and flashed column by column) instead of a push that peaked late. Soft hits are lifted so they still read (`0.4 + 0.8 x strength`).
- Changed: the keys spring back quicker (`STIFF` 15 -> 42, `DAMP` 3.2 -> 6.5, still one soft bounce), so a hit is gone by the next beat and the sheet has something to hit again; `EMIT_GAIN` 380 -> 760 keeps the spectrum waves as strong as before with the stiffer spring. A pressed key also shrinks (0.12 instead of 0.07) and darkens more.
- Changed: the deck's lane no longer bounces. The diamond playhead does not hop on the beat any more, picking it up no longer overshoots, and while a track plays the head never steps back for the small backward jitter some players report (a real seek or a new track still moves it). The deck's keys draw their icons with `MaterialIcon` (Material Symbols Rounded, filled, normal weight) the same way `MediaButton.qml` does; the heavier weight is gone.
- Files: `VisualizerEngine.qml`, `SekaiVisualizer.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `KeyBedMath.js`.

## Unreleased — Visualizer: the key-bed (the keycap columns and the tray are gone)
- Removed: the columns of keycaps, the beat diamonds over them, the long tray that pressed into its shadow on every kick, the sliding green keycap and the sticker-per-column. Nothing else used them.
- New: `SekaiVisualizer.qml` is redrawn from the ground up as a pocket player. Its screen is a sheet of keycaps with the album cover printed on it (read into a mosaic; sides mirrored; the Sekai pattern when a track has no art), and the music PRESSES the keys instead of lighting them. The sheet is a lattice of springs (`KeyBedMath.js`, pure JS, tested by `services/analyzer-test/keybed_test.js`): a kick is a drum-head press in the middle with a ring running out, a snare is a slap that sweeps across (left to right, then back), a hi-hat is a tiny sharp tap that glints, a bass note is a dent at its pitch, a held voice is a green finger that follows the pitch and presses steadily, and twelve emitter diamonds along the foot rock the sheet at a speed that rises with the pitch (the wavelength you see is the pitch you hear). A critical hit also throws a gold star sticker.
- New: a track change re-keys the sheet from the middle out (a wave of presses; each key swaps colour at the bottom of its press). Paused, the colours sleep under a veil and the play key comes up.
- New: the card is a player. A deck under the screen has previous / play / next keycaps (the pill press and spring) and a lane with a diamond playhead that hops on the beat; drag the lane to seek. A tag (title and artist) is stuck on when the track changes and peeled off a few seconds later; hover brings it back.
- New: the surface is interactive when Lock widgets is on (the default): press and drag the keys, double-click to play / pause, right / middle click for next / previous (the sheet slaps that way), wheel for the player's volume (the bottom row of keys rises as far as the volume reaches). Unlocked, the card is for dragging as before.
- New: Settings -> Desktop -> "Visualizer: stay when paused" (on by default, `ipc call visualizer stayPaused true|false`): the card stays while a player is paused and goes when the player is gone and the music has stopped. Before it only drew while audio played.
- Changed: Visualizer colors has three modes: Cover (default), Sekai (the five card attributes), Theme (the two accents). `ipc call visualizer colors 0|1|2`.
- Changed: sizes are S 264x236, M 320x276 (the clock's card), L 420x350, XL 520x430 (Full is gone; it was a strip).
- Changed: the card takes clicks whenever it is on screen (before, only while widgets were unlocked); `VisualizerWidget.qml` owns a `MediaSource`.

## Unreleased — Dark mode: the popups and the launcher get the white inner ring
- Fixed: in Dark mode the bar's popups (Battery, Weather, Calendar, Resources, Discord, Theme, Scale) and the app launcher's search pill and results card showed only the navy outline against the dark fill, with no inner ring, while the bar's own pills have outline -> white ring -> fill. They now have the ring too: `ringWhite` just inside the outline (white with a hint of the theme colour in Dark, the dim grey in Darker, as for every other pill). It is drawn over the edge of the fill, so no card changes size and nothing inside moves.
- Unchanged: Light mode (a white ring on a white card would not show), the Quick Panel / To-Do in the classic Dark look, the outline, the hard shadow and the fills. The tray menu and the launcher's Clipboard / Colour / Emoji / Web cards are not touched.
- New: `Theme.cardRing` (the ring's width: the outline's width in Dark and Darker, 0 in Light), `Pill.innerRing`, and `InnerRing.qml` for a plain Rectangle card.
- Files: `Theme.qml`, `Pill.qml`, `InnerRing.qml`, `Overview.qml`, `BatteryPopup.qml`, `WeatherPopup.qml`, `CalendarPopup.qml`, `ResourcePopup.qml`, `DiscordPopup.qml`, `ThemePopup.qml`, `ScalePopup.qml`.

## Unreleased — Screen Snip: the dim no longer zooms in and out, and stays that way after a reload
- Fixed: the dimmed screen of the region picker (Screen Snip tile, the bar's Screenshot key, `ipc call region ...`) zoomed in when it opened and out when it closed. That is Hyprland's own layer animation, played because the picker's overlay is mapped and unmapped. The `no_anim` layer rule that switches it off was only added live, once, when the shell loaded, so any Hyprland reload (`hyprctl reload`, saving `hyprland.lua`, the anti-flashbang tile, the Hyprland page in Settings) cleared it and the zoom came back. The rule is now put back at start-up and after every reload (`RegionService` listens for Hyprland's `configreloaded` event), and when `hyprland.lua` loads `pjsekai_overrides` it is also kept in `~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua` (marker lines `-- pjsekai:region-no-anim`, `-- pjsekai:region-preview-no-anim`, which the other pjsekai options never touch) so a reload brings it back by itself.
- Unchanged: the dim (48 % black, the hole around the selection), the frozen picture, the toolbar and the preview card look and behave as before. Nothing in the picker's own QML was animating; there is no scale or zoom in it.
- Changed: the rule is applied by a new `rules` mode of `services/region-action.sh` instead of an inline script in `RegionService.qml`.
- Files: `services/RegionService.qml`, `services/region-action.sh`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Parallax settings: the wallpaper no longer blinks black while you change them
- Fixed: turning Parallax on or off, or dragging its **Amount** slider, made the wallpaper blink black. Those settings change the size the wallpaper is decoded at (it is decoded `amount` % bigger than the screen so it stays sharp), and every wallpaper Image had that size bound live, so each change made Qt throw the picture away and decode it again, with nothing to show until it was done. A settled wallpaper's decode size is now frozen. When the size it should have changes, `WallpaperLayer.redecode()` waits for the slider to stop (250 ms), decodes a second copy at the new size hidden, draws it over the one on screen once it is ready and only then removes the old one (`freshSlot` / `freshTimer`). The picture is never empty, never reloaded in place, and no transition runs; for a moment it is just scaled from the old decode (a touch softer if the size went up). A real wallpaper change in the meantime cancels the pending copy.
- Unchanged: the first decode still follows the window's size until it finishes (the window may not have its size yet at start-up). The Parallax drift, the workspace count and everything else are untouched.
- Files: `WallpaperLayer.qml`.

## Unreleased — Wallpaper per monitor: the main monitor's wallpaper is the starting point, and a pick on it stays on it
- Fixed: with \"One wallpaper per monitor\" on, a monitor with no pick of its own showed the old default wallpaper (it goes stale in this mode). Now every monitor starts from what the main monitor (`WallpaperService.themeScreen`, the first monitor) shows right now: turning the mode on, a monitor being plugged in, or a reload with a monitor that has no pick all give that monitor the main monitor's current wallpaper, saved as a pick of its own (`pinMissing()`), with no transition (it is what the monitor already shows).
- Fixed: turning the mode on used to bring back the picks of an earlier session (they were kept, hidden, while the mode was off). The picks are now forgotten when the mode goes off (and any left in `wallpaper-screens` by an older version are dropped on the first start with the mode off).
- Fixed (follow-up): changing the main monitor's wallpaper changes ONLY the main monitor and the mode stays on. A first version of this change made a monitor without a pick follow the main one for good, so a pick on the main monitor spread to every monitor and the mode looked turned off. Now every monitor holds its own pick before anything is changed, so nothing can spread. Nothing ever switches `wallpaper.perMonitor` off except you (the switch, or `ipc call options set`).
- Changed: with the mode on, Shift+click / `ipc call wallpaper set` / the default for \"every monitor\" gives each monitor that wallpaper as its own pick in one step. Picking, for a monitor, the same picture it already shows still records a pick of its own (the \"already set\" check compares with the monitor's own pick).
- Unchanged: after a reload with the mode on, monitors that were given their own wallpaper keep it (no flash, no transition, see the entry below).
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Edit Desktop: the wallpaper is never touched when the mode starts or ends
- Fixed: the monitor being edited no longer blinks black when Edit Desktop starts (or ends). `WallpaperTextureSlot.qml` used to switch `mipmap` on for the mode (`mipmap: EditMode.active`), and on EVERY monitor. Changing `mipmap` on a live Image makes Qt drop the GPU texture and upload the whole full-size wallpaper again right then, which blanked the picture for a moment. `mipmap` is now always off, so the wallpaper's textures are exactly the same before, during and after the mode (the card shows the picture at about 0.6 - 0.75 of its size, which bilinear filtering handles).
- Fixed: a video wallpaper is no longer unloaded when Edit Desktop starts (it used to stop and jump to its first frame, on every monitor). It keeps playing inside the card.
- Fixed: the stage behind the card (`EditBackdrop.qml`) faded in as a whole, over the window's own black, so black showed round the wallpaper as it began to shrink. The navy base is now solid from the first frame; only the diamonds and the card's shadow fade in.
- Files: `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `WallpaperTextureSlot.qml`, `EditBackdrop.qml`.

## Unreleased — Wallpaper per monitor: no flash of the main wallpaper on a shell reload
- Fixed: after a shell reload (or a restart) a secondary monitor no longer shows the main monitor's wallpaper for a moment and then blends back to its own. The cause was start-up order: the saved default wallpaper was read first and the per-monitor picks (and the \"One wallpaper per monitor\" switch itself) a moment later, so for that moment every monitor was told to show the default, and then the monitor with its own pick started a transition to it. Now `WallpaperService.pathFor()` answers nothing until everything it depends on has been read back (new `WallpaperService.ready` = the wallpaper restore is done AND the settings file is loaded). Each `WallpaperLayer` waits for that, then puts its monitor's own wallpaper straight on screen: no transition, nothing temporary, and nothing is saved or changed on disk while restoring, so the state is exactly what it was before the reload.
- Also: the theme colours (matugen) and the lock screen follow `pathFor()`, so they no longer start from the wrong wallpaper at start-up either (matugen used to run once for the default and again for the main monitor's own pick).
- Unchanged: picking, Random, Shift+click, turning the mode on and off, and the first-run fallback behave as before; transitions still run for every real change after start-up.
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`.

## Unreleased — Wallpaper per monitor: turning it off keeps the main monitor's wallpaper
- Fixed: turning off "One wallpaper per monitor" no longer changes the wallpaper you are looking at. Before, every monitor went back to the default (the last "every monitor" pick), so the main monitor could jump to an older wallpaper, and a shell reload kept that older one. Now the wallpaper on the main monitor (the first monitor, `WallpaperService.themeScreen`) becomes the default and is saved (`wallpaper`), so it is what every monitor shows afterwards and what a reload brings back while the mode stays off. The other monitors' own picks are still kept for turning it on again.
- Changed: the settings switch calls `WallpaperService.setPerMonitor(bool)`, which adopts the main monitor's wallpaper BEFORE the flag flips (no blend to the old default and straight back). Turning it off any other way (`ipc call options set wallpaper.perMonitor false`) gets the same result through `onPerMonitorChanged`.
- Unchanged: with the mode on, a pick still only changes the monitor it was made on (Shift+click: all), so a change on one monitor never touches another.
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Dock: left / right edge and one per monitor
- New: Settings -> Desktop -> Dock -> **Position** (`dock.position`: bottom, left or right; bottom by default). On a side edge the pill stands up and its icons run in a column; dividers, hit areas, the auto-hide strip and the hide / reveal slide all follow the edge. It keeps clear of a bar on the same edge. The edge is part of the dock's model entry (like the bar's), so moving it builds a fresh window instead of re-anchoring a live one.
- New: Settings -> Desktop -> Dock -> **Dock on every monitor** (`dock.perMonitor`, off by default, so nothing changes until you turn it on). On: one dock per monitor. Pinned apps are on every dock; an app that is not pinned only gets a slot on the dock of the monitor its window is on (pinned apps still count as running if they are open on any monitor, and a click goes to the window on this monitor first). Pinning, "keep above windows" and auto-hide are shared. Off: one dock on the first monitor.
- Changed: `shell.qml` builds the dock with a `Variants` and owns `ipc call dock peek | autohide | position <edge>` (one handler, not one per dock). `Dock.qml` takes its screen and edge from `modelData`.
- Changed: `RegionService` keeps a per-monitor `docks` map (`setDock`) instead of the single `dockScreen` / `dockTop` / `dockHalfWidth`, so the region picker's toolbar still clears a bottom dock on each monitor. A side dock is not reported (it is not in the toolbar's way).
- Changed: `EditStage` takes `originX` / `originY` for a window that does not start at the screen's corner (a dock on the left edge), so the dock shrinks into the Edit Desktop card in the right place. The dock window's size across now includes the auto-hide gap, so its top edge is the screen's top edge with auto-hide on too.
- New: `DockDivider.qml`.
- Files: `Dock.qml`, `DockDivider.qml`, `shell.qml`, `EditStage.qml`, `RegionSelector.qml`, `services/RegionService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Separate bar per monitor
- New: Settings -> Bar -> **Separate bar per monitor** (`bar.perMonitor`, off by default, so nothing changes until you turn it on). On: each monitor keeps its own bar, both the left / center / right arrangement and which modules are on it. Edit Desktop's BAR tab edits the monitor it is open on and no other (drag, click, right click, the OFF lane and RESET all stay on that monitor). A monitor that has not been edited yet follows the shared layout until its first change, which starts from a copy of it. Off again: the shared layout shows everywhere and each monitor's own is kept for next time.
- Changed: the Settings -> Bar -> Modules switches are still the master switches. With separate bars, taking a module off in Edit Desktop only adds it to that monitor's own off list; putting back a module that Settings has turned off turns it on for this monitor and keeps it off on the others.
- Changed: Settings -> Bar -> Reset bar layout resets the monitor the Settings Panel is on while the option is on.
- New: `ipc call barlayout movescreen <name> <module> <zone> <index> | resetscreen <name>`; `get` also returns each monitor's bar under `screens`.
- Changed: `bar-layout.json` keeps the shared layout at the top level and adds `screens: { "<name>": { left, center, right, off } }` only once a monitor has its own, so older versions still read the file.
- Files: `services/BarLayoutService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `Bar.qml`, `EditBarLayout.qml`, `SettingsPageBar.qml`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Wallpaper per monitor
- New: Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> **One wallpaper per monitor** (`wallpaper.perMonitor`, off by default, so nothing changes until you turn it on). On: the picker sets the monitor it is open on (the board says "for DP-1 (Shift+click: all)"), Shift+click sets every monitor, Random gives each monitor its own pick, and each monitor's lock screen shows its own wallpaper. Off again: the default shows everywhere and each monitor's pick is kept for next time.
- New: `ipc call wallpaper monitors | setmonitor <name> <path> | getmonitor <name> | randommonitor <name>`. Per-monitor picks are saved in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/wallpaper-screens` (`wallpaper-state.sh savescreens | loadscreens`); `wallpaper` stays the default.
- Changed: each `WallpaperLayer` now runs its own transition and decode wait for its monitor (`WallpaperService.pathFor(name)`), so changing one monitor never touches another and the "primary layer drives the shared progress" election is gone. `WallpaperService.progress`, `transitioning`, `previousPath`, `transitionReady`, `transitionCompleted`, `videoActive` and the layer register / ready calls were removed (nothing else used them). `currentPath` is now read-only: the first monitor's wallpaper, which is what the theme colours follow. `setWallpaper(path, screenName)` takes an optional monitor.
- Fixed: picking several video wallpapers quickly could give a video the first frame of an earlier one. First frames are now made one at a time, each with its own path, and only the latest pick per monitor is applied.
- Changed: the first-run fallback in `shell.qml` fills in the default (`ensureDefault`) and never clears a monitor's own pick.
- Files: `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `services/wallpaper-state.sh`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `SekaiWallpaperPicker.qml`, `WallpaperGallery.qml`, `WallpaperCard.qml`, `LockSurface.qml`, `Lockscreen.qml`, `LockRing.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `shell.qml`, `README.md`.

## Unreleased — Visualizer: keycaps (the note highway is gone)
- Removed: the three note-highway looks (Highway, Slant, Flat) and everything only they used: `VisualizerShader.qml`, `shaders/sekai_notes.vert` / `.frag` (+ `.qsb`), the "Visualizer look" setting, the corner "look" button in Edit Desktop and `ipc call visualizer nextDesign | setDesign`. A saved look in `visualizer.json` is simply ignored.
- New: the visualizer is redrawn in the clock's KEYS language (`SekaiVisualizer.qml`, replacing the first "Stage" attempt, a dark box with spectrum bars that never got shipped as anything you liked): columns of small keycaps (hard shadow, navy outline, white ring) lit from the bottom by the spectrum, popping in with an overshoot and shrinking away, with pale chips where they are unlit so the shape is always there; the columns lean like the Settings' slanted plates. A beat diamond over each column marks its peak (hangs, then drops). A tray under the columns carries a diamond per column and presses into its shadow on a kick, while the columns jump a little. Where the analyzer hears a hit a sticker pops over that column and floats up: a cyan note (tap), a red star (flick), a big gold star (critical). A green keycap with a note slides along the top to the pitch of a held voice. No dark box, no shader: it sits straight on the wallpaper like the clock.
- New: Settings -> Desktop -> Visualizer colors (`ipc call visualizer colors 0|1`): the five card attributes (Cool, Cute, Happy, Mysterious, Pure) or the theme's own two accents.
- Changed: sizes are S 420x170, M 600x210, L 860x260, Full 240 high.

## Unreleased — Start-up log clean-up
- Fixed: a missing `warp-cli` no longer fails to start on every poll. The WARP tile looks for it once (`command -v`); without it the tile says "Not installed" and clicking it sends a notification with how to install it (Arch: `yay -S cloudflare-warp-bin`, then `sudo systemctl enable --now warp-svc`). It looks again on each click, so installing it later works without a restart.
- Fixed: `DiscordVoiceService.clientWindowOpen` could come out `undefined` before Hyprland's window list was ready ("Unable to assign [undefined] to bool").
- Fixed: the Quick Panel's "Binding loop detected for property visible" at start-up: `onHiddenLiftChanged` wrote `stage.lift` (which `win.visible` reads) while `visible` was being worked out. It now leaves `lift` alone while it still has its start-up value.
- Fixed: hundreds of `QQuickItem::stackAfter` warnings at start-up. The first-run wallpaper fallback in `shell.qml` was a `Repeater` directly under `ShellRoot` (not an Item), so each delegate had no parent. It is a plain `FolderListModel` + function now.

## Unreleased — Transparent bar: smaller gap to the windows
- Fixed: with the Transparent bar style the gap between the pills and the windows below (or beside) them was bigger than the gap between the pills and the screen edge. The bar reserved its whole strip and Hyprland adds `general.gaps_out` on top of that. In Transparent style the bar now reserves the strip minus `gaps_out` (so the two gaps match) and minus 5 more design pixels (`transparentExtraGap` in `Bar.qml`), so the windows sit a little closer than the edge gap. Float and Straight are unchanged. It follows the gaps setting live (Settings -> Hyprland) and works on every edge. Files: `Bar.qml`.
- Changed: `services/HyprlandSettings.qml` reads Hyprland's values once at start-up (before, only when the Settings / Quick Panel opened), so the bar knows `gaps_out` straight away.

## Unreleased — Phone QP: Bluetooth battery level
- New: in the Bluetooth list (right-click the Bluetooth tile), a connected device that reports its battery shows the level on the right of its row: the same battery frame glyph as the bar, then the percentage (red at 20% and under). Devices that don't report one show nothing.
- Changed: `QuickSource.qml`'s Bluetooth list script runs one `bluetoothctl info` for each connected device (not for the paired-only ones) and reads BlueZ's "Battery Percentage"; each line is now `mac|connected|battery|name`. Devices are `{address, name, connected, battery}` (`battery` is -1 when unknown). Files: `QuickSource.qml`, `QuickPanelFace.qml` (comment only), `BluetoothDialog.qml`.

## Unreleased — Privacy pill: icons no longer vanish
- Fixed: the privacy pill sometimes came up with no icons (or the wrong one) until Quickshell was refreshed. Which icons to draw was decided inside the mic / screen change handlers by checking `live`, which can still hold its old value at that moment, so the update was skipped and the old (empty) state stayed. `micShown` / `screenShown` are now set together from `micLive` / `screenLive` directly (`refreshShown()`, the same way the Discord pill does it), and also once at start-up. Only `PrivacyIndicator.qml` changed.

## Unreleased — Edit Desktop: bar keycaps stay inside their lanes
- Fixed: on the shelf's BAR tab, keycaps (e.g. Media in CENTER, Keyboard layout in OFF) wrapped onto a third row and drew below their lane. The lanes were split by how many keycaps they hold, not by how wide those are, so a lane with a few wide keycaps got one keycap per row.
- Changed: each lane now gets at least the width its keycaps need to fit in the rows the shelf has room for (the keycaps are measured once, off screen); the rest of the width is shared as before.
- Changed: if the keycaps with their names would not all fit (a small screen, or a lane with many in it), every keycap shows just its icon instead, and hovering one shows its name in a small tooltip. Nothing scrolls and nothing leaves its lane.

## Unreleased — audit pass (no look-and-feel changes)
- Fixed: switching Fahrenheit / Celsius now redraws the weather at once (it waited for the next fetch, up to 15 minutes).
- Changed: the bar's popups (theme, media, Discord, battery, calendar, weather, resource) are built the first time they are opened instead of at start-up on every monitor.
- Changed: the Quick Panel's network speed reads /proc/net/dev directly instead of starting `cat` every second.
- Changed: the Settings pages go back to their top when the Settings come up.
- Removed: the desktop weather card (WeatherCard.qml, its two settings, `ipc call weathercard`), the unused wallust accent (services/WallpaperAccent.qml), the unused prism-burst shader, SettingsTab.qml, a few unused properties / functions, and a debug log line in ClockService.

## Unreleased — Visualizer: notes by instrument (note analyzer)
- New: `services/sekai_analyzer.py`, the visualizer's audio tap (python3 + numpy, `parec` or `pw-record`; no aubio). It says what happened instead of only how loud each band is: kick, snare, hi-hat, a new bass note, and whether a clear, held lead tone (a voice) is playing and at what pitch. Replaces cava as the source; cava stays as the fallback (used automatically if the analyzer can't start, or with Settings -> Desktop -> "Visualizer: note analyzer" off / `ipc call visualizer analyzer false`).
- Changed: each instrument has its own note and lanes (kick: wide tap in the low lanes, gold for accents; snare: wide tap in the middle; hat: red flick in the top lanes; bass: tap by pitch in the low lanes; lead tone: the slide). New `ChartMath.stepLive()` / `leadStep()`.
- Fixed: the slide was on nearly all the time. It now needs a clear, steady, held tone (about 0.2 s), rests 0.35 s between slides, ends with a red flick, and is cut after 3.6 s of an unchanging tone (then a 0.9 s rest).
- New: optional aubio engine for the analyzer (`pacman -S python-aubio`): aubio's yinfft pitch tracker for the lead tone and bass, and its beat tracker as the beat clock (ChartMath uses it while it keeps reporting beats). Automatic when installed; Settings -> Desktop -> "Visualizer: use aubio" / `ipc call visualizer aubio true|false`. Falls back to the built-in numpy tracker if aubio is missing or errors. Untested against real aubio at the time of writing (only a stand-in with the same API).
- Changed: the Edit Desktop demo song plays through the new rules too.
- Tests: `services/analyzer-test/run-tests.sh` (made-up song + node checks).
- Known limits: the slide appears about 0.3 s after the tone starts; bass-note timing is the weakest part (late when a kick sits on top of it); the lead detector finds clear single tones, so a voice buried in a busy mix may be missed and a lead synth counts as a voice. Notes still land with their sound (they don't fall towards the line yet).

## Unreleased — Sekai audio visualizer
- New: desktop audio visualizer widget (`VisualizerWidget.qml`), modelled on end4's shader visualizer but drawn as the Project Sekai note highway: 12 lanes in perspective, the lanes' slots under the judgement line as level meters, tap notes (cyan), flicks (red, with up-chevrons), criticals (gold, with a halo) spawned by onsets, and a green slide ribbon with diamond ticks and head notes that follows the spectrum's centre of mass. Hits ring and sparkle at the line. It only draws while audio plays.
- New: `shaders/sekai_notes.vert` / `.frag` (run `shaders/compile-shaders.sh`), `VisualizerEngine.qml` (cava bands -> lanes, onset detection, "chart writing"), `VisualizerShader.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml` (+ `visualizer-state.sh`, `cava-sekai.conf`).
- New: three looks (Highway, Slant, Flat) and four sizes (S, M, L, Full). In Edit Desktop select the card: the corner buttons change look and size. Also Settings -> Desktop -> Widgets, and `qs -c sekaipop ipc call visualizer toggle | nextDesign | setDesign N | nextSize | setSize N | sensitivity X | listenAll true|false | resetPosition`.
- New: the shelf has a Visualizer slot; Edit Desktop plays a made-up demo song so the card and its shelf picture show something without audio.
- Changed: notes now follow the music's rhythm and are far less jittery. New `ChartMath.js` does the detecting: a lane fires on a jump above its recent level (adaptive threshold) and has to fall back before it can fire again; one hit is one note (neighbouring lanes are folded together, at most ~8 notes / s); the tempo and phase are tracked (autocorrelation of the onset strength), and once locked an off-beat hit has to be clearly strong to become a note. The highway pulses on the beat clock too. The slide ribbon now needs sustained energy in the melody lanes and is smoothed, so it no longer flickers on and off. Notes still land exactly with their sound.
- Needs `cava` (audio tap). It runs only while a media player (MPRIS) is playing, or always with "listen to all audio".

## Unreleased — Privacy, updates, media panel, region picker
- New: Discord call panel (`DiscordPopup.qml`, `DiscordMemberRow.qml`, `DiscordKey.qml`). A left click on the Discord pill opens a card under it, drawn in the SEKAI look: a blurple plate with halftone dots, slanted light bands and the channel name, one row per person (a slanted tab and ring in one of the five attribute colours, picked from their Discord id; talking lights the row's outline, sends a ring out of the avatar and jumps rhythm-game bars; muted / deafened people get the red badge), Mute and Deafen keycaps that press into their shadow and go red while on, and an Open Discord button. Middle click on the pill mutes, right click deafens. The pill stays pressed in while the panel is open, and the panel closes when the call ends. Settings → Bar → Discord pill click: Open panel / Mute (the old click). `ipc call discord focus` brings the Discord window forward.
- New: the Discord panel's header shows the server's picture and name next to the face of whoever you are talking with. The bridge asks Discord for the server (`GET_GUILD`; a refusal is ignored); the Vencord companion sends it too (re-run its `install.sh` to update). Nothing shows in a DM / group call.
- Fixed: right-clicking a pinned tray icon opens its menu directly below the icon, centered on it (it used to start at the icon's left edge and so sat off to the icon's bottom-right).
- Changed: the pinned tray apps and the tray (dropdown) button are now one pill: the arrow button is the last cell of the pinned pill, so pinning an app just pops its icon into that pill instead of a separate capsule sliding open beside the button. Motion is quick: ~110ms size glide with a small pop, icons pop in with a light overshoot and nothing slides over; the dropdown opens instantly. The arrow is the same Material glyph as before and still spins when the dropdown opens. The standalone `TrayButton.qml` is no longer used (`TrayCollection.qml`, `Status_Bar.qml`, `TrayPopups.qml`).
- Fixed: the tray right-click menu no longer jumps sideways for a moment when opened from the dropdown. The menu is now its own popup window, mapped once the app's entries have arrived, so it appears at its final size and place instead of resizing the dropdown's window while it is on screen (`TrayPopups.qml`, `TrayMenu.qml`).
- New: Discord pill on the bar (`DiscordPill.qml`, `services/DiscordVoiceService.qml`), ported from p3dro's island: while you are in a Discord voice channel it shows up to three avatars (a white ring around whoever is talking), the channel and head count, and a red badge while you are muted / deafened. Click mutes, right-click deafens, hover lists who is in the call. First time: a "Link Discord" pill. Works with Discord, Vesktop / Equibop (Vencord companion in `services/discordVoice/vencord-companion`). Switch: Bar → Modules → Discord call; `ipc call discord mute|deafen|authorize|get`.
- Fixed: the phone's Screen Snip tile now opens the shell's own region picker, like the bar's Screenshot button (so Copy / Save / Copy text / ... and the preview card work from it too). It was still running a bare `grim` + `slurp`. Settings → General → Own region picker off keeps the old behaviour.
- Changed: the preview card's exit is smooth: it slides all the way off the screen edge while fading, moving from the first frame (it used to sit still, then snap away). The card has its own Hyprland no-animation layer rule (`pjsekai-region-preview`).
- New: preview card after a region Copy / Save (`RegionPreview.qml`): the picture slides in at the bottom-right with Annotate (swappy), Copy, Save and close; click the picture to annotate. Goes away by itself (Settings → General → Screen region → Preview stays) and waits while hovered. Switch: Preview after a screenshot.
- New: status pill on the bar: Wi-Fi / Ethernet, Bluetooth and speaker as icons only, details in a hover tag, click opens the Quick Panel (`services/StatusIconsService.qml`, `StatusPill.qml`). Switch: Bar → Modules.
- Fixed: Bluetooth icon when the adapter is on but nothing is connected. The Quick Panel tile showed "connected" for any powered-on adapter; it now shows `bluetooth_disabled` (off), `bluetooth` (on, nothing connected) and `bluetooth_connected` (a device connected). The paired-device list uses `bluetooth` for devices that are not connected.
- Changed: a bar module that is new to a saved bar layout is put next to the module before it in the default bar instead of after the clock.
- Changed: the privacy pill and the updates badge grow in and shrink away smoothly (scale, fade, and the bar's slot opens / closes) instead of popping.
- Changed: the updates badge sits on the bar with a spinning glyph while it looks for updates (from start-up, on a right-click / `ipc call updates refresh`, and while an update runs) instead of appearing only once the count is known.
- New: privacy indicator on the bar (mic live / screen shared), read from PipeWire. Icons only on the bar; hovering shows which app has the microphone and who is watching the screen. `ipc call privacy dump` helps if it mis-detects.
- New: pending Arch package updates as a badge on the bar (`checkupdates`, plus AUR through paru / yay), amber with a hopping icon and one notification at the nudge level. Click runs the update in a terminal.
- New: media panel from the media pill (a card per player: cover, seek bar, previous / play / next). Left click opens it, middle click plays / pauses, the wheel changes the player's volume. Settings can switch back to the old clicks.
- Changed: the region picker's toolbar never lands on the dock: when the selection reaches the bottom of the screen (a full-screen grab) it sits above the dock, inside the selection.
- Fixed: the Settings phone was soft next to the Quick Panel phone. Its picture is now put on whole device pixels at rest, and its backdrop layer is drawn at the screen's density.
- Changed: the region picker's toolbar is just the buttons (no bar behind them), and a button's name slides out smoothly on hover instead of snapping.
- Fixed: the region picker's toolbar labels were unreadable on hover in dark mode (navy text on a dark button).
- New: right-drag a region in the picker to open it in swappy; a plain right click still cancels.
- Fixed: the screen-region picker no longer zooms in and out (Hyprland layer animation switched off for `pjsekai-region`; the dim is unchanged).
- New: own screen-region picker: copy, save, copy text (OCR), image search (uploads to uguu.se, opens Google Lens), record with or without sound. `ipc call region ...`; the bar's Screenshot and Record keys use it.
- New: dock auto-hide (slides up at the bottom edge) and "Pin on startup".
- New: wallpaper thumbnail cache, random wallpaper (now / every N minutes), parallax between workspaces, video wallpapers (qt6-multimedia).
- New: app colours for kitty, foot, VS Code and the launcher (fuzzel, rofi), each with a switch and a Revert (`services/app-theme.sh`).
- New: scroll on tray icons, a "needs attention" dot on tray icons, scroll hints on the scrollable bar modules.
- New: weather location you type, hourly view in the popup, optional desktop weather card.
- New: Power off / Reboot on the phone warn while a package manager or download is running.
- Both new bar modules (Privacy, Updates) are in Edit Desktop's BAR tab and Settings → Bar → Modules.

## Unreleased — The phone turns into Settings
- Changed: the gear on the Quick Panel no longer puts the phone away and opens a second panel. The same phone rolls a quarter turn, rises to the middle of the screen, and its screen dissolves into Settings. The X (or Escape on the menu) turns it back into the Quick Panel.
- With the Quick Panel down, `ipc call settings show|toggle|page` still opens Settings on their own, as before.
- `SettingsPanel.qml` is gone: Settings live inside the Quick Panel's window (`SettingsStage.qml`, timing in `FlipMath.js`).

## 3.8.0 — Customizable bar
- New: every bar module can be moved into the left, center or right zone, in any order. Arrange it in Edit Desktop, on the new BAR tab of the shelf (drag keycaps between lanes, click to send one to the next zone, RESET to start over).
- New: modules can be taken off the bar from the BAR tab (right click, or drag into the OFF lane) and put back the same way; it is the same switch as Settings → Bar → Modules, which now also has Workspaces.
- New: `qs -c sekaipop ipc call barlayout get | move | reset`; Settings → Bar → Reset bar layout.
- Removed: the scale, bar style and bar position buttons on the bar. They are in Settings Panel → Bar (Style, Position, Size).
- The switches for those three buttons are gone from Bar → Modules.

## 3.7.0 — Edit Desktop shelf
- Redesigned: the side drawer is now a shelf along the bottom of the screen.
- New: the shelf shows each widget as it really looks (a live picture of its card), not an ON / OFF tile.
- New: left click adds or edits a widget, right click removes it (on the shelf or on the card); the pink X is gone.
- New: a selected widget works like on the normal desktop (clock style button and double-click, To-Do tasks, avatar pop) and can still be dragged.

## 3.6.0 — Settings Panel
- New: a landscape phone that pops up in the middle of the screen with every shell setting in six pages: Quick, General, Bar, Desktop, Hyprland, About / Update.
- New: every bar module (and each utility key) can be switched off from Bar → Modules.
- New: Hyprland gaps, borders, rounding, blur, animations, input and workspace options, applied live and kept in `pjsekai_overrides.lua`.
- New: About / Update checks for a newer shell version and shows this changelog.
- `qs -c sekaipop ipc call settings toggle` opens and closes it.

## 3.5.0 — Edit Desktop
- Right-click the desktop to edit it: widgets drag on a framed card, with a drawer of tiles to bring them back.
- Widgets pop in and out from their centre.

## 3.4.0 — Night Light, weather, gamma
- Night Light card with intensity and an automatic schedule.
- Weather popup with feels-like, humidity, wind, UV and the next two days.
- Gamma shares a slider with brightness; reload popup reports shell reloads and errors.

## 3.3.0 — Lockscreen follows the theme
- The lockscreen wears the active group theme or the matugen palette.

## 3.2.0 — Launcher without the tint
- Opening the Overview no longer darkens the screen.

## 3.1.0 — To-Do keeps its place
- The To-Do card stays where you put it through reloads and restarts.

## 3.0.0 — Material Discord
- Discord follows the shell theme (Material Discord + matugen).
